Invariant fields of rational functions and semilinear representations of symmetric groups over them
M.Rovinsky

Abstract
. Let K K be a field and G G be a group of its automorphisms endowed with the compact-open topology, cf. § 1.1 . There are many situations, where it is natural to study the category Sm K ( G ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G) of smooth (i.e. with open stabilizers) K K - semilinear representations of G G . According to Hilbert’s Theorem 90, cf. Proposition 2.2 , the category Sm K ( G ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G) is semisimple (in which case K K is a generator of Sm K ( G ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G) ) if and only if G G is precompact. In this note we study the case of the non-precompact group G = 𝔖 Ψ G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} of all permutations of an infinite set Ψ \Psi . It is shown that the categories Sm K ( 𝔖 Ψ ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}) are locally noetherian; the morphisms are ‘locally split’. Given a field F F and a subfield k ≠ F k\neq F algebraically closed in F F , one of principal results (Theorem 1.4 ) describes the Gabriel spectra (and related objects) of the categories Sm K ( 𝔖 Ψ ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}) for some of 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} -invariant subfields K K of the fraction field F k , Ψ F_{k,\Psi} of the tensor product over k k of the copies of F F labeled by Ψ \Psi . In particular, the object F k , Ψ F_{k,\Psi} turns out to be an injective cogenerator of the category Sm K ( 𝔖 Ψ ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}) for any 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} -invariant subfield K ⊆ F k , Ψ K\subseteq F_{k,\Psi} . As an application of Theorem 1.4 , when transcendence degree of F | k F|k is 1, a correspondence between the 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} -invariant subfields of F k , Ψ F_{k,\Psi} algebraically closed in F k , Ψ F_{k,\Psi} and certain systems of isogenies of ‘generically F F -pointed’ torsors over geometrically irreducible one-dimensional algebraic k k -groups is constructed, so far only in characteristic 0. The only irreducible finite-dimensional smooth representation of 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} is trivial. However, over an invariant subfield K d K_{\mathrm{d}} of k ⁡ ( Ψ ) k(\Psi) , one can construct a series of smooth irreducible K d K_{\mathrm{d}} -semilinear representation of 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} of unbounded finite dimensions, cf. Proposition 5.23 . In general, any smooth 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} -field K K admits a smooth 𝔖 Ψ \mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi} -field extension K ~ | K \widetilde{K}|K such that K ~ \widetilde{K} is a cogenerator of Sm K ~ ( 𝔖 Ψ ) \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}) , cf. Proposition 4.28 . Appendix collects some results on smooth characteristic 0 linear representations of symmetric groups and of the automorphism group of an infinite-dimensional vector space over a finite field.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Motivation and goals

Let
G
be a group of permutations
of a set
\Psi
. We consider
G
as a totally disconnected group whose base of topology is
generated by the left (or right) translates of the stabilizers of the elements of
\Psi
.

The problem of describing continuous representations of
G
in discrete vector spaces (i.e., with open
stabilizers), called
smooth
in what follows, arises naturally in many situations. E.g., some birational
‘motivic’ algebro-geometric questions over a characteristic 0 algebraically closed field
k
are related to
describing certain irreducible smooth representations of the automorphism group
G
of an algebraically closed
field extension
C|k
of infinite transcendence degree. There are some reasons to expect that the numerous
‘interesting’ representations can be embedded into the single
C
-vector space of differential forms

\Omega^{\bullet}_{C|k}=\bigoplus_{q\geq 0}\Omega^{q}_{C|k}
. Note that the
G
-action on
\Omega^{q}_{C|k}

is
C
-
semilinear
. This leads to the study of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
of smooth
C
-semilinear
representations of
G
, that can be considered as ‘quasicoherent sheaves’ in the dominant topology
(in the sense of
[
9
]
).

Classical examples of the study of smooth semilinear representations are given by (i) Hilbert’s Theorem 90
(
[
22
, Satz 1]
) stating that smooth semilinear
Galois
representations are ‘trivial’,
and (ii) the theory of (
q
-)difference equations.
The first case where
G
is not locally (pre)compact (namely,
G
is the symmetric group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

of all permutations of an infinite set
\Psi
) is considered in
[
20
,
12
]
.

In analogy with Hilbert’s Theorem 90, one may ask whether for a given permutation group
G
and a field

K
endowed with a smooth
G
-action there exists a smooth
G
-field extension
B|K
such that all
smooth semilinear representations of
G
are ‘as trivial as they can be’.

The purpose of this paper is (i) to describe the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
of all smooth

K
-semilinear representations of the symmetric group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
over some
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-fields

K
and (ii) to construct, for an arbitrary field
K
endowed with a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action,
a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field extension
B|K
, called a ‘weak period’ field extension, such that
B
is
a cogenerator of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{B}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Compared to the ‘usual’ (i.e.
k
-linear for a field
k
) representations, the series of examples of extensions

K|k
considered here (
K_{?}
for
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c}\}
, depending on a field extension
F|k
, see below)
have the feature that the structure of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(e.g. its Gabriel spectrum) depends rather on
the label
?
of
K
than on
F|k
(with a minor modification if the characteristic of
k
is 2 and

F=k
).

Though the case of the group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
is a toy model of this kind of problems, a faithful and exact functor
relates the above ‘algebro-geometric’ category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
of ‘quasicoherent sheaves’
to the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, where
k(\Psi)=k(x~|~x\in\Psi)
is the field of rational
functions over
k
in variables labeled by the set
\Psi
(and endowed with the natural
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action).
In particular, at least some of ‘interesting’ objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
are transformed to injective objects of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

1.2.
Basic notation

For an abelian group
A
and a set
S
we denote by
A\langle S\rangle
the abelian group
which is the direct sum of copies of
A
indexed by
S
, i.e., the elements of

A\langle S\rangle
are the finite formal sums
\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}[s_{i}]
for all integer

N\geq 0
,
a_{i}\in A
,
s_{i}\in S
, with addition defined in the obvious way.

For a group
G
, a unital associative ring
A
endowed with a
G
-action
(by ring automorphisms), a subgroup
H\subseteq G
,
we consider
A\langle G\rangle
as a unital associative ring; and more generally, we consider

A\langle G/H\rangle
as an
A\langle G\rangle
-
A^{H}\langle N_{G}(H)/H\rangle
-bimodule:

(a[g])(b[g^{\prime}\pmod{H}])(c[h^{\prime}]):=ab^{g}c^{gg^{\prime}}[gg^{\prime}h^{\prime}]
for all
g,g^{\prime}\in G
,
h^{\prime}\in N_{G}(H)/H
,

a,b\in A
and
c\in A^{H}
, where we write
a^{g}
for the result of applying of
g
to
a
.
If
S
is a
G
-set, we consider
A\langle S\rangle
as a left
A\langle G\rangle
-module with the diagonal
G
-action.

The left
A\langle G\rangle
-modules are also called
A
-
semilinear representations
of
G
.

For each set
S
and an integer
s\geq 0
, denote by
\binom{S}{s}
the set of all subsets of
S
of cardinality
s
.
Denote by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{S}
the group of all permutations of the set
S
.

1.2.1.
Notation for fields

For a field
L
and a set
S
,
L(S)
denotes the purely transcendental field
extension of
L
with
S
as a transcendence base (the fraction field of the symmetric

L
-algebra of
L\langle S\rangle
). If a group acts on
L
and on
S
then it acts on
L(S)
as well.
For a subfield
K
of
L
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(L|K)
denotes the group of field automorphisms of
L
identical on
K
.

The labels
\mathrm{a,b,c,d}
and the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-fields
K_{?}
for a label
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c,d}\}

are defined in Notation
1.2
.

For each field
k
and each collection
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in S}
of unital associative
k
-algebras,
indexed by a set
S
, denote by
\bigotimes_{k,~i\in S}A_{i}
the coproduct of
k
-algebras
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in S}
.

For each field
F
, a subfield
k\subseteq F
algebraically closed in
F
, and a set
S
, denote
by
F_{S}=F_{k,S}
the field of fractions of the
k
-algebra
\bigotimes_{k,~i\in S}F
. In particular,
F_{S}=k

if
F=k
;
F(x)_{S}
(here
F(x)
is the field of rational functions over
F
in a variable
x
)
is nothing but the field
F_{S}(S)
of rational functions over
F_{S}
in the variables enumerated
by the set
S
.

Any permutation group of
S
acts smoothly on
F_{S}
.

For each field extension
L|K
and integer
s\geq 0
,
\Omega^{s}_{L|K}:=\bigwedge^{s}_{L}\Omega_{L|K}

denotes the
L
-vector space of differential
s
-forms on
L
over
K
.

1.2.2.
Notation for categories of smooth semilinear representations and their Picard groups

For an associative ring
A
and a permutation group
G

(cf. Definition
2.1
) acting on
A
, denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
the
category of
smooth left
A
-semilinear representations of

G
.
E.g.,
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(1)
is the category of left
A
-modules.

For an object
X
of a Grothendieck category,
E(X)
denotes an injective envelope of
X
.

Remark 1.1
.

If
A
is a division ring and there exists a non-zero object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)

then the
G
-action on
A
is smooth.
[Indeed, for any
A
-semilinear representation
V
of
G
, any non-torsion
v\in V
, any non-zero

a\in A
, one has
\mathrm{Stab}_{av}\cap\mathrm{Stab}_{v}=\mathrm{Stab}_{a}\cap\mathrm{Stab}_{v}
.
If
V
is smooth then the group on the left hand side is open, so the stabilizer

\mathrm{Stab}_{a}
is open, i.e. the
G
-action on
A
is smooth.]

If
K
is a smooth
G
-field then
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is a tensor
k
-linear Grothendieck category, where

k:=K^{G}
. The smooth finite-dimensional
K
-semilinear representations of
G
form a rigid tannakian
category (
K
is the unit,
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(K)=k
; the duals are
V^{\vee}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K}(V,K)
).

Denote by
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
the group of isomorphism classes of invertible objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
,
and set
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}:=\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

1.3.
Results

We are looking for an analogue of Hilbert’s Theorem 90
(cf. Proposition
2.2
) for the symmetric group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
of an infinite set
\Psi
, i.e. we
study the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
of smooth
K
-semilinear representations of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,
where
K
is a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field.

It is shown in Corollary
4.15
that, for any left noetherian associative ring
A

endowed with a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action, the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is locally noetherian.

It is possible to describe the ‘local’ structure of finitely generated objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

(Proposition
4.6
), and it turns out that the morphisms between them are split ‘locally’
(Theorem
4.17
). This is related to a canonical ‘
level
’ filtration on the objects,
introduced in §
4.6
.

1.3.1.
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for the fields
F_{\Psi}
and some of their subfields, the fields
K_{?}

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
F|k
be a field extension such that
k
is algebraically closed in
F
.

We are going to study categories
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for some
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant subfields
K
of
the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
F_{\Psi}
defined in §
1.2.1
, namely
some of those
K
that are subfields
F_{\Psi}^{H}
of the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
F_{\Psi}
fixed
(in an appropriate sense) by the (diagonal) action of discrete or algebraic
k
-groups
H
acting on
F|k
.

It turns out that, for many
F|k
and
H
, the functor from the category of algebraic representation of
H
to

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, given by
V\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle H\rangle}(V,F_{\Psi})
(again, understood properly),
induces a bijection between isomorphism classes of simple finite-dimensional objects of the source and of the target.

Notation 1.2

(The fields
K_{?}
)
.

In what follows, we deal with fields denoted

K_{?}
for the label
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c,d}\}
depending on
\Psi
and
F|k
as above, while
K_{\mathrm{a}}

depends moreover on a finite set
S
and on a subgroup
\Xi
of the free abelian group with basis
S
.

There are inclusions
F_{\Psi}\subset K_{\mathrm{d}}\subset K_{\mathrm{c}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}\subset F(X)_{\Psi}=F_{\Psi}(\Psi)

and
K_{\mathrm{a}}\subseteq F(S)_{\Psi}=F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
.

Let
S
be a finite set, non-empty if
F=k
.
Let
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)=F(S)_{\Psi}
be the purely transcendental
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field extension
of
F_{\Psi}
with a transcendence basis consisting of the variables labeled by the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set

\Psi\times S
, where
u_{s}
denotes the variable corresponding to
(u,s)\in\Psi\times S
.

For all
u\in\Psi
and
\gamma=\sum_{s}m_{s}[j_{s}]\in\Xi
, set
u^{\gamma}:=\prod_{s}u_{j_{s}}^{m_{s}}
.

For each label
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c,d}\}
, we define an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field extension
K_{?}
of
F_{\Psi}
:

K_{\mathrm{a}}\subseteq F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
and
K_{?}\subseteq F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
for
?\in\{\mathrm{b,c,d}\}
.
Thus, they all depend on
\Psi
and
F|k
, and moreover,
K_{\mathrm{a}}
depends also on
S
and a subgroup

\Gamma\subseteq\Xi:=\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle
. The field
K_{\mathrm{a}}
is the fixed subfield of the algebraic group

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\Xi/\Gamma,\mathbb{G}_{m,k})
acting on
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
, while the fields
K_{?}
for

?\in\{\mathrm{b,c,d}\}
are the fixed subfields of, respectively, the algebraic subgroups
\mathbb{G}_{a,k}
,

\mathbb{G}_{a,k}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
,
\mathrm{PGL}_{2,k}
of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2,k}
acting on
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
.

More precisely and explicitly,

K_{\mathrm{a}}=K_{\Psi,S,\Gamma}^{(F|k)}:=F_{\Psi}\left(u^{\gamma},\frac{u_{s}}{v_{s}}~|~\gamma\in\Gamma,~s\in S,~u,v\in\Psi\right)=F_{\Psi}\left(x^{\gamma},\frac{u_{s}}{x_{s}}~|~\gamma\in\Gamma,~s\in S,~u\in\Psi\right),

(so
K_{\Psi,S,\Xi}^{(F|k)}=F(S)_{\Psi};

K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{(F|k)}=F_{\Psi}(u/v~|~u,v\in\Psi);

K_{\Psi,S\sqcup S^{\prime},\Gamma\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle S^{\prime}\rangle}^{(F|k)}=K_{\Psi,S,\Gamma}^{(F(S^{\prime})|k)});

K_{\mathrm{b}}:=F_{\Psi}(u-v~|~u,v\in\Psi)\subset F_{\Psi}(\Psi);\quad K_{\mathrm{c}}:=F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{u-v}{u-w}~|~u,v,w\in\Psi,~\#\{u,v,w\}=3\right)\subset K_{\mathrm{b}};

K_{\mathrm{d}}:=F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{(t-u)(v-w)}{(u-v)(w-t)}~|~t,u,v,w\in\Psi,~\#\{t,u,v,w\}=4\right)\subset K_{\mathrm{c}}\quad\mbox{is the ``cross-ratio'' field}.

Definition 1.3
.

The
Gabriel spectrum
of a Grothendieck category
\mathcal{C}
is
the topological space whose points are isomorphism classes of indecomposable injectives. For each object
X
of

\mathcal{C}
denote by
[X]
the set of points
E
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,E)=0
.
Then base of opens consists of sets of the form
[X]
as
X
ranges over the compact (finitely presentable)
objects. As
[X]\cap[Y]=[X\oplus Y]
, these sets are closed under finite intersection, so an arbitrary
open set will have the form
\bigcup_{i}[X_{i}]
with some compact
X_{i}
.

Some
remarks on Gabriel spectra
are collected in §
5.9
.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
K
be a field endowed
with a non-trivial smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action,
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
be the Gabriel spectrum of
the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. Fix an arbitrary element
x\in\Psi
.

Then, for all integer
s\geq 0
, the injective hulls of the objects

K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
represent
pairwise distinct points
P_{s}=P_{s}^{(K)}
of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
.
Any set containing
P_{s}
for infinitely many
s\geq 0
is dense in
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
.
The closure of
P_{s}
in the set
\{P_{t}~|~t\geq 0\}
is
\{P_{t}~|~0\leq t\leq s\}
if
s\geq s_{0}(K)
, where

[K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}:K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}]=\infty
for any
J
of order
\geq s_{0}(K)
.

Keeping Notation
1.2
, let
p\geq 0
be the characteristic of
k
.

(1)

The simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{?}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are invertible for each
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c}\}
,
but not for
?=\mathrm{d}
.

•

The invertible objects
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\subseteq F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are injective for all
\lambda\in\Xi
.

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\cong\Xi/\Gamma
and its elements
P_{0,\overline{\lambda}}
for all

\overline{\lambda}\in\Xi/\Gamma
are presented by
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
for all
\lambda\in\Xi
.

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}=\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}=0
.

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\cong\mathbb{Z}
is generated by the class of
\Omega^{1}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}\cong(x-y)K_{\mathrm{c}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}
.

(2)

For
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c}\}
, any point of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{?}}
of level
n\geq 0

is contained in the
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{?}}
-orbit of
P_{n}
, trivial if
n>2
.

(3)

The objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
of finite length are semisimple.
For any integer
N\geq 1
, the indecomposable objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
of
length
N
are isomorphic, if
p=0
.

(4)

The injection
E:\mathrm{Pic}_{K}\to\mathrm{Cl}_{K}
,
\mathcal{L}\mapsto E(\mathcal{L})
, to
the set of closed points of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
, commutes with the
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}
-action,
it is bijective for
K=K_{\mathrm{b}}
and for
K=K_{\mathrm{a}}
if
F\neq k
or
\Gamma\neq 0;

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\{P_{1}\}
if

\Xi\neq\Gamma=0
and
F=k

(
in this case any infinite subset of
\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
is dense
)
,

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}
consists of the class
P_{0}
of the
(
non-noetherian, artinian
if and only if
p=0)
object
E(K_{\mathrm{b}})=K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\subset F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
,

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}
is the union of the free
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}
-orbits of
P_{0}
and of
P_{1}
,
where
P_{0}
is the class of
E(K_{\mathrm{c}})=K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]\subset F_{\Psi}(\Psi)

and
P_{1}
is the class of
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi\rangle
.

(5)

For each
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b,c}\}
, the non-closed points of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{?}}

are represented by noetherian objects. The non-closed points are
P_{2},P_{3},\dots
and

•

P_{1}
if either
?=\mathrm{c}
or

?=\mathrm{b}
and
F\neq k
or
?=\mathrm{a}
and
\Gamma\neq 0
,

•

the class
P_{2}^{\prime}
of
(x-y)\cdot K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle

if
?=\mathrm{c}
,
F=k
and
p\neq 2
.

If, for an integer
s\geq 1
,
K_{?}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is not injective then

?\in\{\mathrm{b,c}\}
,
p=2
,
s=2
,
F=k
and

E(K_{?}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle)\cong K_{?}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
,
where
\Delta_{\Psi}
is the diagonal in
\Psi^{2}
.

(6)

For
?\in\{\mathrm{a,b}\}
and any
s>0
, the closure of
P_{s}
is the set

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{?}}\cup\{P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{s}\}
.

For
?=\mathrm{c}
, the closure of
P_{s}

(
for
s\geq 2)
is

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\cup\{P_{1,n}~|~n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{P_{2},\dots,P_{s}\}
if
s>2
and
p=2
,

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\cup\{P_{1,n}~|~n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\cup\{P_{2}^{\prime},P_{2},\dots,P_{s}\}
if
s>2
and
p\neq 2
,

•

\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\cup\{P_{1,n}~|~(-1)^{n}=1\mbox{ {\rm in} }k\}\cup\{P_{2}\}
if
s=2;

the closure of
P_{1,n}
is
\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\cup\{P_{1,n}\}
.

The fact that the points
P_{s}^{(K)}
are well-defined and pairwise distinct is explained in Remark
4.7
.
The density of infinite sets of
P_{n}
’s is shown in Lemma
5.38
(
1
).

In terminology of
[
14
, §4.20, p.295]
, the categories

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for
\Gamma=\Xi
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are
local
,
while
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for
\Gamma\neq\Xi
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are not.

Example 1.5
.

For an
F|k
as above, the points of the Gabriel spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

are
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\cong\Omega^{s}_{F_{\Psi}(\Psi)|F_{\Psi}}
(the space
of differential
s
-forms on
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
over
F_{\Psi}
) for all integer
s\geq 0
.

Theorem
1.4
provides several examples of fields
K
and a non-precompact group

G
of their automorphisms such that the Gabriel spectrum (and in particular the simple objects, cf.
Lemma
5.37
) of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
admits
an explicit description. In all examples
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, though the description depends
crucially on the “type” (but not that essentially on the characteristic!) of the field
K
.

Though Theorem
1.4
avoids description of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, the finite-dimensional simple
objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are described in Proposition
5.23
.
Namely, the valuations of
K_{\mathrm{c}}
trivial on
K_{\mathrm{d}}
can be considered as points of the projective line
\mathbb{Y}

over
K_{\mathrm{d}}
endowed with a natural
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action, while any finite-dimensional simple object is isomorphic
to the socle of the module of global sections of some plurianticanonical sheaf on
\mathbb{Y}
. These objects
are self-dual. Their dimensions are arbitrary odd integers if the characteristic
p
of
k
is 0, and are
arbitrary products of positive integers
\leq p
if
p>0
(which is a version of the Steinberg’s
tensor product theorem for
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
).
It is also shown that there are infinite-dimensional simple objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Remarks 1.6
.

(1)

If
k
is a trivial

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field of characteristic 0 then
k
is an injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
cf. Theorem
A.17
.
However, this is not the case if
\mathrm{char}(k)>0
, cf. Proposition
5.39
.

(2)

Proposition
2.2
below implies that the pair
(G,K)
cannot be reconstructed from the
category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. However, for a given set
\Psi
, one can reconstruct the field
K_{\mathrm{a}}
with torsion
free
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
from the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
as follows. The field
k
is the
endomorphism ring of any closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
. Let
S
be a basis of
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}
.
If there is a (unique) point
I_{1}
of

\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
whose closure is
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}\sqcup\{I_{1}\}
(i.e. either
F\neq k
or

\Gamma\neq 0
) then
L:=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})}(I_{1})
is the fraction field of the group

F
-algebra of
\Gamma
, and
K_{\mathrm{a}}\cong K_{\Psi,S,0}^{(L|k)}
. Otherwise,
K_{\mathrm{a}}\cong K_{\Psi,S,0}^{(k|k)}
.

(3)

Let
G
be a permutation group and
L|K
be a smooth
G
-field extension. Then

•

any injective object of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L}(G)
is injective as an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
;

•

any simple object
W
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)

can be embedded into any simple quotient in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L}(G)
of the object
L\otimes_{K}W
.

Corollary 1.7
.

Any smooth irreducible representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
over a field
k
can be embedded into

(x-y)^{-n}k\left(\frac{u-v}{u-w}~|~u,v,w\in\Psi\right)\subset x^{-n}k\left(\frac{u}{v}~|~u,v\in\Psi\right)\subset k(\Psi)

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
x,y\in\Psi
.

Proof.

This follows from Remark
1.6
(
•
‣
3
) and
Theorem
1.4
(
1
). ∎

1.3.2.

Given a smooth
G
-field, it is natural to study its
G
-field extensions and
G
-invariant subfields, and
to compare semilinear representations of
G
over such
G
-fields.

In general, any smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
K
admits a ‘weak period’ extension, i.e. a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
extension
\widetilde{K}|K
such that
\widetilde{K}
is a cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(Proposition
4.28
),
while any such
\widetilde{K}
admits a
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-subfield isomorphic to
k(\Psi)
(Example
2.5
),
where
k=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
. However,
\widetilde{K}
constructed in Proposition
4.28
is not optimal,
in the sense that
\widetilde{K}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
.

In the setting of Theorem
1.4
,
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
is a minimal period extension
of
K_{\mathrm{a}}
, while
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
is a minimal period extension of
K_{\mathrm{d}}
(as well as of
K_{\mathrm{b}}
and of
K_{\mathrm{c}}
).

For a constant field
k
, some
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant subfields of its ‘minimal period’ extension

k(\Psi)
(and of its slightly more general version
F_{\Psi}
, for a transcendence degree 1 field extension

F|k
) can be constructed as
L_{\Psi}^{H}
for (closed) subgroups
H
of automorphisms of intermediate subfields

L
in
F|k
(e.g.
H
is a subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2,k}
if
L\cong k(X)
). Theorem
5.13
shows
that, in characteristic 0, if
K\neq k
is an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant field extension of
k
in
F_{\Psi}
then
(i) the transcendence degree
d
of
F_{\Psi}
over
K
is
\leq 3
, and
(ii) ‘essentially’,
K
is obtained by the above construction when

d\geq 1
(and its isomorphism class depends only on
d
if
d\geq 2
, while the case
d=1
admits more options).

The case
d=0
is also interesting, since it is there where there exist smooth
irreducible semilinear representations of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
of finite dimensions
>1
(namely,

2,\ 3,\ 4,\ 5
, and
q
if
k
contains
\mathbb{F}_{q}
), cf. Examples
5.25
,

5.31
,
5.32
.
In particular, though in Theorem
1.4

all points of a given level form a
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{?}}
-orbit, this is not the case if
K_{?}
is replaced by
an arbitrary
K
, even in level 0.

As for smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field extensions
L|K
with
L^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
,
Example
5.28
lists some of
K
with no non-trivial finite smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
extensions, while
Proposition
5.33
shows that the only ‘isotrivial’ finitely generated
L|F_{\Psi}
is
trivial:
L=F_{\Psi}
.

1.3.3.

In the appendix, for
\Psi
being either a countable set or a vector space over a finite field, some
results on the category of smooth representations over a field of characteristic 0 of the automorphism
group of
\Psi
are proved. It is shown (Lemma
A.8
) that this category is locally of
finite length, while the principal result (Theorem
A.17
)
is a bijective correspondence between the simple objects and the indecomposable injectives
for this category (resulting in a description of the Gabriel spectrum of this category).

2.
Hilbert’s theorem 90 and period fields

Definition 2.1
.

A
permutation group
is a Hausdorff topological group
G

admitting a base of open subsets consisting of the left and right shifts of subgroups.

If we denote by
B
a collection of open subgroups such that the finite intersections of conjugates
of elements of
B
form a base of open neighbourhoods of
1
in
G
(e.g., the set of all open
subgroups of
G
), then
G
acts faithfully on the set
\Psi:=\coprod_{U\in B}G/U
, so (i)

G
becomes a
permutation group of the set

\Psi
, (ii) the
shifts of the pointwise stabilizers
G_{T}
of the finite subsets
T\subset\Psi

form a base of the topology of
G
. Clearly,
G
is totally disconnected.

As we mainly deal not with a permutation group
G
itself, but rather with smooth
G
-sets (or representations, etc.),
we may replace
G
by the
prodiscrete left cancellative semigroup

\varprojlim_{U\in B}G/U
.

It is easy to see that, for any collection of smooth
G
-sets and any collection of finitary relations
on them, their common stabilizer in
G
is closed.
In particular, if
\Psi
is such a structure as a group, ring, field,
module (over a ‘constant’ ring), affine or projective space, etc. then
its automorphism group is a closed subgroup of the symmetric group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.

The following is a version of Speiser’s generalization of Hilbert’s theorem 90,
cf.
[
22
, Satz 1]
, or
[
21
, Prop. 3, p.159]
, or
[
4
, Proposition 5]
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
K
be a field of characteristic
p\geq 0
endowed with a smooth
action of a permutation group
G
, and
H\subseteq G
be a closed normal subgroup. Then

•

the functor
\Gamma_{H}:\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(G/H)
,
V\mapsto V^{H}
, is an equivalence if and only
if
H
acts on
K
faithfully and
H
is precompact, i.e., any open subgroup of
H
is of finite index;

•

the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is semisimple if and only if
G
is precompact
and
p
divides index of no open subgroup
of the action kernel
N:=\ker[G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(K)]
.

Proof.

As it is noted after Definition
2.1
,
G
is a permutation group
of a set
\Psi
, so
K\langle\Psi\rangle
is a smooth faithful representation of
G
.
If
\Gamma_{H}
is an equivalence then its left adjoint
K\otimes_{K^{H}}(-)
should be quasi-inverse.
Obviously, the adjunction map
K\otimes_{K^{H}}K\langle\Psi\rangle^{H}\to K\langle\Psi\rangle
is surjective
only if the
H
-action on
K
is faithful. On the other hand,
K\langle G/U\rangle^{H}\neq 0
for each open
subgroup
U\subseteq G
, so
U\cap H
is of finite index in
H
, i.e.
H
is precompact.

If
G
is finite then
[
22
, Satz 1]
, appropriately reformulated, implies that any
K
-semilinear
representation of
G
is a sum of copies of
K
. Namely, with
k:=K^{G}
, the field extension
K|k
is finite,
so the multiplication and the natural
G
-action on
K
give rise to a
k
-algebra homomorphism from

K\langle G\rangle
to the algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k}(K)
of endomorphisms of
K
considered as
k
-vector space,
which is (a) surjective by Jacobson’s density theorem and (b) injective by the independence of characters.
Then any
K\langle G\rangle
-module is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
K
.

If
H
is precompact and faithful on
K
, we have to check that the adjunction map

K\otimes_{K^{H}}V^{H}\xrightarrow{\xi}V
is surjective for any object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.
For each
v\in V
, consider the intersection
S
of all conjugates of the stabilizer of
v
in
H
.
Thus,
v
is contained in the
K^{S}
-semilinear representation
V^{S}
of the group
H/S
.
As
H/S
is finite and the action of
H/S
on
K^{S}
is faithful (cf.
[
8
, p.151]
),

V^{S}=K^{S}\otimes_{(K^{S})^{H/S}}(V^{S})^{H/S}=K^{S}\otimes_{K^{H}}V^{H}
, i.e.,
v
is contained in the image of
\xi
.

If
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is semisimple then the projection
\pi_{U}:K\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{[g]\mapsto 1}K
,
splits for any open subgroup
U\subseteq G
, so
K\langle G/U\rangle^{G}
contains some
\alpha\neq 0
, and thus,

[G:U]<\infty
, i.e.
G
is precompact. Then
\alpha=\sum_{i}a_{i}\sum_{[g]\in O_{i}}[g]
, where
O_{i}

are
N
-orbits on
G/U
, so
O_{i}\cong N/(U\cap N)
, and therefore,
\pi_{U}(\alpha)=\#(N/(U\cap N))\sum_{i}a_{i}
.
Then
\pi_{U}(\alpha)\neq 0
only if
p\nmid[N:U\cap N]
.

Conversely, under our assumption on
N
, the categories
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(N)
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G/N)
are semisimple,
so for any
V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
the functors
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(N)}(V,-):\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G/N)

and
\Gamma_{G/N}:\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G/N)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(1)
are exact. Then their composition

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)}(V,-):\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(1)
is exact as well, so

\mathrm{Ext}^{>0}_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)}(-,-)=0
, i.e.,
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is semisimple. ∎

Example 2.3
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite-dimensional vector space over a finite field,

U\subset\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
be an open subgroup. There is a unique maximal compact normal subgroup
H
of
U
.
Namely, let
J
be the minimal subspace of
\Psi
such that
U
contains be the pointwise stabilizer of
J
in

\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
(equivalently,
J
is the maximal finite-dimensional subspace of
\Psi
stabilized by
U
).
Let
K
be a field endowed with a smooth
U
-action faithful on
H
. Then
U/H=\mathrm{PGL}(\Psi/J)
and

\Gamma_{H}:\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(U)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(\mathrm{PGL}(\Psi/J))
,
V\mapsto V^{H}
, is an equivalence of categories.

Similarly (and simpler), if
\Psi
is an infinite set and
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
be an open subgroup,

K
be a field endowed with a smooth and faithful
U
-action
then
\Gamma_{H}:\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(U)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi\smallsetminus J})
,
V\mapsto V^{H}
,
is an equivalence of categories, where
J
is the maximal finite subset of
\Psi

stabilized by
U
, and
H
is the maximal finite normal subgroup of
U
.

Recall that a family
\mathcal{S}
of objects in a category is
generating
(resp.,
cogenerating
)
if any pair of distinct morphisms
g_{1},g_{2}:X\rightrightarrows Y
admits a morphism
\theta:S\to X
(resp.,

\theta:Y\to S
) with
S\in\mathcal{S}
such that
g_{1}\circ\theta\neq g_{2}\circ\theta
(resp.,

\theta\circ g_{1}\neq\theta\circ g_{2}
).
For a Grothendieck category, a family
\mathcal{S}
is generating (resp., cogenerating)
if and only if any object is
a quotient of a direct sum
(resp.,

a subobject of a direct product
) of objects in
\mathcal{S}
. An object
U
is called
a
generator
(resp.,
cogenerator
) if the family
\{U\}
generating (resp., cogenerating).

Definition 2.4
.

For a permutation group
G
and a smooth
G
-field
F
,
a smooth
G
-field extension
K|F
is called a
weak
G
-period extension
of
F

if
K
is a cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.

If, moreover,
K^{G}=F^{G}
then
K
is called a (strong)
G
-period extension
of
F
.

The first natural questions are: Given a permutation group
G
and
a smooth
G
-field
F
, does there exist a
G
-period extension of
F
? Can it be chosen to be
‘minimal’ in some sense?

Example 2.5
.

Let
k
be a field and
K|k
be a weak
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-period
extension. Then
K
contains a
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-subfield isomorphic to
k(\Psi)
, so
k(\Psi)

can be considered as a minimal
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-period extension of
k
.

Proof.

Fix some
x\in\Psi
. There should exist an embedding of
K\langle\Psi\rangle
into
a product of copies of
K
, so
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}}
.
As
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}
is a maximal proper subgroup of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, fixing an element of

K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}}\smallsetminus K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
, we identify the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set
\Psi
with an

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbit in
K
. Suppose that some pairwise distinct elements
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\Psi\subset K

are algebraically dependent over
k
, i.e.
F(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=0
for a non-zero polynomial
F
over
k
,
and
n\geq 2
is minimal. Then
F(x_{1}^{g},\dots,x_{n}^{g})=0
for any
g\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. The

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\}}
-orbit of
x_{1}
, i.e.
\Psi\setminus\{x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\}
,
gives infinitely many solutions of the equation
F(x,x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=0
,
while it has at most
\deg F
solutions. This is contradiction. ∎

3.
Open subgroups, permutation modules, generators and projectives

A permutation group
G
is called
unibased
if, for any open proper subgroup,
the finite intersections of its conjugates form a base of open subgroups of
G
.

A reason to introduce this class is a ‘simplicity’ property (Proposition
3.3
):
any non-zero Grothendieck subcategory of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is equivalent to
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
itself.

Example 3.1
.

The following groups are unibased.

(i) Simple finite groups.

(ii) Let
G
be the automorphism group modulo centre of one of the following structures:
an infinite set
\Psi
(i.e.
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
), an infinite-dimensional vector space

V
(i.e.
G=\mathrm{PGL}(V)
), an algebraically closed field extension
F|k
of infinite
transcendence degree of an algebraically closed field
k
(i.e.
G=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)
).

Lemma 3.2
.

Any unibased group
G
is topologically simple; its topology is induced by any non-trivial
continuous homomorphism
\varphi
from
G
to a permutation group. In particular, a permutation group is unibased
if and only if it is topologically simple and
minimal
in the sense of
[
23
]
.

Proof.

If
S
is a non-trivial closed normal proper subgroup of
G
, fix an element

g\in G\smallsetminus S
and a neighbourhood of
g
in
G\smallsetminus S
. We may choose this neighbourhood
of the form
gU
for an open subgroup
U
of
G
. As
g\notin SU
,
SU
is a proper open subgroup of

G
, while all conjugates of
SU
contain
S
, and thus, cannot generate a Hausdorff topology on
G
.

The target of
\varphi
admits an open subgroup
U
that does not contain the image
of
\varphi
, so
\varphi^{-1}(U)
is an open proper subgroup of
G
. ∎

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
G
be a unibased group, and
K
be a non-trivial smooth
G
-field.
Then
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is equivalent to its arbitrary
non-zero subcategory closed under direct products and subquotients.

Proof.

Let
V\neq 0
be an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. Consider
G
as a subgroup
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(K)
. The objects
K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
for all finite subsets
T\subseteq K

such that
V^{G_{T}}\neq 0
form a system of generators of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, so it suffices to show
that there is an embedding of
K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
into a direct product of copies of
V
, or equivalently,
that there is a family of morphisms
K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\to V
with vanishing common kernel.

Fix a nonzero
\alpha\in V^{G_{T}}
and consider, for all
t\in K^{G_{T}}
, the morphisms

t\alpha:K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\to V
,
a[\sigma]\mapsto a(t\alpha)^{\sigma}
, for all
a\in K
and
\sigma\in G
.
Let
\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}\sigma_{i}\in K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
be an element in the common kernel
of these morphisms. The elements of the set
G/G_{T}
can be considered as (pairwise distinct)

K^{\times}
-valued characters of the group
(K^{G_{T}})^{\times}
, since
G_{K^{G_{T}}}=G_{T}
, so
the element
\xi
can be considered as a
K
-linear relation between characters. Due to the
linear independence of such characters, one has
a_{1}=\dots=a_{N}=0
,
i.e.,
\bigcap_{t\in(K^{G_{T}})^{\times}}\ker(K\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\xrightarrow{t\alpha}V)=0
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
G
be a permutation group, and
K
be a
G
-field. Assume that for
any open subgroup
U\subset G
there is an open subgroup
U^{\prime}\subset U
such that each conjugate of

U^{\prime}
meets
U
in a subgroup of infinite index in
U
.
Then there are no non-zero projectives in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.

Proof.

Let
P\neq 0
be an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. Fix a non-zero
e\in P
. For
each
u\in P
, fix an open subgroup
U_{u}\subset\mathrm{Stab}_{e}\cap\mathrm{Stab}_{u}
such that
each conjugate of
U_{u}
meets
\mathrm{Stab}_{e}
in a subgroup of infinite index. Consider the
surjection
\bigoplus_{u\in P}K\langle G/U_{u}\rangle\xrightarrow{\pi}P
,
[g]_{u}\mapsto gu
. If
the support of an element in
K\langle G/U_{u}\rangle^{\mathrm{Stab}_{e}}
contains
[g]\in G/U_{u}

then it contains
\mathrm{Stab}_{e}gU_{u}/U_{u}
. This set is of the same cardinality as

g^{-1}\mathrm{Stab}_{e}gU_{u}/U_{u}=g^{-1}\mathrm{Stab}_{e}g/(g^{-1}\mathrm{Stab}_{e}g\cap U_{u})\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{e}/(\mathrm{Stab}_{e}\cap gU_{u}g^{-1})
. As the latter set is infinite,

K\langle G/U_{u}\rangle^{\mathrm{Stab}_{e}}=0
, so
\pi
does not split, and thus,
P
is not projective. ∎

Example 3.5
.

The automorphism groups of infinite sets, infinite-dimensional vector spaces,
algebraically closed field extensions
F|k
of infinite transcendence degree
satisfy assumptions of Lemma
3.4
.

For any set
\Psi
and a subset
T\subseteq\Psi
, we denote by

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}
the pointwise stabilizer of
T
in the group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.
Let
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,T}:=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi\smallsetminus T}\times\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{T}
denote the
group of all permutations of
\Psi
preserving
T
(in other words,
the setwise stabilizer of
T
in the group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, or
equivalently, the normalizer of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}
in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
).

3.1.
Substructures

Let
G\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
be a permutation group of a set
\Psi
.

For a subset
S\subset\Psi
, (i) we denote by
G_{S}
the pointwise stabilizer of
the set
S
; (ii) we call the fixed set
\Psi^{G_{S}}
the
G
-
closure
of
S
.
We say that a subset
S\subset\Psi
is
G
-
closed
if
S=\Psi^{G_{S}}
.

Any intersection
\bigcap_{i}S_{i}
of
G
-closed sets
S_{i}
is
G
-closed:
as
G_{S_{i}}\subseteq G_{\bigcap_{j}S_{j}}
, one has
G_{S_{i}}s=s
for any

s\in\Psi^{G_{\bigcap_{j}S_{j}}}
, so
s\in\Psi^{G_{S_{i}}}=S_{i}
for any
i
, and thus,

s\in\bigcap_{i}S_{i}
. This implies that the subgroup generated by
G_{S_{i}}
’s
is dense in
G_{\bigcap_{i}S_{i}}
(and coincides with
G_{\bigcap_{i}S_{i}}
if
at least one of
G_{S_{i}}
’s is open).

The
G
-closed subsets of
\Psi
form a small category with the
morphisms being all those embeddings that are induced by elements of
G
:

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,Y):=\{g\in G~|~g(X)\subseteq Y\}/G_{X}
.

For a
G
-closed subset
T\subset\Psi
,
(hiding
G
and
\Psi
from notation) set
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T):=N_{G}(G_{T})/G_{T}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
\Psi
be either
(i)
an infinite set, or
(ii)
an
infinite-dimensional vector space over a field, or
(iii)
an algebraically closed field extension of
a field
k
of infinite transcendence degree. Let
G
be the automorphism group of
\Psi
, i.e. either

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, or
\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
, or the field automorphism group of
\Psi
over
k
. For an integer

n
, let
T\subset\Psi
be a subset of order
n
, resp. a subspace of dimension
n
, resp. a subfield of
transcendence degree
n
over
k
. Then there exist elements
g_{0},\dots,g_{n}\in G
such that

G=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n}G_{T}g_{i}G_{T}g_{i}^{-1}
.

Let, in the cases
(i)
and
(ii)
,
T^{\prime}\subset\Psi
be a finite

G
-closed subset, while
T,T^{\prime}
be algebraically closed subfields in
\Psi|k
of finite transcendence degree
in the case
(iii)
. Then the subgroups
G_{T}
and
G_{T^{\prime}}
generate the subgroup
G_{T\cap T^{\prime}}
.
If
T\subsetneqq T^{\prime}
and
V^{G_{T}}=V^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
for a
G
-set
V
then
V^{G_{T}}=V^{G}
.

Proof.

Choose
n+1
subsets (resp. subspaces, resp. subfields) in general position
T_{0},\dots,T_{n}

isomorphic to
T
, i.e.
\#(T\cup\bigcup_{i=0}^{n}T_{i})=(n+2)n
(resp.
\dim(T+\sum_{i=0}^{n}T_{i})=(n+2)n
,
resp.
\mathrm{tr.deg}(TT_{0}\cdots T_{n}|k)=(n+2)n
). Looking at the
G_{T}
-orbit of the identical embedding

T_{i}\hookrightarrow\Psi
, we see that
G_{T}G_{T_{i}}=\{g\in G~|~g(T)\cap T_{i}=\varnothing\}
(resp.

G_{T}G_{T_{i}}=\{g\in G~|~\dim(g(T)+T_{i})=2n\}
, resp.

G_{T}G_{T_{i}}=\{g\in G~|~\mathrm{tr.deg}(g(T)T_{i}|k)=2n\}
), so
G=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n}G_{T}g_{i}G_{T}g_{i}^{-1}

for elements
g_{0},\dots,g_{n}\in G
such that
g_{i}(T)=T_{i}
for all
i
.

More generally, let us show that
G_{T}G_{T^{\prime}}=\{g\in G_{T\cap T^{\prime}}~|~g(T^{\prime})\cap T=T\cap T^{\prime}\}=:\Xi
.
The inclusion
\subseteq
is trivial. On the other hand, the set
\Xi/G_{T^{\prime}}=G_{T}/(G_{T}\cap G_{T^{\prime}})

consists of all embeddings of
T\cup T^{\prime}
(or rather of its
G
-closure) into
\Psi
identical on
T

that are induced by elements of
G
, while such embeddings form a
G_{T}
-orbit.

Let
T_{0}\subseteq T
be a minimal
G
-closed subset such that
V^{G_{T}}=V^{G_{T_{0}}}
. Assuming

T_{0}
is not initial (i.e.
\neq\varnothing
or 0), let
g\in N_{G}(G_{T^{\prime}})
be an element such that

g(T_{0})\neq T_{0}
. Then

V^{G_{T^{\prime}}}=V^{G_{T^{\prime}}}\cap g(V^{G_{T^{\prime}}})=V^{G_{T_{0}}}\cap g(V^{G_{T_{0}}})=V^{G_{T_{0}}}\cap V^{G_{g(T_{0})}}=V^{\langle G_{T_{0}},G_{g(T_{0})}\rangle}=V^{G_{T_{0}\cap g(T_{0})}},

contradicting the minimality of
T_{0}
. ∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
G
be either
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
for an infinite set
\Psi
, or

\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\Psi)
for an infinite-dimensional vector space
\Psi
over a finite field

\mathbb{F}_{q}
. For any open subgroup
U
of
G
there exists a unique
G
-closed subset
T\subset\Psi

such that
G_{T}\subseteq U
and the following equivalent conditions hold:
(a)

T
is minimal;
(b)

G_{T}
is normal in
U
;
(c)

G_{T}
is of finite index in
U
. In particular,

(i)
such
T
is finite,
(ii)
the open subgroups of
G
correspond bijectively to the pairs

(T,H)
consisting of a finite
G
-closed subset
T\subset\Psi
and a subgroup
H\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)
under

(T,H)\mapsto\{g\in N_{G}(G_{T})~|~\text{{\rm restriction of $g$ to $T$ belongs to $H$}}\}
.

Proof.

Any open subgroup
U
in
G
contains the subgroup
G_{T}
for a finite
G
-closed
subset
T\subset\Psi
. Assume that
T
is chosen to be minimal. If
\sigma\in U
then

U\supseteq\sigma G_{T}\sigma^{-1}=G_{\sigma(T)}
, and therefore, (i)
\sigma(T)
is also minimal, (ii)

U
contains the subgroup generated by
G_{\sigma(T)}
and
G_{T}
. By Lemma
3.6
, the
subgroup generated by
G_{\sigma(T)}
and
G_{T}
is
G_{T\cap\sigma(T)}
and thus,
U
contains the subgroup

G_{T\cap\sigma(T)}
. The minimality of
T
means that
T=\sigma(T)
, i.e.,
U\subseteq N_{G}(G_{T})
. If

T^{\prime}\subset\Psi
is another minimal subset such that
G_{T^{\prime}}\subseteq U
then, by Lemma
3.6
,

G_{T\cap T^{\prime}}\subseteq U
, so
T=T^{\prime}
, which proves (b) and (the uniqueness in the case) (a). It follows
from (b) that
G_{T}\subseteq U\subseteq N_{G}(G_{T})
, so
G_{T}
is of finite index in
U
. As the subgroups

G_{T}
and
G_{T^{\prime}}
are not commensurable for
T^{\prime}\neq T
, we get the uniqueness in the case (c). ∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
G
be a group acting on a field
K
. Let
U\subset G

be a subgroup of infinite index, and
V
be a
K\langle U\rangle
-module. Let
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
be
a
G
-invariant subfield. Then there are no non-zero
K^{\prime}\langle G\rangle
-submodules in

K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V
finite-dimensional over
K^{\prime}
.

Example. If
V=K
then
K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V\xrightarrow{\sim}K\langle G/U\rangle
,

a[g]\otimes f\mapsto af^{g}[g]
.

Proof.

One has a canonical decomposition

K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V=\bigoplus_{[g]\in G/U}V_{[g]}
into a direct sum of
K
-vector
spaces, where
V_{[g]}=K\langle gU\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V
. By the
support
of an element

\alpha=(\alpha_{[g]})_{[g]\in G/U}\in K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V
we mean the subset of

G/U
consisting of those
[g]\in G/U
for which
\alpha_{[g]}\neq 0
. Let

V^{\prime}\subseteq K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle U\rangle}V
be a
K^{\prime}\langle G\rangle
-submodule finite-dimensional
over
K^{\prime}
, and
S\subset G/U
be the union of the supports in
G/U
of all elements of
V^{\prime}
. Then
S
is finite
and
G
-stable. However, the only finite and
G
-stable subset of
G/U
is empty, so
V^{\prime}=0
. ∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
G
be a group acting on a field
K
. Then the following conditions
on a subgroup
U\subseteq G
are equivalent:

•

an element
g\in G
acts identically on
K^{U}

if and only if
g\in U
;

•

K\langle G/U\rangle
embeds into a product of copies of
K
.

Assume that
U
satisfies these conditions, and
V\xrightarrow{\lambda}K

is a morphism of
K\langle G\rangle
-modules inducing a non-zero map
V^{U}\to K^{U}
.
Then
K\langle G/U\rangle
embeds into a product of copies of
V
.

Proof.

For any
K\langle G\rangle
-module
V
, define a morphism of
K\langle G\rangle
-modules

K\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{\varphi_{V}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(V^{U},V)
by
\sum_{g}b_{g}[g]\mapsto[v\mapsto\sum_{g}b_{g}v^{g}]
.
The first condition means that the elements
[g]\in G/U
can be considered as certain pairwise distinct
one-dimensional characters
\chi_{[g]}:(K^{U})^{\times}\to K^{\times}
. By Artin’s independence of
characters theorem, the characters
\chi_{[g]}
are linearly independent in the
K
-vector space of all
functions
(K^{U})^{\times}\to K
, so
\varphi_{K}
is injective. Conversely, if
\varphi_{K}
is injective then

\chi_{[g]}\neq\chi_{[1]}
for any
[g]\in G/U\smallsetminus\{[1]\}
, i.e.
g
acts non-trivially on
K^{U}
.

Fix some
v\in V^{U}
with
\lambda(v)=1
. Then the composition

K\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{\varphi_{V}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(V^{U},V)\xrightarrow{\lambda}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(V^{U},K)\xrightarrow{|_{K^{U}\cdot v}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(K^{U}\cdot v,K)\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K^{G}}(K^{U},K)

coincides with
\varphi_{K}
, which is injective. ∎

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
G
be a group,
K
be a
G
-field,
k\subseteq K^{G}
be a subfield,

F|k
be a field extension. Assume that
k
is algebraically closed in
K
.
Let
L
be the fraction field of
K\otimes_{k}F
.

Then any simple
K\langle G\rangle
-submodule
M
of
L
coincides with

K\cdot a
for some non-zero
a\in L^{G}
.

Proof.

Let
Q\in L^{\times}
be a non-zero element of
M
, so
Q=\alpha/\beta
is a ratio of
a pair of elements
\alpha,\beta\in K\otimes_{k}A
for a finitely generated
k
-subalgebra
A
of
F
.
There is a finite field extension
k^{\prime}|k
and a
k
-algebra homomorphism
\varphi:A\to k^{\prime}
with
a non-zero image of
\alpha\beta
under the
G
-equivariant
K
-algebra homomorphism

\Phi:K\otimes_{k}A\to K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
,
\sum_{i}b_{i}\otimes a_{i}\mapsto\sum_{i}b_{i}\varphi(a_{i})
.
The homomorphism
\Phi
extends to
(K\otimes_{k}A)[g\beta^{-1}~|~g\in G]
with a non-zero restriction to a
morphism of left
K\langle G\rangle
-modules
M\to K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}\cong\bigoplus_{\text{a $k$-basis of $[k^{\prime}:k]$}}K
,
so
M\cong K
, and thus,
M=K\cdot a
for some non-zero
a\in M^{G}\subseteq L^{G}
. ∎

Remark 3.11
.

If
G
has no open proper subgroups of finite index (e.g. if

G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
for an infinite set
\Psi
) and
K
is smooth then
K^{G}
is algebraically closed in
K
:
any finite extension of
K^{G}
in
K
is fixed by an open subgroup of
G
of finite index, i.e. by
G
.

If
K^{G}
is algebraically closed in
K
then all
G
-fields
L
in Lemma
3.10
come from
the particular case
k=K^{G}
, since
L
is the fraction field of
K\otimes_{K^{G}}\tilde{F}
,
where
\tilde{F}
is the fraction field of
K^{G}\otimes_{k}F
.

Corollary 3.12
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
J\subset\Psi
be a subset
with infinite complement. Let
F
be a field and
k\subsetneqq F
be a subfield algebraically
closed in
F
. Then any simple
F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-submodule

M
of
F_{\Psi}
coincides with
aF_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}
for some
a\in F_{J}^{\times}
.
In particular,
M
is isomorphic to
F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}
.

Proof.

This is Lemma
3.10
for
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
,

K=F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}
,
k=K^{G}
, with
F
replaced by
F_{J}
(so
L=F_{\Psi}
). ∎

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
G
be a group acting on a field

K
. Let
U\subset G
be a subgroup of infinite index such that an element

g\in G
acts identically on
K^{U}
if and only if
g\in U
. Set
k:=K^{G}
. Let
F|k
be a field extension
such that
k
is algebraically closed either in
F
or in
K
. Let
L
be the fraction field of
K\otimes_{k}F
.
Then

•

L\langle G/U\rangle
contains no simple
K\langle G\rangle
-submodules;

•

for each integer
s\geq 0
, there is a natural bijection
between
s
-dimensional
k
-vector subspaces
\Xi
in
K^{U}
and
K\langle G\rangle
-submodules
V_{\Xi}

in
K\langle G/U\rangle
such that
K\langle G/U\rangle/V_{\Xi}\cong K^{s}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.9
,
L\langle G/U\rangle
embeds into a product of
copies of
L
, so any simple
K\langle G\rangle
-submodule
V
of
L\langle G/U\rangle
embeds into
L
.
By Lemma
3.10
,
V
is isomorphic to
K
, contradicting Lemma
3.8
.

The image of
K\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{\varphi_{K}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(K^{U},K)
is dense, which means that
for any finite-dimensional
k
-vector subspace
\Xi\subset K^{U}
the composition

K\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{\varphi_{K}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(K^{U},K)\xrightarrow{\text{restriction to $\Xi$}}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(\Xi,K)

is surjective: otherwise there was a non-zero element

\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes f_{i}\in K\otimes_{k}\Xi
vanishing on the image, i.e.,
\sum_{i}a_{i}\otimes f_{i}^{g}=0
for all

g\in G/U
, contradicting linear independence of characters
(K^{U})^{\times}\to K^{\times}
.

To each
k
-vector subspace
\Xi\subset K^{U}
we associate the
K\langle G\rangle
-submodule

V_{\Xi}:=\bigcap_{\xi\in\Xi}\ker\xi
, where all
\xi
’s are considered as morphisms
K\langle G/U\rangle\to K
.
Conversely, to each
K\langle G\rangle
-submodule
V\subset K\langle G/U\rangle
we associate
the
k
-vector subspace
\Xi_{V}:=\bigcap_{v\in V}\ker v\subset K^{U}
, where all
v
’s are considered as

k
-linear maps
K^{U}\to K
. ∎

Example.
Let
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
and
U\subset\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
be a maximal proper subgroup, i.e.,
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}

for a finite non-empty subset
I\subset\Psi
(so
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U
can be identified with the set
\binom{\Psi}{\#I}
).
Suppose that
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}}\neq k
. Then we are under assumptions of Lemma
3.13
, so there
are no irreducible
K
-semilinear subrepresentations in
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{\#I}\rangle
.

Remarks 3.14
.

(1)

Let

G
be as in Lemma
3.7
. It follow from the explicit description of open subgroups
in Lemma
3.7
that index of any proper open subgroup
U
in
G
is infinite.

One cannot claim that, for an arbitrary smooth
K
, there are no
elements in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\smallsetminus U
acting identically on
K^{U}
, even if
K^{U}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
.
For instance, if
K=F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
for
s>1
,
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}
for a finite

S
,
\#S=s
, then
K^{U}\cong F
, but
H=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}
acts trivially on
K^{U}
.

However, this is the case if
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
, or equivalently,

K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}(\Psi)
embeds into
K
.

(2)

One cannot claim that
K\langle G/U\rangle
is trivial,
when
U
is of finite index in
G
, even if
G
acts faithfully on
K
. E.g., let
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
,

G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x,y\}}=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{2}\times U
,
K=K^{\prime}=k(\frac{z-y}{x-y}~|~z\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y\})
. Then
K^{U}=k

and
K\langle G/U\rangle^{G}=(K\langle G/U\rangle^{U})^{G}=(k\langle G/U\rangle)^{G}=k([1]+[(12)])
.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
K
be a field,
G
be a group of automorphisms of the field
K
.
Let
U\subseteq H\subseteq G
be open subgroups of
G
, where the index of
U
in
H
is finite.
Suppose that
[K^{U}:K^{H}]=\#(H/U)
.
Then there is a natural isomorphism
\rho_{G}:K\langle G/H\rangle\otimes_{K^{H}}K^{U}\xrightarrow{\sim}K\langle G/U\rangle
,
given by
\sum_{i}a_{i}[g_{i}]\otimes f_{i}\mapsto\sum_{i}\sum_{[\xi]\in G/U,~[\xi]\bmod H=[g_{i}]}a_{i}f_{i}^{\xi}[\xi]
.

Proof.

Any element
\alpha
of
K\langle G/H\rangle\otimes_{K^{H}}K^{U}
can be presented as

\sum_{i}a_{i}[g_{i}]\otimes f_{i}
, where
[g_{i}]
are determined uniquely if they are pairwise distinct. Then

\rho_{G}(\alpha)=0
only if
\sum_{i:~[\xi]\bmod H=[g_{i}]}a_{i}f_{i}^{\xi}=0
for all
\xi
, i.e. only if
\alpha=0
.
As
[K^{U}:K^{H}]=\#(H/U)
,
K
-dimensions of the source and the target of the embedding

\rho_{H}:K\langle H/H\rangle\otimes_{K^{H}}K^{U}=K\otimes_{K^{H}}K^{U}\to K\langle H/U\rangle
coincide,
so
\rho_{H}
is bijective. Then the induction functor
K\langle G\rangle\otimes_{K\langle H\rangle}(-)

transforms the bijection
\rho_{H}
to the isomorphism
\rho_{G}
. ∎

Example 3.16
.

If
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
for an infinite set
\Psi
then, by
Lemma
3.7
, the assumption of Lemma
3.15

for all
U\subseteq H
is equivalent to the faithfulness of the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{J}
-action on
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}

for all finite subsets
J\subset\Psi
. This is the case if, for a field
k
,
K
contains
one of the following fields:

•

k(x/y~|~x,y\in\Psi)
;

•

k(x-y~|~x,y\in\Psi)
and
either
k
is not of characteristic 2, or
U
coincides with
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
for no
x\neq y
in
\Psi
;

•

F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}~|~x,y,z\in\Psi\right)
,

F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{(w-x)(y-z)}{(x-y)(z-w)}~|~w,x,y,z\in\Psi\right)
, where
F\neq k
;

•

k\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}~|~x,y,z\in\Psi\right)
, where
U
coincides
with
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
for no
x\neq y
in
\Psi
,

•

k\left(\frac{(w-x)(y-z)}{(x-y)(z-w)}~|~w,x,y,z\in\Psi\right)
, where
U
coincides with

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
for no
x\neq y
in
\Psi
and
U
contains
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y,z\}}
as
a subgroup of index
\leq 3
for no pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
.

Then Lemma
3.15
asserts that, for any such open subgroup
U\subseteq G
, one has

K\langle G/U\rangle\cong K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{r}
for some integer
s\geq 0
and
r\geq 1
.

Definition 3.17
.

A pair
(K,G)
consisting of a permutation group
G

and a
G
-field
K
is called a
Galois pair
if there is an open subgroup
U_{0}

such that the following equivalent conditions hold:

(1)

for any open subgroup
U\subseteq U_{0}
and any subgroup

\widetilde{U}\subseteq G
containing
U
as a proper subgroup of finite index one has
K^{U}\neq K^{\widetilde{U}}
;

(2)

for any open subgroup
U\subseteq U_{0}
and any subgroup
\widetilde{U}\subseteq G

containing
U
as a proper normal subgroup of
finite index the
\widetilde{U}/U
-action on
K^{U}
is faithful.

Proof.

(
1
)
\Rightarrow
(
2
). If
U\subseteq U_{0}
is normal in
\widetilde{U}
and

h\in\widetilde{U}/U
acts trivially on
K^{U}
then
K^{U}=K^{\langle g,U\rangle}
, where
g
is a preimage of
h
in

\widetilde{U}
, so
\langle g,U\rangle=U
, i.e.
g\in U
, so the
\widetilde{U}/U
-action on
K^{U}
is faithful.

(
2
)
\Rightarrow
(
1
). If
U\subseteq U_{0}
and
\widetilde{U}\subseteq G
contains
U
as a
subgroup of finite index, set
U^{\prime}:=\bigcap_{g\in\widetilde{U}}gUg^{-1}\subseteq U
. Then
\widetilde{U}/U^{\prime}
acts
faithfully on
K^{U^{\prime}}
, i.e.
\widetilde{U}/U^{\prime}
is the Galois group of
K^{U^{\prime}}|K^{\widetilde{U}}
, and therefore,

K^{U}:=(K^{U^{\prime}})^{U/U^{\prime}}=(K^{U^{\prime}})^{\widetilde{U}/U^{\prime}}=:K^{\widetilde{U}}
if and only if
U=\widetilde{U}
. ∎

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
K
be a field endowed with a faithful smooth
G
-action,
where
G
is either
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
for an infinite set
\Psi
, or a quotient by a central subgroup of
the automorphism group of an infinite-dimensional vector space
\Psi

over a field. Then
(K,G)
is a Galois pair.

Proof.

Let
J\subset\Psi
be a maximal
G
-closed subset such that
K^{G_{J}}=K^{G}
, and

x_{1},x_{2},\dots
be a sequence of elements of
\Psi
in general position with respect to
J
, i.e. each

x_{i+1}
is not in the
G
-closure
J^{(i)}
of
J\cup\{x_{1},\dots,x_{i}\}
. As
G_{J\cup\{x_{i}\}}
and

G_{J\cup\{x_{j}\}}
generate
G_{J}
for all
i\neq j
,
K^{G_{J\cup\{x_{i}\}}}\cap K^{G_{J\cup\{x_{j}\}}}=K^{G_{J}}
,
so the group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\{x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(J^{(N)}|J)
acts faithfully on

\sum_{i=1}^{N}K^{G_{J\cup\{x_{i}\}}}
. Then, for any
N\geq 5
, the group
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(J^{(N)})
acts faithfully on

K^{G_{J^{(N)}}}\supseteq\sum_{i=1}^{N}K^{G_{J\cup\{x_{i}\}}}
,
since all non-central normal subgroups of
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(J^{(N)})
(i.e.
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{J^{(N)}}
and
\mathfrak{A}_{J^{(N)}}

if
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
;
\mathrm{SL}(J^{(N)})\cdot\langle\mbox{central subgroup}\rangle
if
G=\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
)
meet the subgroup
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\{x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\}}\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(J^{(N)}|J)
non-trivially. ∎

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
K
be a field and
G
be a group of automorphisms
of the field
K
. Let
B
be such a system of open subgroups of
G
that any open subgroup contains a
subgroup conjugated, for some
H\in B
, to an open subgroup of finite index in
H
. Suppose that
(K,G)

is a Galois pair. Then the objects
K\langle G/H\rangle
for all
H\in B
form a system of generators
of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
of smooth
K
-semilinear representations of
G
.

Proof.

Let
V
be a smooth semilinear representation of
G
. Then the stabilizer of any vector

v\in V
is open, i.e., the stabilizer of some vector
v^{\prime}
in the
G
-orbit of
v
admits an open subgroup

U\subseteq G
which is of finite index in some
H\in B
. The
K
-linear envelope
W
of the (finite)

H
-orbit of
v^{\prime}
is a smooth
K
-semilinear representation of
H
. We may assume that
U
is normal
in
H
. Then the elements of the
H
-orbit of
v^{\prime}
are fixed by
U
, so
W=K\otimes_{K^{U}}W^{U}
.

If
(K,G)
is a Galois pair then we may assume that
U\subseteq U_{0}
as in Definition
3.17
,
so the
H/U
-action on
K^{U}
is faithful. By Proposition
2.2
, this implies that

W^{U}=K^{U}\otimes_{(K^{U})^{H/U}}(W^{U})^{H/U}=K^{U}\otimes_{K^{H}}W^{H}
. Then
W=K\otimes_{K^{H}}W^{H}
,
i.e.,
v^{\prime}
belongs to the
K
-linear envelope of the
K^{H}
-vector subspace fixed by
H
.
As a consequence, there is a morphism from a finite cartesian power of
K\langle G/H\rangle

to
V
, containing
v^{\prime}
(and therefore, containing
v
as well) in the image. ∎

Example 3.20
.

Let
K
be a field endowed
with a smooth faithful
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action.
Then (i) the assumptions of Lemma
3.19
hold if
B
is the set of subgroups
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,T}
for
a collection of finite subsets
T\subset\Psi
of unbounded cardinality, (ii)
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle

is isomorphic to
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,T}\rangle
for any
T
of order
N
.

Thus, any collection of the objects
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle
for infinitely many
N
form a system
of generators of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
of smooth
K
-semilinear representations of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.

Let
K
admit an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariant field embedding
\iota:k\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}~|~x,y,z\in\Psi\right)\to K

for a prime field
k
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and some
x\neq y
in
\Psi
, denote by
(x-y)^{n}K
the
one-dimensional
K
-vector space spanned by
(x-y)^{n}
endowed with the semilinear
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action

\sigma:(x-y)^{n}\mapsto(x-y)^{n}\cdot\left(\frac{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}{x-y}\right)^{n}
. Obviously, the isomorphism
class of
(x-y)^{n}K
is independent of
x,y\in\Psi
. Then
n\mapsto(x-y)^{n}K
gives rise to homomorphism
from
\mathbb{Z}
to the Picard group of the isomorphism classes of invertible objects of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, which is trivial if and only if

\iota
extends to an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariant field embedding of
k(u-v~|~u,v\in\Psi)
.

For any object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, set
(x-y)^{n}V:=(x-y)^{n}K\otimes_{K}V
. Then

\bigwedge\nolimits_{K}^{N}K\langle\Psi\rangle\xrightarrow[\sim]{[s_{1}]\wedge\dots\wedge[s_{N}]\mapsto\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq N}(s_{i}-s_{j})\{s_{1},\dots,s_{N}\}}(x-y)^{\binom{N}{2}}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle

is an isomorphism. If
K\cong k(\Psi)
for a
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-subfield
k
then

\bigwedge_{K}^{N}K\langle\Psi\rangle\cong\Omega^{N}_{K|k}
(differential
N
-forms on
K
over
k
),

[s_{1}]\wedge\dots\wedge[s_{N}]\leftrightarrow ds_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge ds_{N}
. ∎

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
G
be a group,
U\subseteq G
be a subgroup,
A
be
a
G
-ring,
V
be an
A\langle G\rangle
-module admitting an
A
-basis
B
fixed by
U
.

Then there is an isomorphism of
A\langle G\rangle
-modules

\eta:\bigoplus_{B}A\langle G/U\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}V\langle G/U\rangle
.

Proof.

Define
\eta
by

(\sum_{[g]\in G/U}a_{b,g}[g]_{b})_{b\in B}\mapsto\sum_{b\in B}\sum_{[g]\in G/U}a_{b,g}b^{g}\otimes[g]
.
Then
\eta
is
G
-equivariant and transforms the
A
-basis
B\times(G/U)
of the source to the
A
-basis

\bigsqcup_{[g]\in G/U}g(B)\otimes[g]
of the target, so it extends by
A
-linearity to an isomorphism. ∎

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
G
be a group,
U\subseteq G
be a subgroup such that

[U:U\cap(gUg^{-1})]=\infty
unless
g\in U
,
K
be a
G
-field,
and
V
be a
K\langle G\rangle
-module.
Then
(i)

V\langle G/U\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{B}K\langle G/U\rangle

if and only if
V^{U}
contains a
K
-basis of
V;

(ii)

V\langle G/U\rangle
is indecomposable if
V
is invertible
(
i.e.
\dim_{K}V=1)
.

Proof.

As
UgU=g(g^{-1}Ug)U
consists of
[g^{-1}Ug:(g^{-1}Ug)\cap U]=[U:U\cap(gUg^{-1})]
classes
in
G/U
, the only finite
U
-orbit on
G/U
is
\{[U]\}
, so
V\langle G/U\rangle^{U}\subseteq V\cdot[U]
,
and (i) follows from Lemma
3.21
.

If
V
is invertible then

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V\langle G/U\rangle)=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(K\langle G/U\rangle)\cong K\langle G/U\rangle^{U}=K^{U}\cdot[U]
is a field, so
V\langle G/U\rangle
is indecomposable. ∎

For a wide class of categories, one can define the
length
of an object. In particular, the length
of a
G
-closed subset
X
is the minimal cardinality of the subsets
S\subset\Psi
such that
X
is the

G
-closure of
S
.

Examples.
1. Let
\Psi
be an infinite set, possibly endowed with a structure of
a projective space. Let
G
be the group of automorphisms of
\Psi
, respecting the structure,
if any. Let
J
be the
G
-closure of a finite subset in
\Psi
, i.e., a finite subset or
a finite-dimensional subspace. Let
U
be the stabilizer of
J
in
G
. Then
G/U
is identified
with the set of all
G
-closed subsets in
\Psi
of the same length as
J
.

2. By Lemma
3.22
, the object
K\langle G/U\rangle
of the category

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is indecomposable in the following examples:

(1)

G
is the group of projective automorphisms of an infinite projective space
\Psi
(i.e.,
either
\Psi
is infinite-dimensional, or
\Psi
is defined over an infinite field),
U
is
the setwise stabilizer in
G
of a finite-dimensional subspace
J\subseteq\Psi
. Then
G/U

is identified with the Grassmannian of all subspaces in
\Psi
of the same dimension as
J
.

(2)

G
is the group of permutations of an infinite set
\Psi
,
U
is
the stabilizer in
G
of a finite subset
J\subset\Psi
. Then
G/U
is identified
with the set
\binom{\Psi}{\#J}
of all subsets in
\Psi
of order
\#J
.

(3)

G
is the automorphism group of an algebraically closed extension
F
of
a field
k
,
U
is the stabilizer in
G
of an algebraically closed subextension

L|k
of finite transcendence degree. Then
G/U
is identified with the set
of all subextensions in
F|k
isomorphic to
L|k
.

4.
Structure of smooth semilinear representations of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

The following result will be used in the particular case of the trivial
G
-action on the
A
-module
V
(i.e.,

\chi\equiv id_{V}
), claiming the injectivity of the natural map
A\otimes_{A^{G}}V^{G}\to V
(since
V_{id_{V}}=V^{G}
).

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
G
be a group,
A
be
a division ring endowed with a

G
-action
G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{ring}}(A)
,
V
be an
A\langle G\rangle
-module
and
\chi:G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits_{A}(V)
be a
G
-action on the
A
-module
V
.

Set
V_{\chi}:=\{w\in V~|~\sigma w=\chi(\sigma)w\text{ {\rm for all} $\sigma\in G$}\}
.

Then
V_{\chi}
is an
A^{G}
-module and the natural map

A\otimes_{A^{G}}V_{\chi}\to V
is injective.

Proof.

This is well-known: Suppose that some elements

w_{1},\dots,w_{m}\in V_{\chi}
are
A^{G}
-linearly independent,
but
A
-linearly dependent for a minimal
m\geq 2
.
Then
w_{1}=\sum_{j=2}^{m}\lambda_{j}w_{j}
for some
\lambda_{j}\in A^{\times}
.

Applying
\sigma-\chi(\sigma)
for each
\sigma\in G
to both sides of the latter
equality, we get
\sum_{j=2}^{m}(\lambda_{j}^{\sigma}-\lambda_{j})\chi(\sigma)w_{j}=0
,
and therefore,
\sum_{j=2}^{m}(\lambda_{j}^{\sigma}-\lambda_{j})w_{j}=0
.
By the minimality of
m
, one has
\lambda_{j}^{\sigma}-\lambda_{j}=0
for
each
\sigma\in G
, so
\lambda_{j}\in A^{G}
for any
j
, contradicting
the
A^{G}
-linear independence of
w_{1},\dots,w_{m}
. ∎

4.1.
Growth estimates

For each integer
N\geq 0
, fix a
G
-closed subset
\Psi_{N}\subset\Psi
of length
N
.
For a division ring endowed with a
G
-action and an
A\langle G\rangle
-module

M
, define a function
d_{M}:{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\coprod\{\infty\}
by

d_{M}(N):=\dim_{A^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}}(M^{G_{\Psi_{N}}})
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
G
be either
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

(
and then
q:=1)
or the group
of automorphisms of an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space
\Psi
identical on a fixed subspace of finite dimension

v\geq 0
. Then, for any integer
N\geq 0
, the
G
-closed subsets of length
N
form a non-empty

G
-orbit. Let
A
be a division ring endowed with
a
G
-action. If
M
is a non-zero
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule of
A\langle G/G_{\Psi_{n}}\rangle

for some
n\geq 0
then
d_{M}
grows as a
q
-polynomial of degree
n
:

\frac{d_{m+n}(N)}{d_{m}(N)d_{n}(n)}\leq d_{M}(N)\leq d_{n}(N)
for some
m\geq 0
, where
d_{n}(N)
is the number
of embeddings
\Psi_{n}\hookrightarrow\Psi_{N}
induced by elements of
G
, and

d_{n}(N)=\begin{cases}q^{(N+v)n}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-q^{i-N})&\mbox{if $q>1$, so $\frac{d_{m+n}(N)}{d_{m}(N)d_{n}(n)}=\frac{q^{Nn}}{\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(q^{n}-q^{i})}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-q^{i+m-N})$}\\
\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(N-i)&\mbox{if $q=1$, so
$\frac{d_{m+n}(N)}{d_{m}(N)d_{n}(n)}=\frac{1}{n!}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(N-m-i)$.}\end{cases}

Proof.

As
M^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}\subseteq A\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi_{N}})/(N_{G}(G_{\Psi_{N}})\cap G_{\Psi_{n}})\rangle

and (by Lemma
4.1
)
A\otimes_{A^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}}M^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}\to M\subseteq A\langle G/G_{\Psi_{n}}\rangle

is injective, there is a natural inclusion

A\otimes_{A^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}}M^{G_{\Psi_{N}}}\hookrightarrow A\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi_{N}})/(N_{G}(G_{\Psi_{N}})\cap G_{\Psi_{n}})\rangle=A\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{N})/\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{N}|\Psi_{n})\rangle,

if
n\leq N
. (Here
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{N}|\Psi_{n})
denotes the automorphisms of
\Psi_{N}
identical on
\Psi_{n}
.) Then
one has
d_{M}(N)\leq\#(\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{N})/\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{N}|\Psi_{n}))=d_{n}(N)
.

To obtain a lower bound of
d_{M}(N)
, fix a non-zero element
\alpha\in M\subseteq A\langle G/G_{\Psi_{n}}\rangle
.
Let
\Psi_{m}
be the
G
-closure of the union of the images of
\Psi_{n}
under the elements of
G/G_{\Psi_{n}}

in the support of
\alpha
.
Fix a
G
-closed subset
\Psi_{m-n}\subset\Psi_{m}
of length
m-n
. For each length-
m

G
-closed subset

\Psi_{m-n}\subset P\subseteq\Psi_{N}
, fix an element
\sigma_{P}\in G_{\Psi_{m-n}}
such that
\sigma_{P}(\Psi_{m})=P
.
Then the supports of
\sigma_{P}\alpha
for all such
P
are pairwise disjoint, apart from those embeddings of

\Psi_{n}
whose images meet
\Psi_{m-n}
non-trivially. This implies that
\sigma_{P}\alpha
are linearly independent,
which gives
d_{M}(N)\geq\binom{N-m}{n}_{q}
.

The lower bound of
d_{M}(N)
is given by the number of
G
-closed
subsets in
\Psi_{N}
with length-0 intersection with
\Psi_{m}
.
Indeed, for any non-zero element
\alpha\in M\subseteq A\langle G/G_{\Psi_{n}}\rangle
there exist
an integer
m\geq 0
and elements
\xi,\eta\in G
such that
\xi\alpha
is congruent
to
\sum_{\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{n})}b_{\sigma}\eta\sigma
for some non-zero collection

\{b_{\sigma}\in A\}_{\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(\Psi_{n})}
modulo monomorphisms whose
images have intersection of positive length with a fixed finite
\Psi_{m}
. ∎

Let
q
be either 1 or a primary integer. Let
S
be a plain set if
q=1

and an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space if
q>1
. For each integer
s\geq 0
, we
denote by
\binom{S}{s}_{q}
the set of subobjects of
S
(
G
-closed subsets of
\Psi
,
if
S=\Psi
, where
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
if
q=1
and
G=\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(\Psi)
if
q>1
) of length
s
.
In other words,
\binom{S}{s}_{1}:=\binom{S}{s}
, while
\binom{S}{s}_{q}
is
the Grassmannian of the
s
-dimensional subspaces in
S
if
q>1
.

Recall, that an injection
M\hookrightarrow N
in an abelian category is called
an
essential extension
if any non-zero subobject of
N
has a non-zero
intersection with the image of
M
, cf.
[
3
, Ch. 6, §2]
.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
G
be either

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

(
and then
q:=1)
or the group of automorphisms of an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space

\Psi
fixing a finite-dimensional subspace of
\Psi
. Let
A
be a
division ring endowed with a
G
-action. Let
\Xi
be a finite subset in

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A\langle G\rangle}(A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle,A\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)

for some finite
G
-closed
T^{\prime}\subsetneqq T\subset\Psi
. Then

(1)

any non-zero
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule
of
A\langle\binom{\Psi}{m}_{q}\rangle
is essential for any integer

m\geq 0
;

(2)

there are no non-zero isomorphic

A\langle G\rangle
-submodules in
A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
and
A\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
;

(3)

the common kernel
V_{\Xi}
of all elements of
\Xi
is
an essential
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule in

A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
.

Proof.

(
1
) It follows from the lower growth estimate of Lemma
4.2
that,
for any non-zero
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule
M
of
A\langle\binom{\Psi}{m}_{q}\rangle
,

d_{M}(N)\sim d_{A\langle\binom{\Psi}{m}_{q}\rangle}(N)
when
N\to\infty
, so the growth of intersection of
any pair of non-zero submodules is the same.

(
2
) follows immediately from Lemma
4.2
, since for any non-zero

A\langle G\rangle
-submodule
M
of
A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
, there exist constants
\beta>\alpha>0

such that
\alpha<d_{M}(N)/d_{A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle}(N)<\beta
for all
N\gg 0
.

(
3
) Suppose that there exists a non-zero submodule

M\subseteq A\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
such that
M\cap V_{\Xi}=0
. Then
restriction of some
\xi\in\Xi
to
M
is non-zero. If
\xi|_{M}
is not
injective, replacing
M
with
\ker\xi\cap M
, we can assume that

\xi|_{M}=0
. In other words, we can assume that restriction to
M
of
any
\xi\in\Xi
is either injective or zero. In particular, restriction
to
M
of some
\xi\in\Xi
is injective, i.e.
\xi
embeds
M
into

A\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
, contradicting (
2
). ∎

4.2.
Smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-sets and
F_{\Psi}
-semilinear representation
of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
as sheaves

Let
\mathrm{FI}
be the category of all finite sets, where the morphisms are the injections.

This category does not admit coproducts,
since, for any non-empty
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
and their elements
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, there are diagrams

J_{1}\hookrightarrow J\hookleftarrow J_{2}
gluing together and separating
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.

Consider the opposite category
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
as a site, where any non-empty sieve
is covering.

A presheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
is a sheaf if and only if, for any
diagram
J_{1}\hookleftarrow J_{0}\hookrightarrow J_{2}
in
\mathrm{FI}
, the diagram

\mathcal{F}(J_{0})\to\mathcal{F}(J_{1})\times\mathcal{F}(J_{2})\rightrightarrows\mathcal{F}(J_{1}\sqcup_{J_{0}}J_{2})

is an equalizer, where
\sqcup_{J_{0}}
is the colimit of
J_{1}\hookleftarrow J_{0}\hookrightarrow J_{2}

in the category of sets.

For each ‘reasonable’ category
\mathcal{C}
, each
J\in\mathrm{FI}
and each
\mathcal{C}
-valued
sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
, denote by
\mathcal{F}_{+J}
the sheaf

I\mapsto\mathcal{F}(I\sqcup J)
. Thus,

•

(-)_{+J}:\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}_{+J}

is an endofunctor on the category of
\mathcal{C}
-valued sheaves on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
;

•

(-)_{+\varnothing}
is the identity functor;

•

any embedding of finite sets
J_{1}\hookrightarrow J_{2}
induces a morphism of functors

(-)_{+J_{1}}\to(-)_{+J_{2}}
;

•

for each pair of finite
J,J^{\prime}
, there is a natural
isomorphism
(-)_{+J}\circ(-)_{+J^{\prime}}\cong(-)_{+(J\sqcup J^{\prime})}
.

For a sheaf of rings
\mathcal{A}
, we say
‘
an
\mathcal{A}
-module
’ instead of ‘
a sheaf of
\mathcal{A}
-modules
’.

If
\mathcal{A}
is a sheaf of rings and
\mathcal{F}
is an
\mathcal{A}
-module then

\mathcal{F}_{+J}
is an
\mathcal{A}_{+J}
-module, so there is a natural morphism of functors

\mathcal{A}_{+J}\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}}}(-)\to(-)_{+J}
on the category of
\mathcal{A}
-modules.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set. Let
F
be a field and
k
be
a subfield algebraically closed in
F
. To each sheaf of sets
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}

we associate the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set
\mathcal{F}(\Psi):=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{J\subset\Psi}\mathcal{F}(J)
, where
J

runs over the finite subsets of
\Psi
. This gives rise to the following equivalences of categories:
