\displaystyle\nu_{\Psi}:\{\text{{\rm sheaves of sets on $\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}$}}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}$-sets}}\},\quad\mathcal{F}\mapsto\mathcal{F}(\Psi);

\displaystyle\{\text{{\rm sheaves of $k$-vector spaces on $\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}$}}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi});

\displaystyle\{\text{{\rm$\mathcal{O}$-modules}}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}),\quad\text{where $\mathcal{O}$ is the sheaf of fields $S\mapsto F_{S}$ on $\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}$}.

The functor
\nu_{\Psi}
admits a quasi-inverse
\nu_{\Psi}^{-1}
such that for any infinite
subset
\Psi^{\prime}\subseteq\Psi
the functor
\nu_{\Psi^{\prime}}\circ\nu_{\Psi}^{-1}
is given by

M\mapsto M^{(\Psi^{\prime})}_{(\Psi)}=M^{\prime}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{J\subset\Psi^{\prime}}M^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}\subseteq M^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\Psi^{\prime}}}
,
where
J
runs over the finite subsets of
\Psi^{\prime}
, and gives rise to the following equivalences of categories:

\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}$-sets}}\}\xrightarrow{\sim}\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}$-sets}}\};\ \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}});\ \mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi^{\prime}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}).

For infinite subsets
\Psi^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\Psi^{\prime}\subseteq\Psi
and
V\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
one has

\Psi^{(\Psi^{\prime\prime})}_{(\Psi^{\prime})}=\Psi^{\prime\prime}\cup(\Psi\smallsetminus\Psi^{\prime});\quad(F_{\Psi})^{(\Psi^{\prime\prime})}_{(\Psi^{\prime})}=F_{\Psi^{\prime\prime}\cup(\Psi\smallsetminus\Psi^{\prime})};\quad(V^{(\Psi^{\prime})}_{(\Psi)})^{(\Psi^{\prime\prime})}_{(\Psi^{\prime})}=V^{(\Psi^{\prime\prime})}_{(\Psi)}.

Fix an embedding of a finite set
J
into
\Psi
. Then the functor

\nu_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}\circ(-)_{+J}\circ\nu_{\Psi}^{-1}
is isomorphic to the functor

\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}$-sets}}\}\xrightarrow{(-)^{(\Psi\smallsetminus J)}_{(\Psi\smallsetminus J)}:=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}}\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}$-sets}}\}=\{\text{{\rm smooth $\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}$-sets}}\}
.

Proof.

For any pair of sheaves
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, a map of sets

\alpha:\mathcal{F}(\Psi)\to\mathcal{G}(\Psi)
and any embedding
\iota:S\hookrightarrow\Psi
such
that
\alpha(\mathcal{F}(\Psi)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota(S)}})\subseteq\mathcal{G}(\Psi)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota(S)}}

there is a unique map
\alpha_{S}:\mathcal{F}(S)\to\mathcal{G}(S)
making commutative the square

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{F}(S)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{S}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{G}(S)\\
\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{F}}$\hss}&&\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{G}}$\hss}\\
\mathcal{F}(\Psi)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{G}(\Psi).\end{array}

If
\alpha
is a morphism of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-sets then
\alpha_{S}
is
independent of
\iota
, since all embeddings
S\hookrightarrow\Psi
form a single

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbit. This gives gives rise to a natural bijection

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}(\mathcal{F}(\Psi),\mathcal{G}(\Psi))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
, the inverse map is given by restriction to finite subsets
of
\Psi
.

To construct a functor
\nu_{\Psi}^{-1}
quasi-inverse to
\nu_{\Psi}
, for each finite
set
S
fix an embedding
\iota_{S}:S\hookrightarrow\Psi
, which is identical if
S

is a subset of
\Psi
. Then to a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set
M
we associate the presheaf

S\mapsto M^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota_{S}(S)}}
and to each embedding
j:S\hookrightarrow T
we
associate a unique map
M^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota_{S}(S)}}\hookrightarrow M^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota_{T}(T)}}

induced by the element

\iota_{T}j\iota_{S}^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota_{T}(T)}\backslash\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\iota_{S}(S)}
.
It follows from Lemma
3.6
that this presheaf is a sheaf. ∎

Example 4.5
.

Given a sheaf of abelian groups
\mathcal{F}
, an integer
s\geq 0

and
J\in\mathrm{FI}
, define the presheaves of abelian groups

h^{J}_{\mathcal{F}}:I\mapsto\mathcal{F}(I)\langle\{J\hookrightarrow I\}\rangle
and

\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{F}}:I\mapsto\mathcal{F}(I)\langle\binom{I}{s}\rangle
.
E.g.,
h^{\varnothing}_{\mathcal{F}}=\bar{h}^{0}_{\mathcal{F}}=\mathcal{F}
and

h^{\{\ast\}}_{\mathcal{F}}=\bar{h}^{1}_{\mathcal{F}}:I\mapsto\mathcal{F}(I)\langle I\rangle
. Then
h^{J}_{\mathcal{F}}

and
\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{F}}
are sheaves of abelian groups, faithfully flat for
\mathcal{F}=\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
.

4.3.
Local structure of finitely generated objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a non-constant sheaf of fields
on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
, and
V
be a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}
-module.
Then there is an integer
n\geq 0
such that for any finite set
J
of order
\geq n

•

the
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-module
V_{+J}
is isomorphic to

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}})^{\kappa_{s}}
for some integer
N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
;

•

V
can be embedded into a product of copies of
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.21
, for each finite set
J^{\prime}
, the
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}
-module
V_{+J^{\prime}}

is finitely generated, while by Lemma
3.19
, there is a surjection of
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}
-modules

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N(J^{\prime})}\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}}{}^{m_{s,J^{\prime}}}\to V_{+J^{\prime}}
for some
N(J^{\prime})\geq 0

and
m_{s,J^{\prime}}\geq 0
. Choose such a surjection so that
N(J^{\prime})
is minimal and
m_{N(J^{\prime}),J^{\prime}}\geq 1
is minimal
as well. It is clear, that for each finite set
J^{\prime\prime}
with
\#J^{\prime\prime}\geq\#J^{\prime}
, one has
N(J^{\prime\prime})\leq N(J^{\prime})
and

m_{N(J^{\prime\prime}),J^{\prime\prime}}\leq m_{N(J^{\prime}),J^{\prime}}
if
N(J^{\prime\prime})=N(J^{\prime})
.

Fix some
J^{\prime}
with minimal possible
N(J^{\prime})
and
m_{N,J^{\prime}}\geq 1
. The pair
(N,m)
depends only on
V
,
so denote it
(N_{V},m_{V})
. We replace
\mathcal{O}
by
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}
and
V
by
V_{+J^{\prime}}
.
Obviously,
(N_{V},m_{V})=(N_{V_{+J}},m_{V_{+J}})
for any finite
J
.

By induction on
N
, we prove the statement for all
V
with
N_{V}=N
, the case
N=0
being trivial.

Let
\alpha:(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}\to V
and
\beta:\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N-1}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m_{s}}\to V

be two morphisms such that
\alpha+\beta:(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}\oplus\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N-1}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m_{s}}\to V
is surjective.

Suppose that
\alpha
is not injective, and
0\neq(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m})\in(\ker\alpha)(I)
for a finite
I
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\xi_{m}=\sum_{i=1}^{b}a_{i}I_{i}\neq 0
for some
I_{i}\subseteq I

of order
N
and non-zero
a_{i}
. Set
J:=I\smallsetminus I_{1}
. Then
\alpha
factors
through a quotient
(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}/\langle(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m})\rangle
. On the other hand,
the inclusion
(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-,0)}}{{\hookrightarrow}}(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}

induces a surjection of
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-modules

(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})_{+J}^{m-1}\oplus\bigoplus_{\varnothing\neq\Lambda\subseteq J}\bar{h}^{N-\#\Lambda}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}\xrightarrow{(-,0)+\sum_{\Lambda}(\underbrace{0,\dots,0}_{m-1},-\cup\Lambda)}((\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}/\langle(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{m})\rangle)_{+J}

giving rise to a surjection of
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-modules
(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})_{+J}^{m-1}\oplus\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N-1}\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}{}^{\binom{\#J}{N-s}+m_{s}}\to V_{+J}
.
This contradicts the minimality assumption on
m
, thus showing that
\alpha
is injective.

By Lemma
4.2
and the induction hypothesis, for any subsheaf
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(\beta)

there is some
C>0
such that
\dim_{{\mathcal{O}}(J)}\mathcal{F}(J)\leq C\cdot\#J^{N-1}
for all sufficiently
big
J
. For any non-zero subsheaf
\mathcal{F}\subseteq(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}
, Lemma
4.2
gives
an estimate
\dim_{{\mathcal{O}}(J)}\mathcal{F}(J)\geq C^{\prime}\cdot\#J^{N}
for some
C^{\prime}>0
and all sufficiently
big
J
. This implies that
\mathrm{Im}(\alpha)\cap\mathrm{Im}(\beta)=0
. Therefore,

V\cong(\bar{h}^{N}_{\mathcal{O}})^{m}\oplus\mathrm{Im}(\beta)
. By induction hypothesis,
\mathrm{Im}(\beta)_{+J}

is of required type for some
J
, thus completing the induction step.

Fix a finite set
I
such that
\mathcal{O}(I)\neq\mathcal{O}(\varnothing)
.
By Remark
3.11
,
\mathcal{O}(\varnothing)
is algebraically closed in
\mathcal{O}(J)

for any
J
, so
\mathcal{O}(I)
is transcendental over
\mathcal{O}(\varnothing)
, and therefore,

\mathcal{O}(I)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}
is transcendental over
\mathcal{O}(\varnothing)
as well. By Lemma
3.6
,

\mathcal{O}(J)\neq\mathcal{O}(I)
for any
I\subsetneqq J
. Then Lemma
3.9
implies that,
for each integer
s>0
, the sheaf
\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+I}}
can be embedded into a product of copies of

\mathcal{O}_{+I}
. Now any
\mathcal{O}
-module
V
embeds into
V_{+J}
for any finite
J
, so if
V
is
finitely generated we can use the above description of
V_{+J}
for sufficiently big
J
to conclude that,
V

can be embedded into a product of copies of
\mathcal{O}_{+I}
for any sufficiently large finite
I
. ∎

Remark 4.7
.

By Krull–Schmidt–Remak–Azumaya Theorem the integers

N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
in Proposition
4.6
are uniquely determined.
Clearly,
N
and
\kappa_{N}
are independent of
J
. We call
N
the
level
of
V
.
It is easy to show that any non-zero submodule of

K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle
is of level
N
, while the key point of the proof of the Proposition
is that any quotient of
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle
by a non-zero submodule is of level
<N
.

In particular, the objects
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are monoform, so their
injective hulls are indecomposable, and they correspond to pairwise distinct points in the Gabriel spectrum.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
x\in\Psi
,

\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}})
, and
K
be an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field.
Then any non-zero submodule of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}\rangle}\mathcal{L}

(
e.g. of
K\langle\Psi\rangle)
is of finite codimension
;
the cokernel of any non-zero morphism of

K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules
\varphi:K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\to K\langle\Psi\rangle

is at most
(s-1)
-dimensional.

Suppose that
K
is a non-trivial smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field,
K
is a cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

and
K\langle\Psi\rangle
is injective.
Set
k:=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
and
F:=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}}
. Then there are natural bijections

(1)

between the
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules of

K\langle\Psi\rangle
of codimension
s
and the
s
-dimensional
k
-vector subspaces in
F
;

(2)

between the isomorphism classes of

K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules of
K\langle\Psi\rangle
of codimension
s
and the

F^{\times}
-orbits of the
s
-dimensional
k
-vector subspaces in the space
F
.

Proof.

Any non-zero element of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}\rangle}\mathcal{L}

can be presented as
\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{s}a_{i}[g_{i}]\otimes e
for some
a_{i}\in K^{\times}
, non-zero

e\in\mathcal{L}
and pairwise distinct
g_{i}\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}
. For any

g\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\smallsetminus(\bigcup_{i=2}^{s}g_{i}\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}})
, there exists

h\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{g_{2}x,\dots,g_{s}x\}}
such that
hg_{1}x=gx
. Then, for any
a\in K
, one has

a[g]\otimes e\in(b\cdot h)\alpha+\sum_{i=2}^{s}K[g_{i}]\otimes e
, where
b=a(a_{1}^{h})^{-1}(e/he)^{hg_{1}}\in K
.
This means that
\alpha
generates a
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodule of codimension
\leq s-1
.
The image under
\varphi
of any element of
\binom{\Psi}{s}
is a non-zero linear combination
of
s
elements of
\Psi
, so the cokernel of
\varphi
is at most
(s-1)
-dimensional.

We already know that the
K
-vector space
K\langle\Psi\rangle/M
is finite-dimensional for any
non-zero
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodule
M
of
K\langle\Psi\rangle
, so assuming that
K
is a
period field, the
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
K\langle\Psi\rangle/M
is isomorphic to a sum
of copies of
K
, and therefore,
M
is the common kernel of the elements of a finite-dimensional

k
-vector subspace of
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\Psi\rangle,K)=F
.
Clearly, the
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\Psi\rangle)^{\times}
-action
preserves the isomorphism classes of the common kernels (since

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\Psi\rangle)=F
is a field). On the other hand,

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\Psi\rangle/M,K\langle\Psi\rangle)=0

and, if
K\langle\Psi\rangle
is injective,
the restriction morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\Psi\rangle)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(M,K\langle\Psi\rangle)
is an isomorphism, i.e.,
any morphism of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules
M\to K\langle\Psi\rangle

is induced by an endomorphism of
K\langle\Psi\rangle
. ∎

Corollary 4.9
.

Let
K
be a field endowed with a non-trivial smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action.

Then, for any smooth finitely generated
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V
, there is a unique
polynomial
P_{V}(X)
over
\mathbb{Q}
such that
P_{V}(\#T)=\dim_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}

for any sufficiently large finite subset
T\subset\Psi
.

In particular,
V
is admissible, i.e.,
\dim_{K^{U}}V^{U}<\infty
for any open subgroup
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.6
, there is a finite subset

J\subset\Psi
and an isomorphism of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-modules

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi\smallsetminus J}{s}\rangle^{\kappa_{s}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V

for some integer
N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
. Then

\dim_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}=\sum_{s=0}^{N}\kappa_{s}\binom{\#(T\smallsetminus J)}{s}
for any finite
subset
T\subset\Psi
containing
J
, i.e.
P_{V}(X)=\sum_{s=0}^{N}\kappa_{s}\binom{X-\#J}{s}
is the unique
polynomial whose value at
\#T
is
\dim_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}
for such
T
. ∎

4.4.
Injective objects and resolutions

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a non-constant sheaf of fields on

\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Then, for any finitely generated
\mathcal{O}
-module
W
,
there is a finite set
J
and a resolution of the form

(1)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{+J}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}W\to\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}{}^{\kappa_{0,s}}\to\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N-1}\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}{}^{\kappa_{1,s}}\to\cdots\to\bigoplus_{s=0}^{1}\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}{}^{\kappa_{N-1,s}}\to\mathcal{O}_{+J}^{\kappa_{N}}\to 0

for some integer
\kappa_{ij}\geq 0
, where
N
is the level of
W
.

Proof.

We construct recursively an ascending sequence of finite sets

\varnothing=:J_{0}\subseteq J_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq J_{N}
and a sequence of
\mathcal{O}_{+J_{i}}
-modules

W_{i}
of level
\leq N-i
, where
W_{0}:=W
. Choose
J_{i+1}
so that
(W_{i})_{+(J_{i+1}\smallsetminus J_{i})}

is as in Proposition
4.6
. Define
W_{i+1}
as the cokernel of the morphism

\mathcal{O}_{+J_{i+1}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J_{i}}}W_{i}\hookrightarrow(W_{i})_{+(J_{i+1}\smallsetminus J_{i})}
.
Then
W_{i+1}
is of level
<N-i

and the sequence

0\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i})}}(W_{i})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i})}\hookrightarrow K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i+1})}}(W_{i})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i+1})}\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i+1})}}(W_{i+1})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{i+1})}\to 0

is exact. Combining all these short exact sequences we get a resolution

0\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{0})}}(W_{0})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{0})}\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{1})}}(W_{0})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{1})}\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{2})}}(W_{1})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{2})}\to\cdots\\
\cdots\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N-1})}}(W_{N-2})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N-1})}\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N})}}(W_{N-1})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N})}\to K\otimes_{K^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N})}}(W_{N})^{(\Psi_{N})}_{(\Psi_{N})}\to 0

of required type (
1
). ∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
C
be a collection of objects in
an abelian category
\mathcal{A}
such that
(i)

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits(P)
is a division ring for all
P\in C
,

(ii)

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P,P^{\prime})=0
for all
P\neq P^{\prime}
in
C
.

(1)

Suppose in addition that
C
is cogenerating. Then all objects in
C
are injective.

(2)

Suppose in addition that
\mathcal{A}
is a locally finitely generated Grothendieck category, while
any finitely generated object of
\mathcal{A}
admits an embedding into a product of objects in
C
.
Then each finitely generated object in
C
is injective. If all objects in
C
are finitely generated
then
C
cogenerates
\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

Assuming that
C
is cogenerating, if
P_{0}\in C
and
i:P_{0}\to V
is a monomorphism
then there exists a morphism
f:V\to P
for some
P\in C
such that
f\circ i:P_{0}\to P
is non-zero,
so
P=P_{0}
and
(f\circ i)^{-1}\circ f:V\to P_{0}
is a splitting of
i
, i.e.,
P_{0}
is injective.

Assuming that
\mathcal{A}
is locally finitely generated, if
P_{0}\in C
is finitely generated and admits
a non-trivial essential extension
E
then
E
can be chosen to be finitely generated as well. Any morphism

E\to P
for
P\neq P_{0}
in
C
vanishes on
P_{0}
, so there exists a morphism
f:E\to P_{0}
non-vanishing
on
P_{0}
. But
\ker f\cap P_{0}=0
, so
\ker f=0
, i.e.
E=P_{0}
, and thus,
P_{0}
is injective. Now, if all
objects in
C
are finitely generated then they are injective, so for any object
W
and each
finitely generated
W_{0}\subseteq W
there is an embedding
W_{0}\hookrightarrow\prod_{i\in I(W_{0})}P_{i}
into a
product of objects in
C
, while this embedding extends to a morphism
f_{W_{0}}:W\to\prod_{i\in I(W_{0})}P_{i}
,
thus giving an embedding
(f_{W_{0}}):W\to\prod_{W_{0}}\prod_{i\in I(W_{0})}P_{i}
. ∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a non-constant sheaf of fields, and
\mathcal{V}

be a level 0 simple
\mathcal{O}
-module. Then
\mathcal{V}
is injective if and only if, for any finite
set
J
, any
\mathcal{O}
-module embedding
\iota:\mathcal{V}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{+J}
is split.

Proof.

The ‘only if’ direction is trivial. In the opposite direction, let an
\mathcal{O}
-module

\mathcal{E}
be an essential extension of
\mathcal{V}
. We are going to figure out whether
\mathcal{E}

can be non-trivial, so we may assume that
\mathcal{E}
is finitely generated. By Proposition

4.6
, there is a finite set
J
and an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-modules

\mathcal{E}_{+J}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}})^{\kappa_{s}}
for some
integer
N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
. Then
\mathcal{E}
is a finitely generated
\mathcal{O}
-submodule
of
\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}})^{\kappa_{s}}
. Fix a non-zero
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-morphism

\lambda:\mathcal{E}_{+J}\to\mathcal{O}_{+J}
(e.g.
\lambda
can be chosen to be the composition
of the natural projection
\mathcal{E}_{+J}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}(\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}})^{\kappa_{s}}\to\mathcal{O}_{+J}^{\kappa_{0}}

with any
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-morphism
\mathcal{O}_{+J}^{\kappa_{0}}\to\mathcal{O}_{+J}
, which is non-zero on the
image of
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{E}
; by Lemma
3.8
,
\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}

does not contain level 0
\mathcal{O}
-submodules if
s>0
). Then
\lambda
embeds
\mathcal{E}

into
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
. By our assumption, we can choose a morphism of
\mathcal{O}
-modules

\xi:\mathcal{O}_{+J}\to\mathcal{V}
such that the composition
\xi\circ\lambda\circ\iota

splits the embedding
\iota
. ∎

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
G
be a permutation group,
\Psi
be a smooth
G
-set,

K
be a smooth
G
-field.
Let
V
be an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
such that

V\otimes_{K}K(\Psi)
is injective and either
K
or
V
is injective as well.
Then the object
V\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is injective for any integer
s\geq 0
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{K}\subset K(\Psi)
be the subfield generated over
K
by
squares of the elements of
\Psi
. There is an isomorphism

\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}\widetilde{K}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}K(\Psi)
,

[S]\mapsto\prod_{t\in S}t\cdot\widetilde{K}
, so each

\widetilde{K}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is isomorphic to a direct
summand of the object
K(\Psi)
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}}(G)
.

As
\widetilde{K}
and
K(\Psi)
are isomorphic
G
-field extensions of
K
(under
t^{2}\mapsto t
for all
t\in\Psi
),
they are isomorphic as objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, as well as
V\otimes_{K}\widetilde{K}
and
V\otimes_{K}K(\Psi)
are.

We assume that
V\otimes_{K}K(\Psi)
is injective in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, so each

V\otimes_{K}\widetilde{K}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is an injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. As we also
assume that either
K
or
V
is injective, the inclusion
V\hookrightarrow V\otimes_{K}\widetilde{K}

admits a splitting
V\otimes_{K}\widetilde{K}\xrightarrow{\pi}V
. Then the inclusion
V\otimes_{K}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\hookrightarrow V\otimes_{K}\widetilde{K}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

splits as well:
\sum_{i}a_{i}[S_{i}]\mapsto\sum_{i}\pi(a_{i})[S_{i}]
, and thus,
V\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle=V\otimes_{K}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is an injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. ∎

4.5.
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is locally noetherian; local structure
of morphisms between compact objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

In the following result, our principal examples of the ring
A
will be division rings
endowed with an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action, though the localization of
\mathbb{Z}[x~|~x\in\Psi]

at all non-constant indecomposable polynomials (which is a PID) gives one more example.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
A
be an associative left noetherian ring endowed
with an arbitrary
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action, and
W
be a left
A\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module generated by
finitely many smooth vectors. Then the left
A\langle U\rangle
-module
W
is noetherian for any open
subgroup
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.
(
Example:

W=A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
for
s\geq 0.)

Proof.

The module
W
is a quotient of a finite direct sum of
A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
for some
integer
s\geq 0
, so it suffices to show that the left
A\langle U\rangle
-module
A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle

is noetherian for any integer
s\geq 0
, i.e. that any
A\langle U\rangle
-submodule

M\subset A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
is finitely generated for all
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}

with finite
S\subset\Psi
. We proceed by induction on
s\geq 0
,
the case
s=0
being trivial. Assume that
s>0
and the

A\langle U\rangle
-modules
A\langle\Psi^{j}\rangle
are noetherian for all
j<s
.
Fix a subset
I_{0}\subset\Psi\smallsetminus S
of order
s
.

Let
M_{0}
be the image of
M
under the
A
-linear projector

\pi_{0}:A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle\to A\langle I_{0}^{s}\rangle\subset A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle

omitting all
s
-tuples containing elements other than those of
I_{0}
. As
A

is noetherian and
I_{0}^{s}
is finite, the
A
-module
M_{0}
is finitely generated.
Let the
A
-module
M_{0}
be generated by the images of some elements

\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{N}\in M\subseteq A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
. Then

\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{N}
belong to the
A
-submodule
A\langle I^{s}\rangle
of

A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
for some finite subset
I\subset\Psi
.

Let
J\subset I\cup S
be the complement to
I_{0}
. For each pair
\gamma=(j,x)
, where

1\leq j\leq s
and
x\in J
, set
\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}:=\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{s})\in\Psi^{s}~|~x_{j}=x\}
.
This is a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
-set. Then the set
\Psi^{s}
is the union of the

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
-orbit consisting of
s
-tuples of pairwise distinct elements of

\Psi\smallsetminus J
and of a finite union of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
-orbits embeddable into

\Psi^{s-1}
:
\bigcup_{\gamma}\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}\cup\bigcup_{1\leq i<j\leq s}\Delta_{ij}
,
where
\Delta_{ij}:=\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{s})\in\Psi^{s}~|~x_{i}=x_{j}\}
are diagonals.

As (i)
M_{0}\subseteq\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\alpha_{j}+\sum_{\gamma\in\{1,\dots,s\}\times J}A\langle\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}\rangle
, (ii)

g(M_{0})\subset A\langle\Psi^{s}\rangle
is determined by
g(I_{0})
, (iii) for any

g\in U
such that
g(I_{0})\cap J=\varnothing
there exists

g^{\prime}\in U_{J}
with
g(I_{0})=g^{\prime}(I_{0})
(
U_{J}
acts transitively
on the
s
-configurations in
\Psi\smallsetminus J
),
one has inclusions of
A\langle U_{J}\rangle
-modules

\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\langle U\rangle\alpha_{j}\subseteq M\subseteq\sum_{g\in U}g(M_{0})\subseteq\sum_{g\in U_{J}}g(M_{0})+\sum_{\gamma\in\{1,\dots,s\}\times J}A\langle\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}\rangle.

On the other hand,
g(M_{0})\subseteq g(\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\alpha_{j})+\sum_{\gamma\in\{1,\dots,s\}\times J}A\langle\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}\rangle

for
g\in U_{J}
,
and therefore, the
A\langle U_{J}\rangle
-module

M/\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\langle U\rangle\alpha_{j}
becomes a subquotient of the
noetherian, by the induction assumption,
A\langle U_{J}\rangle
-module

\sum_{\gamma\in\{1,\dots,s\}\times J}A\langle\Psi^{s}_{\gamma}\rangle
, so
the
A\langle U_{J}\rangle
-module
M/\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\langle U\rangle\alpha_{j}

is finitely generated, and thus,
M
is finitely generated as well. ∎

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
A
be a left noetherian associative
ring endowed with a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action. Then any smooth finitely generated
left
A\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
W
is noetherian if considered as a left

A\langle U\rangle
-module for any open subgroup
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.

Proof.

The module
W
is a quotient of a finite direct
sum of
A\langle\Psi^{m}\rangle
for some integer
m\geq 0
, while
A\langle\Psi^{m}\rangle

are noetherian by Proposition
4.14
. ∎

In particular, the category of smooth
A\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules
is locally noetherian, i.e., any smooth finitely generated left

A\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module is noetherian.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
K
be a field endowed with an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action;

k:=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
;
Q_{s}
and
\Lambda_{s}
be
k
-vector spaces.
Then any embedding of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules

\iota:\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}Q_{s}\hookrightarrow M:=\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{s}
splits, and

\mathrm{Coker}(\iota)\cong\bigoplus_{s\geq 0}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{s}^{\prime}

for subspaces
\Lambda_{s}^{\prime}\subseteq\Lambda_{s}
of codimension
\dim Q_{s}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
N\geq 0
. As

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle,K\langle\binom{\Psi}{t}\rangle)=0

for
s<t
,
\iota
factors through
\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{s}\subseteq M
.
Fix a subset
S\subset\Psi
of order
N
. Let
\iota_{N}
be the restriction of
\iota
to the
summand
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle\otimes_{k}Q_{N}
. As
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle)\cong K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}}
, it follows from Lemma
4.2
that the
composition of
\iota_{N}
with the projection
M\to K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{N}
is given
on
[S]\otimes Q_{N}
by an injective element
\alpha
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k}(Q_{N},\Lambda_{N})\otimes_{k}K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}}
.
Choose a
k
-vector subspace
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}\subseteq\Lambda_{N}
such that

\Lambda_{N}\otimes_{k}K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}}=\mathrm{Im}(\alpha)\dotplus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}\otimes_{k}K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}}
.
Then
M=\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N-1}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{s}\oplus\mathrm{Im}(\iota_{N})\oplus K\langle\binom{\Psi}{N}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}\oplus\bigoplus_{s>N}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\otimes_{k}\Lambda_{s}
, which reduces the level. ∎

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
K
be a field endowed with a faithful smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action. Then for any morphism

\varphi:M^{\prime}\to M
between smooth finitely generated
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules
there exist an open subgroup
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
and decompositions

M^{\prime}=\ker\varphi\oplus M^{\prime}_{0}
and
M=\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\oplus M_{0}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(U)
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.15
,
\ker\varphi
is finitely generated.
By Proposition
4.6
, there exists an open subgroup
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

such that restrictions to
U
of the inclusions
\iota_{1}:\ker\varphi\hookrightarrow M^{\prime}
and

\iota_{2}:\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\hookrightarrow M
are as in Lemma
4.16
.
Then by Lemma
4.16
,
\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
are split in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(U)
,
i.e.
M^{\prime}=\ker\varphi\oplus M^{\prime}_{0}
and
M=\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\oplus M_{0}
. ∎

Remark 4.18
.

Obviously, a surjection
\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\lambda_{s}}\to\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\kappa_{s}}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

need not be split:
K\langle\Psi\rangle\to K
,
\sum_{i}a_{i}[x_{i}]\mapsto\sum_{i}a_{i}
.

Corollary 4.19
.

In notation of Corollary
4.9
, for any exact sequence

0\to V_{1}\to V_{2}\to\cdots\to V_{n}\to 0
of finitely generated objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
one has
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{i}P_{V_{i}}=0
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.17
, this sequence splits
after restriction to an open subgroup of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, thus giving the claim. ∎

4.6.
The level filtration

Definition 4.20
.

Let
G
be a permutation group of a set
\Psi
,
A
be an
associative ring endowed with a smooth
G
-action.
For each integer
s\geq 0
and each object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
, define

•

\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}V=\widetilde{N}_{s}^{\Psi,G}V
as the image of the natural morphism

\bigoplus_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}A\langle G/G_{J}\rangle\otimes_{A^{G_{J}}}V^{G_{J}}\to V;

•

N_{s}V=N_{s}^{G}V=N_{s}^{\Psi,G}V
as the sum of all subobjects
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
such that there exists
an open subgroup
U\subseteq G
with the property that
\widetilde{N}_{s}^{U}V^{\prime}=V^{\prime}
.

We say that an object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
is
of level

\leq s

with respect to

\Psi
if
V=N_{s}^{\Psi,G}V
.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
G
be a permutation group of a set
\Psi
,
A

be an associative ring endowed with a smooth
G
-action,
V
be an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
. Then

•

\widetilde{N}_{0}^{G}
and
N_{0}^{G}

are independent of
\Psi
;

•

\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}\subseteq N_{s}^{G}
,

\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{G}=\widetilde{N}_{t}^{G}\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}=\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}
and

N_{s}^{G}N_{t}^{G}=N_{t}^{G}N_{s}^{G}=N_{s}^{G}
for
s\leq t
;

•

\widetilde{N}_{0}^{G}V\subseteq\widetilde{N}_{1}^{G}V\subseteq\widetilde{N}_{2}^{G}V\subseteq\cdots

and
N_{0}^{G}V\subseteq N_{1}^{G}V\subseteq N_{2}^{G}V\subseteq\cdots
are functorial exhausting
filtrations. Both filtrations stabilize for finitely generated
V
.

Let
U\subseteq G
be an open subgroup. Then any smooth
A\langle G\rangle
-module
V
is also smooth when considered
as an
A\langle U\rangle
-module, and
N_{s}^{G}V\subseteq N_{s}^{U}V
. If
V\neq N_{0}^{G}V
then
N_{0}^{G}V\neq N_{0}^{U}V
.

Suppose that the set
U\backslash G/U^{\prime}
is finite for any open subgroup
U^{\prime}\subseteq G
.
Then any smooth finitely generated
A\langle G\rangle
-module
is also finitely generated as an
A\langle U\rangle
-module.

Proof.

The equality
\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{G}=\widetilde{N}_{t}^{G}\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}=\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}

for
s\leq t
is evident. Then
V^{\prime}=\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}
satisfies
\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}V^{\prime}=V^{\prime}
, so

V^{\prime}=\widetilde{N}_{s}^{G}V\subseteq N_{s}^{G}V
.

Let us show that
N_{s}^{G}V\subseteq N_{s}^{U}V
, or equivalently, that
V^{\prime}\subseteq N_{s}^{U}V
for any subobject

V^{\prime}\subseteq V
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
such that there exists an open subgroup
U^{\prime}\subseteq G
with the property
that
\widetilde{N}_{s}^{U^{\prime}}V^{\prime}=V^{\prime}
. By definition, the natural morphism
\bigoplus_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}A\langle U^{\prime}/U^{\prime}_{J}\rangle\otimes_{A^{U^{\prime}_{J}}}(V^{\prime})^{U^{\prime}_{J}}\to V^{\prime}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(U^{\prime})
is surjective. Set
U^{\prime\prime}:=U\cap U^{\prime}
.
The left
U^{\prime\prime}
-action on
U^{\prime}/U^{\prime}_{J}
gives a splitting

A\langle U^{\prime}/U^{\prime}_{J}\rangle=\bigoplus_{[g]\in U^{\prime\prime}\backslash U^{\prime}/U^{\prime}_{J}}A\langle U^{\prime\prime}gU^{\prime}_{J}/U^{\prime}_{J}\rangle.

Using the equality
U^{\prime\prime}\cap gU^{\prime}_{J}g^{-1}=U^{\prime\prime}_{g(J)}
and the
U^{\prime\prime}
-equivariant bijection

U^{\prime\prime}gU^{\prime}_{J}/U^{\prime}_{J}\xrightarrow{\sim}U^{\prime\prime}/(U^{\prime\prime}\cap gU^{\prime}_{J}g^{-1})
(
[g]\mapsto[1]
), we see that
the image of
A\langle U^{\prime\prime}gU^{\prime}_{J}/U^{\prime}_{J}\rangle\otimes_{A^{U^{\prime}_{J}}}(V^{\prime})^{U^{\prime}_{J}}
in
V

(
[ug]\otimes v\mapsto ugv
) coincides with the image of

A\langle U^{\prime\prime}/U^{\prime\prime}_{g(J)}\rangle\otimes_{A^{U^{\prime}_{g(J)}}}(V^{\prime})^{U^{\prime}_{g(J)}}
(
[u]\otimes gv\mapsto ugv
),
i.e. the natural morphism
\bigoplus_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}A\langle U^{\prime\prime}/U^{\prime\prime}_{J}\rangle\otimes_{A^{U^{\prime}_{J}}}(V^{\prime})^{U^{\prime}_{J}}\to V^{\prime}
is surjective, and therefore,
the natural morphism
\bigoplus_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}A\langle U^{\prime\prime}/U^{\prime\prime}_{J}\rangle\otimes_{A^{U^{\prime}_{J}}}(V^{\prime})^{U^{\prime\prime}_{J}}\to V^{\prime}
is surjective as well,
i.e.
\widetilde{N}_{s}^{U^{\prime\prime}}V^{\prime}=V^{\prime}
.

The
A\langle G\rangle
-modules
A\langle G/U^{\prime}\rangle
for all open subgroups
U^{\prime}
of
G
form a generating family
of the category of smooth
A\langle G\rangle
-modules. It suffices, thus, to check that
A\langle G/U^{\prime}\rangle
is
a finitely generated
A\langle U\rangle
-module for all open subgroups
U^{\prime}
of
G
. Choose representatives

\alpha_{i}\in G/U^{\prime}
of the elements of
U\backslash G/U^{\prime}
. Then
G/U^{\prime}=\coprod_{i}U\alpha_{i}
,
so
A\langle G/U^{\prime}\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{i}A\langle U/(U\cap\alpha_{i}U^{\prime}\alpha_{i}^{-1})\rangle

is a finitely generated
A\langle U\rangle
-module. ∎

Examples.
1. The finiteness assumption of Lemma
4.21
is valid for any

Roelcke precompact
group
G
, i.e., such that the set
U^{\prime}\backslash G/U^{\prime}
is finite for any open subgroup

U^{\prime}\subseteq G
. Examples of such groups include the profinite groups, the symmetric groups
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,
the automorphism group of a vector space over a finite field [this is shown in Lemma
3.6
];
the open subgroups of any Roelcke precompact group, the products of Roelcke precompact groups.

2. For
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
or
\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
, the restriction functor splits the generators

A\langle\binom{\Psi}{t}_{q}\rangle
into finite direct sums of indecomposable generators via canonical
isomorphisms of
A\langle G_{J}\rangle
-modules

A\langle\binom{\Psi}{t}_{q}\rangle=\bigoplus_{\Lambda\subseteq J}M_{\Lambda}
, where
M_{\Lambda}

is the free
A
-module on
the set of all subobjects of
\Psi
of length
t
and meeting
J
along
\Lambda
.

Example 4.22
.

1. If an object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
is a sum of
G
-invariant finitely generated
A
-submodules
then
V=N_{0}^{\Psi}V
. [Indeed, any finitely generated
A
-module is fixed by an open subgroup of
G
.]

2. If
G
is locally precompact and an open subgroup of
G
acts faithfully on a field
K
then
any object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is of level 0. [Indeed, let
U
be an open precompact subgroup of
G
acting
faithfully on
K
. Then
V=K\otimes_{K^{U}}V^{U}
, so
V=\widetilde{N}_{0}^{U}V
, and therefore,
V=N_{0}^{G}V
.]

For each
j\geq 0
, let
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\leq j}(G)
be the full subcategory of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
of the objects of level

\leq j
. Then the inclusion functor
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\leq j}(G)\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
admits a right adjoint
N_{j}:\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\leq j}(G)
,
V\mapsto N_{j}V
, the maximal subobject of
V
of level
\leq j
.

Corollary 4.23
.

In notation of Corollary
4.9
, for any object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
N_{s}V
consists
of the elements
v\in V
such that
\deg P_{K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle v}\leq s
. In particular, the level filtration

•

on
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is strictly compatible with injections, i.e.

N_{s}V=V\cap N_{s}W
for any inclusion
V\subset W
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\ (
so that
N_{s}E(V)=0
whenever
N_{s}V=0);

•

is strictly compatible
with
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field extensions
\widetilde{K}|K
:
N_{s}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V)=\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}N_{s}V
.

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\leq s}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is a cocomplete Serre subcategory of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
non-complete if
K
is non-trivial.

Proof.

We may assume that
V
is finitely generated, so, for a finite
J\subset\Psi
, its
restriction to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
splits as
\bigoplus_{j}K\langle\binom{\Psi\smallsetminus J}{j}\rangle^{\kappa_{j}}
.
By Lemma
4.2
, any element outside of

\bigoplus_{j\leq s}K\langle\binom{\Psi\smallsetminus J}{j}\rangle^{\kappa_{j}}
generates a

K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}\rangle
-submodule that grows as a polynomial of degree
>s
.

By Corollary
4.19
, the polynomial
P
is additive on exact sequences, so (i)
the level of a subquotient of
V
does not exceed the level of
V
; (ii) if
V
is an extension of
V_{1}

by
V_{2}
then the level of
V
coincides with the maximum of the levels of
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.
∎

Definition 4.24
.

Let
G
be a group and
U\subseteq G
be a subgroup. We say that
U
is an

f-subgroup
if there exist integers
N,m\geq 1
and elements
g_{ij}\in G
for
1\leq i\leq m

and
1\leq j\leq N
such that
\bigcup_{j=1}^{N}g_{1j}Ug_{2j}U\cdots Ug_{mj}=G
.

Example 4.25
.

In the following examples
U
is an f-subgroup of a group
G
.

•

U
is a subgroup of
G
of finite index;

•

U
is an f-subgroup of an f-subgroup of
G
;

•

U
contains an f-subgroup of
G
;

•

U
is an open subgroup of a Roelcke precompact group
G
;

•

U
is a parabolic subgroup of a linear algebraic group
G
over an algebraically closed field;

•

U
is an open subgroup of
G
, which is either
\mathrm{GL}(V)

for an infinite-dimensional vector space
V
or
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)
for an algebraically closed field
extension
F|k
of infinite transcendence degree.

Proof.

The first 4 cases are evident. The last one follows from Lemma
3.6
.
The 5-th case follows from the Bruhat decomposition. ∎

Lemma 4.26
.

Let
G
be a group acting on a left noetherian ring
A
,

U\subseteq G
be an f-subgroup,
V
be an
A\langle G\rangle
-module generated as an
A
-module by

V^{U}
. Then
V
is a sum of
A\langle G\rangle
-submodules noetherian as
A
-modules.

Proof.

For each finitely generated
A
-submodule
B\subseteq V
, denote by
B^{\prime}
an
A
-submodule of
V

containing
B
and generated by finitely many elements of
V^{U}
. Then
B^{\prime}
is an
A\langle U\rangle
-module. For any

v\in V
and any finite collection
g_{1},g_{2},\dots,g_{m}\in G
, the
A
-module
A\langle g_{1}Ug_{2}U\cdots Ug_{m}\rangle v

is contained in the finitely generated
A
-module
(g_{1}((g_{2}(\cdots(g_{m-2}((g_{m-1}((Ag_{m}v)^{\prime}))^{\prime}))^{\prime}\cdots))^{\prime}))^{\prime}
.
As
U
is an f-subgroup of
G
,
A\langle G\rangle v=\sum_{j=1}^{N}A\langle g_{1j}Ug_{2j}U\cdots Ug_{mj}\rangle v
,
so this shows that any cyclic
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule of
V
is contained in a finitely generated
A
-module.
As
A
is noetherian, any cyclic
A\langle G\rangle
-submodule of
V
is a noetherian
A
-module. ∎

Corollary 4.27
.

Let
G
be a permutation group such that any open subgroup is an f-subgroup,
A
be a
left noetherian associative ring endowed with a smooth
G
-action,
V
be an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
.
Then
N_{0}^{G}V
is the sum of all subobjects of
V
noetherian as
A
-modules.

Proof.

If an object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
is finitely generated as
A
-module then its generators are fixed
by an open subgroup of
G
, and thus,
V
is of level 0. Conversely, a level 0 object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)

is a sum of subobjects
V_{i}
generated as
A
-modules by some elements fixed by an open subgroup
U_{i}
of
G
.
By Lemma
4.26
,
V_{i}
is a sum of
A\langle G\rangle
-submodules noetherian as
A
-modules.
∎

4.7.
Existence of weak period extensions

It is not clear so far, whether there exist
G
-period extensions (Definition
2.4
).
However, weak
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-period extensions (Definition
2.4
) do exist.

Proposition 4.28
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a non-constant sheaf of
fields on
\mathrm{FI}^{\mathrm{op}}
. For a fixed infinite set, say
\mathbb{N}
, let

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{J\subset\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{O}_{+J}
be the colimit over the finite
subsets
J
. Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
is a weak period sheaf over
\mathcal{O}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.11
, it suffices to check that any finitely
generated
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
-module
W
can be embedded into a product of copies of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
.

The
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
-module
W
is a quotient of a finite direct sum of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
-modules

h^{I}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\otimes h^{I}_{\mathbb{Z}}
by a submodule.
By Proposition
4.14
, this submodule is finitely generated, so there is a finite set

J
and an
\mathcal{O}_{+J}
-module
W_{0}
such that
W=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}W_{0}
.

By Proposition
4.6
, there is a finite set
J^{\prime}\supseteq J
such that
the
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}
-module
W_{1}:=(W_{0})_{+(J^{\prime}\smallsetminus J)}
is a sum of sheaves

\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}}
. By Lemmas
3.9
and
3.7
,

\bar{h}^{s}_{\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}}
embeds into a product of copies of
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}
for
sufficiently big
J^{\prime}
. As
\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}W_{0}
is embedded into
W_{1}
,

W=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J}}W_{0})

is embedded into
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{+J^{\prime}}}W_{1}
,
which is a sum of sheaves
\bar{h}^{s}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}}
. ∎

5.
The spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for
K=K_{\mathrm{a}},K_{\mathrm{b}},K_{\mathrm{c}}

5.1.
A construction of
G
-extensions with Picard group containing a given abelian group

For a permutation group
G
and a smooth
G
-field
K
, denote by
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
the group of
isomorphism classes of invertible objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, called the
Picard group
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
,
i.e.
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)=H^{1}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(G,K^{\times})
.

The group
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
acts continuously on the Gabriel spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
by

\mathcal{L}:I\mapsto\mathcal{L}\otimes_{K}I
.

Example 5.1

(The fields
K_{\mathrm{a}}=K_{\Psi,S,\Gamma}^{L}
)
.

Fix a quadruple
\mathrm{a}=(\Psi,S,\Gamma,L)
, where
\Psi
and
S
are sets,

L
is a field,
\Gamma
is a subgroup of the group
\Xi:=\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle
.

Let
L(\Psi\times S)=L(S)_{L,\Psi}
be the purely transcendental field extension of
L
with a
transcendence basis consisting of the variables labeled by the set
\Psi\times S
. Denote by
u_{s}
the variable
corresponding to
(u,s)\in\Psi\times S
. For each
s\in S
, sending
u\in\Psi
to
u_{s}
gives
rise to a field embedding
L(\Psi)\hookrightarrow L(\Psi\times S)
,
f\mapsto f_{s}=f^{[s]}
. For all

f\in L(\Psi)^{\times}
and
\gamma=\sum_{s}m_{s}[j_{s}]\in\Xi
, set
f^{\gamma}:=\prod_{s}f_{j_{s}}^{m_{s}}
. Then the
embeddings
u\mapsto u_{s}
give rise to the homomorphism
\Xi\otimes L(\Psi)^{\times}\to L(\Psi\times S)^{\times}
,

\gamma\otimes f\mapsto f^{\gamma}
.

Define a subfield
K_{\mathrm{a}}=K_{\Psi,S,\Gamma}^{L}
of
L(\Psi\times S)
by

K_{\mathrm{a}}:=L\left(u^{\gamma},\frac{u_{s}}{v_{s}}~|~\gamma\in\Gamma,~s\in S,~u,v\in\Psi\right)=L\left(x^{\gamma},\frac{u_{s}}{x_{s}}~|~\gamma\in\Gamma,~s\in S,~u\in\Psi\right)

for an arbitrary fixed
x\in\Psi

(so
K_{\Psi,\varnothing,0}^{L}:=L
,
K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{L}=L(u/v~|~u,v\in\Psi)
).

If
G
is a group acting on the set
\Psi
and on the field
L
then

•

G
acts on the fields
L(\Psi)
and
L(\Psi\times S)
, while the above homomorphisms

L(\Psi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)_{s}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}L(\Psi\times S)
and

\Xi\otimes L(\Psi)^{\times}\to L(\Psi\times S)^{\times}
are
G
-equivariant,

•

G
preserves
the one-dimensional
K_{\mathrm{a}}
-vector subspaces
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\subseteq L(\Psi\times S)
for all
\lambda\in\Xi
,

•

L(\Psi\times S)|K_{\mathrm{a}}
is a
G
-field extension of degree equal to the index of
\Gamma
.

This shows that, for any non-precompact permutation group
G
, any smooth
G
-field
L
, any abelian group
\Lambda
,
there exists a smooth
G
-field extension
K_{\mathrm{a}}|L
and a group embedding
\Lambda\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
.

Lemma 5.2
.

We keep notation of Example
5.1
.

(1)

The
K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle G\rangle
-module
L(\Psi\times S)
embeds into

\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
if
\Gamma
is finitely generated.

(2)

Assume that the
G
-action on
L(\Psi)
is smooth. Then sending
\lambda
to the class of

x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
gives rise to a homomorphism
\Xi\to\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
that
factors through
\iota:\Xi/\Gamma\to\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
. If, moreover, all
G
-orbits on
\Psi
are infinite
then the homomorphism
\iota
is injective.

(3)

Assume that the
G
-action on
L(\Psi)
is smooth, all
G
-orbits on
\Psi
are infinite,

\Gamma
is finitely generated,
\mathrm{Pic}_{L(\Psi\times S)}(G)=0
. Then

•

the map

\iota:\Xi/\Gamma\to\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
,
\lambda\mapsto[x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}]
, is an isomorphism, and

•

the invertible subobjects
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\subset L(\Psi\times S)
form a system of cogenerators
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
if
L(\Psi\times S)
is a cogenerator of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L(\Psi\times S)}(G)
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\Xi
admits a basis
S^{\prime}
such that
\Gamma
is generated
by the elements
m(s)s
for all
s\in S^{\prime}
. (E.g. if
\Gamma
is finitely generated then such an
S^{\prime}
is
provided by the Smith normal form.) Let
S_{0}:=\{s\in S^{\prime}~|~m(s)=0\}
and
S_{+}:=S^{\prime}\smallsetminus S_{0}
.
Fix a total order on
S_{0}
, and order
\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle
lexicographically. Then
the Hahn
\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle
-power series over the field

\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}:=L(x_{j},u_{s}/v_{s}~|~u,v\in\Psi,~s\in S,~j\in S_{+})
form a
G
-field

\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}((x^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle})):=\{\sum_{\lambda\in J}a_{\lambda}x^{\lambda}~|~J\subset\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle\mbox{ is well-ordered, }a_{\lambda}\in\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\}

containing naturally
L(\Psi\times S)
. On the other hand, the
\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}\langle G\rangle
-module

\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}((x^{\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle}))
is
naturally embedded into
\prod_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle}x^{\lambda}\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}
,
while
\widetilde{K_{\mathrm{a}}}=\bigoplus_{\xi\in\Gamma^{\perp}}x^{\xi}K_{\mathrm{a}}
, where

\Gamma^{\perp}=\{\sum_{s\in S_{+}}m_{s}[s]\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{+}\rangle~|~0\leq m_{s}<|m(s)|\}
.
This means that the
K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle G\rangle
-module
L(\Psi\times S)
embeds into

\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
.

If
\mathrm{Pic}_{L(\Psi\times S)}(G)=0
then any element of
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
can be considered as a
subobject of
L(\Psi\times S)
, i.e. a one-dimensional
K_{\mathrm{a}}
-vector subspace
\varphi K_{\mathrm{a}}
for a rational function

\varphi
. Let
S_{0}
be a finite subset such that
\varphi\in L(\Psi\times S_{0})\subseteq L(\Psi\times S)
.
Set
K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}:=L(\Psi\times S_{0})\cap K_{\mathrm{a}}
. Then
\varphi K_{\mathrm{a}}=\varphi K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}}K_{\mathrm{a}}
. We know that
the
K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}\langle G\rangle
-module
L(\Psi\times S_{0})
embeds into

\prod_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}
, so
\varphi K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}\cong x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}

for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle
, and thus,
\varphi K_{\mathrm{a}}\cong x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
.

As
L(\Psi\times S)
is a cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(G)
, to show that the subobjects

x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\subset L(\Psi\times S)
form a system of cogenerators, it suffices to verify that

L(\Psi\times S)
embeds into
\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
. Each element of
L(\Psi\times S)

belongs to
L(\Psi\times S_{0})
for a finite subset
S_{0}\subseteq S
, while
L(\Psi\times S_{0})
embeds
into
\prod_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S_{0}\rangle}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)\times\text{`0'}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
.

If all
G
-orbits on
\Psi
are infinite then
L(\Psi\times S)^{G}\subseteq L
, so
(x^{\gamma}K_{\mathrm{a}})^{G}\neq 0

only if
(x^{\gamma})^{-1}\in K_{\mathrm{a}}
, which means that
\gamma\in\Gamma
. ∎

Remark 5.3
.

Keeping notation of Lemma
5.2
, fix a subset
\Gamma^{\perp}

of
\Xi
projecting bijectively onto the quotient
\Xi/\Gamma
.
As any simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is isomorphic to
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
for a unique

\lambda\in\Gamma^{\perp}
, and the
k
-vector space

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}},\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}})

is isomorphic to
\prod_{\Gamma}k
, the maximal semisimple
K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodule
(the socle) of
\prod_{\lambda\in\Xi}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
is isomorphic to

\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Gamma^{\perp}}x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}\otimes_{k}\prod_{\Gamma}k
. Then the socle of

F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
is
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Gamma^{\perp}}(x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}})^{\oplus\Gamma}
.

Fix some pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
. For each
w\in\Psi
, define the cross-ratio

\xi_{w}:=\frac{(x-w)(z-y)}{(x-z)(w-y)}\in K_{\mathrm{d}}\cup\{\infty\}
of the quadruple

(w,x,y,z)
so that
(w,x,y,z)
is projectively equivalent to
(\xi_{w},0,\infty,1)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Keeping Notation
1.2
, the elements
x,y,z\in\Psi

are algebraically independent over
K_{\mathrm{d}}=F_{\Psi}\left(\xi_{u}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\}\right)
,

K_{\mathrm{c}}=K_{\mathrm{d}}\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}\right)=K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{(F|k)}\cap K_{\mathrm{b}}
,

x/y,x-y
are algebraically independent over
K_{\mathrm{c}}
,
K_{\mathrm{b}}=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x-y)
,
K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{(F|k)}=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x/y)
,
and
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)=K_{\mathrm{d}}(x,y,z)=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x,y)=K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{(F|k)}(x)=K_{\mathrm{b}}(x);
for all

?\in\{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{c},\mathrm{d}\}
, the field extensions
K_{\mathrm{a}}|F_{\Psi}
,
K_{?}|F_{\Psi}
,

F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)|K_{\Psi,S,0}^{(F|k)}
,
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)|K_{?}
are purely transcendental.

Proof.

One has
K_{\mathrm{a}}=F_{\Psi}\left(x^{\gamma},\frac{u_{s}}{x_{s}}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x\}\right)
, since

u^{\gamma}/x^{\gamma}=(u/x)^{\gamma}
and
u_{s}/v_{s}=\frac{u_{s}/x_{s}}{v_{s}/x_{s}}
;

K_{\mathrm{b}}=F_{\Psi}\left(u-x~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x\}\right)
, since
u-v=(u-x)-(v-x)
;

K_{\mathrm{c}}=F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{x-u}{x-y}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y\}\right)
, since

\frac{u-v}{u-w}=\frac{\frac{x-u}{x-y}-\frac{x-v}{x-y}}{\frac{x-u}{x-y}-\frac{x-w}{x-y}}
;

K_{\mathrm{d}}=F_{\Psi}\left(\xi_{u}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\}\right)
, since

\frac{(\xi_{t}-\xi_{u})(\xi_{v}-\xi_{w})}{(\xi_{v}-\xi_{u})(\xi_{t}-\xi_{w})}=\frac{(t-u)(v-w)}{(v-u)(t-w)}

for all pairwise distinct
t,u,v,w\in\Psi
with the obvious meaning when one of
\xi
’s is
\infty
.
Thus, all these fields are purely transcendental over
F_{\Psi}
.

As
\frac{u}{v}=\frac{1-\frac{x-u}{x-y}(1-\frac{y}{x})}{1-\frac{x-v}{x-y}(1-\frac{y}{x})}
,

K_{\Psi,\{\ast\},0}^{(F|k)}=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x/y)
; as
u-v=(x-y)(\frac{x-v}{x-y}-\frac{x-u}{x-y})
,
K_{\mathrm{b}}=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x-y)
;
as
\frac{x-u}{x-z}\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}+\frac{(x-u)(y-z)}{(x-z)(y-u)}-1\right)=\frac{(x-u)(y-z)}{(x-z)(y-u)}\times\frac{x-y}{x-z}
,
K_{\mathrm{c}}=K_{\mathrm{d}}\left(\frac{x-y}{x-z}\right)
. Thus, the extensions

F_{\Psi}(\Psi)|K_{?}
for
?\in\{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{c},\mathrm{d}\}
and
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)|K_{\mathrm{a}}

are purely transcendental if
\Xi/\Gamma
is torsion free. ∎

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.4
in the case
K=K_{\mathrm{a}}

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\Psi
be a set,
F
be a field and
k
be a proper subfield
algebraically closed in
F
,
K=F_{\Psi}
. Then
K
is an injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.12
, it suffices to construct, for any finite
J\subset\Psi
,
a morphism of
K^{(J)}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-modules
\xi:K\to K^{(J)}
identical on
K^{(J)}
. We consider

K
as the fraction field of the algebra
K^{(J)}\otimes_{k}F_{J}
and embed both the algebra and the field into a
field of series with coefficients in
K^{(J)}\otimes_{k}\overline{k}
for an algebraic closure
\overline{k}
of
k
.

For each ordered group
\Gamma
, let
\overline{k}((\Gamma))
be the field of Hahn power series over
\overline{k}
,
i.e. the set of formal expressions of the form
\sum_{s\in\Gamma}a_{s}\cdot s
, where
a_{s}\in\overline{k}

and the set
\{s\in\Gamma~|~a_{s}\neq 0\}
is well-ordered.
Fix a totally ordered divisible group
\Gamma
, such that transcendence degree of the field
extension
k((\Gamma))|k
is at least that of
F|k
.
By
[
11
]
, there is a field embedding
F_{J}\hookrightarrow\overline{k}((\Gamma))
over
k
, so the

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
-field
K
becomes a subfield of
(K^{(J)}\otimes_{k}\overline{k})((\Gamma))
with
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
acting
on the coefficients. Define
\widetilde{\xi}:K\to K^{(J)}\otimes_{k}\overline{k}
as the ‘constant term’ of the Hahn
power series expression:
\sum_{s\in\Gamma}a_{s}\cdot s\mapsto a_{0}
. Fix a
k
-linear functional
\nu:\overline{k}\to k

identical on
k
. Finally, we define
\xi:K\to K^{(J)}
as
(id\cdot\nu)\circ\widetilde{\xi}
. ∎

Lemma 5.6
.

If any non-zero object in a Grothendieck category

\mathcal{A}
admits a simple subquotient, e.g.
\mathcal{A}=\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{A}(G)
for a permutation group
G
and a
unital
G
-ring
A
, then injective hulls of the simple objects form a system of cogenerators.

Proof.

Assume that, for any non-zero morphism
X\to Y
, its image admits a subobject
Z
with a simple
quotient
Q
. Then the composition
Z\to Q\hookrightarrow E(Q)
extends to a morphism
\varphi:Y\to E(Q)

non-zero on
Z
, and therefore, with a non-zero composition
X\to Y\xrightarrow{\varphi}Q\hookrightarrow E(Q)
.
Thus, all such
E(Q)
form a system of cogenerators. ∎

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
\Psi
be a set,
F
be a field
and
k\subsetneqq F
be a subfield algebraically closed in
F
.

Let
K\subseteq F_{\Psi}
be an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant subfield. Then the object
F_{\Psi}
is
an injective cogenerator of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.
In particular,
(i)

any smooth
K
-semilinear representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
can be embedded into a direct product of
copies of
F_{\Psi}
;
(ii)
any smooth
F_{\Psi}
-semilinear representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

of finite length is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
F_{\Psi}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.6
,
for any smooth simple
F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
M
there is a finite
subset
J\subset\Psi
and an isomorphism of
F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-modules

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}F_{\Psi}\langle\binom{\Psi\smallsetminus J}{s}\rangle^{\kappa_{s}}\xrightarrow{\sim}M
for some integer
N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
. By Lemma
4.4
,
the
F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
M
admits a simple

F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-submodule
M^{\prime}
. By Lemma
3.13
,
Remark
3.14
(
1
) and Corollary
3.12
,
there are no simple
F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-submodules in

F_{\Psi}\langle\binom{\Psi\smallsetminus J}{s}\rangle
for
s>0
, so
M^{\prime}
is isomorphic to

F_{\Psi\smallsetminus J}
, again by Corollary
3.12
, and thus,
M
is isomorphic to
F_{\Psi}
.
As
F_{\Psi}
is injective (Proposition
5.5
), Lemma
5.6

shows that
F_{\Psi}
is an injective cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. ∎

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
F|k
,
\Psi
and
K\subseteq F_{\Psi}

be as in Theorem
5.7
. Then any smooth
K
-semilinear irreducible
representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
can be embedded into
F_{\Psi}
.

Proof.

For any smooth simple
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module

V
the
F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V\otimes_{K}F_{\Psi}
admits
a simple quotient isomorphic, by Theorem
5.7
, to
F_{\Psi}
.
This means that
V
can be embedded into
F_{\Psi}
. ∎

As a consequence, we get a description of level 1 ‘rank 1’ objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
:

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
F
be a field and

k\subsetneqq F
be a subfield algebraically closed in
F
. Then there are natural bijections

(1)

between the
F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules of

F_{\Psi}\langle\Psi\rangle
of codimension
s
and the
s
-dimensional
k
-vector subspaces in
F
;

(2)

between the isomorphism classes of

F_{\Psi}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules of
F_{\Psi}\langle\Psi\rangle
of codimension
s
and the

F^{\times}
-orbits of the
s
-dimensional
k
-vector subspaces in the space
F
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.7
, this follows from Lemma
4.8
.
∎

Corollary 5.10
.

The indecomposable objects
K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are injective for all integer
s\geq 0
.

Proof.

This is Lemma
4.13
, since by Theorem
5.7

and Lemmas
5.2

and
4.11
(
1
),
K_{\mathrm{a}}
and
K_{\mathrm{a}}(\Psi)
are injective
objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. ∎

5.2.1.
Invariant subfields of
F_{\Psi}
and one-dimensional groups in characteristic 0

Let
G
be a group, and
\widetilde{K}
be a smooth
G
-field of characteristic
p\geq 0
. Some of
G
-invariant subfields

L\subset\widetilde{K}
may arise, e.g., as the fixed field of a group of
G
-equivariant field automorphisms of
\widetilde{K}
.

Our nearest goal (Theorem
5.13
) is to show that, for a class of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-fields

\widetilde{K}
of characteristic 0, a modification of this construction provides ‘almost all’

G
-invariant subfields (cf. Example
5.11
).

Set
k:=\widetilde{K}^{G}
. Denote by
\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}}(\Omega_{\widetilde{K}|L},\widetilde{K})

the Lie algebra of derivations of
\widetilde{K}
over
L
, and by
\mathbb{D}
the Lie algebra

\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)^{G}=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle}(\Omega_{\widetilde{K}|L},\widetilde{K})

of
G
-equivariant derivations of
\widetilde{K}
over
L
(so
\mathbb{D}
is a left
k
-vector space).

Consider the following squarewise commutative diagram of natural maps:

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$G$-invariant}\\
\mbox{subfields of $\widetilde{K}$}\\
\mbox{containing $L$}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ann}^{G}_{\widetilde{K}}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{restricted Lie}\\
\mbox{$k$-subalgebras of $\mathbb{D}$}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\alpha}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$G$-invariant subfields of}\\
\mbox{$\widetilde{K}$ containing $L\widetilde{K}^{p}$ and}\\
\mbox{separably closed in $\widetilde{K}$}\end{array}\right\}\\
\|&\circlearrowleft&H^{0}(G,-)\uparrow\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\widetilde{K}\otimes_{k}(-)$\hss}&\circlearrowleft&\|\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$G$-invariant}\\
\mbox{subfields of $\widetilde{K}$}\\
\mbox{containing $L$}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$G$-invariant}\\
\mbox{restricted Lie $\widetilde{K}$-sub-}\\
\mbox{algebras of $\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)$}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\beta}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$G$-invariant subfields of}\\
\mbox{$\widetilde{K}$ containing $L\widetilde{K}^{p}$ and}\\
\mbox{separably closed in $\widetilde{K}$}\end{array}\right\}\\
\gamma\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle K\mapsto\widetilde{K}\mbox{-span of }\Omega_{K|L}$\hss}&&\bigcap\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle$-sub-}\\
\mbox{modules of $\Omega_{\widetilde{K}|L}$}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Ann_{\Omega}}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{$\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle$-submodules}\\
\mbox{of $\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)$}\end{array}\right\},\end{array}

where

\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(K):=\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|K)
;

\mathrm{Ann}_{\Omega}(V):=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\widetilde{K}}(\Omega_{\widetilde{K}|L}/V,\widetilde{K})
;

\beta(\mathfrak{a}):=\bigcap_{\xi\in\mathfrak{a}}\ker\xi
;

\alpha(\Lambda):=\beta(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{k}\Lambda)=\bigcap_{\xi\in\Lambda}\ker\xi
.

Then
\beta
is surjective and the composition
\beta\circ\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(-)

is idempotent, so it is identical on the target of
\beta
.
The composition with
\mathrm{Ann}_{\Omega}(-)\circ\gamma
factors through
\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(-)
.

The restriction of
\mathrm{Ann}_{\Omega}(-)
(resp. of
\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(-)
) to the subset
of
\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle
-submodules of finite codimension (resp. of subfields of finite transcendence
codegree) factors through the subset of smooth finite-dimensional
\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle
-submodules of

\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)
.

We are going to restrict our attention to the case of those
\widetilde{K}
for which any smooth
finite-dimensional
\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle
-modules is a direct sum of copies of
\widetilde{K}
.
Then the images under
\mathrm{Ann}_{\Omega}(-)
(resp. under
\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(-)
) of

\widetilde{K}\langle G\rangle
-submodules of finite codimension (resp. of subfields of finite transcendence
codegree) are contained in
\widetilde{K}\otimes_{k}\mathbb{D}\subseteq\mathrm{Der}(\widetilde{K}|L)
. This
implies that the image of
\alpha
contains all subfields of finite transcendence codegree, and the
composition
\alpha\circ H^{0}(G,-)\circ\mathrm{Ann}_{\widetilde{K}}(-)
is identical on such subfields.

Now consider
\widetilde{K}=F_{\Psi}
for a non-trivial field extension
F|k
of characteristic
0
such that
k

is algebraically closed in
F
. The group of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariant field automorphisms of
F_{\Psi}

over
k
is isomorphic to the group
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)
of field automorphisms of
F
over
k
.

Example 5.11
.

If
F
is the function field of a curve
C
of genus
g
over

k
then
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)
is finite if
g>1
, a semi-direct product of
E(k)
for an elliptic curve
E
and
a cyclic group of order
\leq 6
if
g=1
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)\cong\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(k)
if
C
is rational.
Obviously, any subgroup of
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F|k)
fixes the same subfield
K
of
F_{\Psi}
as its Zariski closure
H
,

\mathrm{tr.deg}(F_{\Psi}|K)=\dim H
, and
K
is algebraically closed in
F_{\Psi}
iff
H
is connected.
On the other hand, any infinite
algebraic subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
is, up to conjugation, of one of the following types:

\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
,
B=\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
\mathbb{G}_{m}
,

D_{\infty}=\mathbb{G}_{m}\rtimes\mu_{2}
,
\mathbb{G}_{a}\rtimes\mu_{n}
(
n\geq 1
).

Remark 5.12
.

Given a group
G
, a
G
-field
K
, and a group
H
of

G
-equivariant field automorphisms of
K
, one may wonder, whether the

G
-equivariant field automorphisms of
K^{H}
can be extended to
K
. One can check that this is the case for

G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,
K=k(\Psi)
, and
H
being a connected subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
.

For each
x\in\Psi
, let
(x):F\hookrightarrow F_{\Psi}
be the field embedding identifying

F
with the
x
-th tensor factor in
\bigotimes_{k,\Psi}F
; let
F_{x}
be the image of
(x)
;
for each
R\in F
, let
R(x)
be the image of
R
under
(x)
.

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
\Psi
be an infinite set,
F|k
be a transcendence
degree 1 field extension of characteristic

0
such that
k
is algebraically closed in
F
,
K\neq k
be an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant field extension
of
k
in
F_{\Psi}
algebraically closed in
F_{\Psi}
. Then the transcendence degree
d
of

F_{\Psi}
over
K
is
\leq 3
.
If
d=3
then there exists a unique
R\in\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(k)\backslash(F\smallsetminus k)

such that
K=k\left(\frac{(R(w)-R(x))(R(y)-R(z))}{(R(w)-R(z))(R(x)-R(y))}~|~w,x,y,z\in\Psi\right)
.
If
d=2
then there exists a unique
R\in\mathbb{P}_{k}(F/k)
such that

K=k\left(\frac{R(u)-R(w)}{R(u)-R(v)}~|~u,v,w\in\Psi\right)
.

If
d=1
then there is a system

(\pi_{ij}:W_{i}\to W_{j})_{ij}
of isogenies between torsors
W_{i}
over geometrically irreducible one-dimensional
algebraic
k
-groups
E_{i}
endowed with a compatible system of
k
-field embeddings
\sigma_{i}:k(W_{i})\hookrightarrow F

(i.e.
\sigma_{i}\pi_{ij}^{*}=\sigma_{j}
for all
i,j
) such that
K=\bigcup_{i}(\overline{k}(W_{i})_{\overline{k},\Psi}^{E_{i}(\overline{k})})^{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{k}|k)}\subset\bigcup_{i}k(W_{i})_{\Psi}\subseteq F_{\Psi}
, where

E_{i}(\overline{k})
acts on
\overline{k}(W_{i})_{\Psi}
diagonally.

Proof.

Suppose that
K
contains an element of
F_{\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}}\setminus k
. Then
d\leq n-1
.
For each
x\in\Psi
, the elements of

\mathrm{Der}(F_{\Psi})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}|\{x\}}
preserve
F_{x}
, so the restrictions

\mathrm{Der}(F_{\Psi}|F_{\Psi\smallsetminus\{x\}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}}\xrightarrow{\rho_{x}}\mathrm{Der}(F_{x}|k)

and

\mathrm{Der}(F_{\Psi}|k)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\xrightarrow{r_{x}}\mathrm{Der}(F_{x}|k)
are well-defined. In fact,
\rho_{x}

and
r_{x}
are bijective. Denote by
\eta\mapsto\eta_{x}
the inverse of the composition of
\rho_{x}
with the
isomorphism
\mathrm{Der}(F_{x}|k)\xrightarrow[\sim]{\iota_{x}}\mathrm{Der}(F|k)
(induced by
(x):F\xrightarrow{\sim}F_{x}
). The composition
\iota_{x}\circ r_{x}
is
independent of
x
, and thus, gives a natural Lie
k
-algebra isomorphism

\mathrm{Der}(F|k)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Der}(F_{\Psi}|k)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
,
\eta\mapsto\sum_{u\in\Psi}\eta_{u}
.

It follows from the preceeding discussion, that
K
is the common kernel of
the elements of a finite-dimensional Lie
k
-subalgebra
\Lambda
of
\mathrm{Der}(F|k)
:

K=\bigcap_{\eta\in\Lambda}\ker(\sum_{x\in\Psi}\eta_{x})
.

If
d=2
then let
\eta_{1}
be a non-zero element of
[\Lambda,\Lambda]
, and
\eta_{2}\in\Lambda
be such that

[\eta_{1},\eta_{2}]=\eta_{1}
. Then
\eta_{1}(\eta_{2}/\eta_{1})=1
. Set
R:=\eta_{2}/\eta_{1}\in F\smallsetminus k
, so

\eta_{1}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}
and
\eta_{2}=R\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}
.
As
\eta_{1}
is defined uniquely up to a
k^{\times}
-multiple, and
\eta_{2}
is defined uniquely modulo

[\Lambda,\Lambda]
, the class of
R
in
\mathbb{P}_{k}(F/k)
is well-defined.
Then
K
is contained in the common kernel of
\sum_{x\in\Psi}\frac{\partial}{\partial R(x)}

and
\sum_{x\in\Psi}R(x)\frac{\partial}{\partial R(x)}
, which means that it contains
the intersection of the subfield generated over
k
by
R(x)-R(y)
for all

x,y\in\Psi
and the subfield generated over
k
by
R(x)/R(y)
for all
x,y\in\Psi
.

Fix a rank one valuation
v:F^{\times}/k^{\times}\to\mathbb{Q}
of
F
(e.g. by
embedding of
F\subseteq F\otimes_{k}\overline{k}
into the field of
Puiseux series
\overline{k}((X^{\mathbb{Q}})):=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits\overline{k}((X^{1/N}))
). Let us
show that if
\Lambda
is a
d
-dimensional Lie
k
-subalgebra of
k((X^{\mathbb{Q}}))
then
d\leq 3
. Let

S=\{m_{1},\dots,m_{d}\}\subset\mathbb{Q}
be the set of values of
v
on

(\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X})^{-1}\Lambda\smallsetminus\{0\}
, and
m_{1}<\cdots<m_{d}
.
Choose a basis
(Q_{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X},\dots,Q_{d}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X})
of
\Lambda

such that
Q_{i}=X^{m_{i}}(1+\dots)
. Then
[Q_{i}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X},Q_{j}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X}]=(m_{j}-m_{i})X^{m_{i}+m_{j}-1}(1+\dots)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X}
, so
m_{i}+m_{j}-1\in S
for all
i<j
. As

m_{1}+m_{2}-1\geq m_{1}
, one has
m_{2}\geq 1
. Then
m_{d}>1
(if
d\geq 3
), so
m_{d-1}+m_{d}-1>m_{d-1}
, and thus,

m_{d-1}+m_{d}-1=m_{d}
, which implies
m_{d-1}=1
. This means that
d=3
(and
m_{2}=1
, so
m_{1}=1-m
and

m_{3}=m+1
for some
m>0
). Then
Q_{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X}
and
Q_{3}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}X}

generate a two-dimensional Lie subalgebra
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda
.

If
d=3
then rescale
\eta_{2}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
so that
[\eta_{3},\eta_{2}]=\eta_{3}
. Then, as we have seen in
the case
d=2
,
\eta_{2}=R\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}
and
\eta_{3}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}

for some
R\in F\smallsetminus k
. On the other hand,
[\eta_{1},\eta_{3}]=a\eta_{2}+b\eta_{3}

for some
a,b\in k
, i.e.
-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}(\eta_{1}/\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R})\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}=aR\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}+b\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}
,
or
-\mathrm{d}(\eta_{1}/\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R})=\mathrm{d}(aR^{2}/2+bR)
, and thus,

\eta_{1}=-(aR^{2}/2+bR+c)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R}
for some
c\in k
,
so
\Lambda/\Lambda^{\prime}
is spanned by
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}R^{-1}}
.
Then
K
is the common kernel of
\sum_{x\in\Psi}\frac{\partial}{\partial R(x)}
,

\sum_{x\in\Psi}R(x)\frac{\partial}{\partial R(x)}
and
\sum_{x\in\Psi}R(x)^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial R(x)}
,
which means that it is the intersection of the subfields
k\left(R(x)-R(y)~|~x,y\in\Psi\right)
,

k\left(R(x)/R(y)~|~x,y\in\Psi\right)
, and
k\left(1/R(x)-1/R(y)~|~x,y\in\Psi\right)
.

If
d=1
, we employ the Lefschetz principle, i.e. reduce the problem to the case
of a countable
k
and embed
k
into
\mathbb{C}
. Let
0\neq\eta\in\mathrm{Der}(F|k)
be such that

\sum_{x\in\Psi}\eta_{x}
annihilates
K
. For each
f\in K
, there is a smooth projective curve
\overline{C}

over
k
with an embedding
k(\overline{C})\hookrightarrow F
and a finite subset
S\subset\Psi
such that

f\in k(\overline{C})_{S}\subset k(\overline{C})_{\Psi}
and
\eta\in\mathrm{Der}(k(\overline{C})|k)
. Let

C\subseteq\overline{C}
be the complement to the set of poles of the 1-form
1/\eta
. If considered locally
in the analytic topology on
C
,
1/\eta=du
for an analytic function
u(x)=\int_{*}^{x}1/\eta
. Fix some

v\in\Psi
. Then
f=\varphi(u_{i}-u_{v}~|~i\in S\smallsetminus\{v\})
for an analytic function
\varphi
. As
the path from
\ast
to
x
varies, the values of the branches of
u(x)
are modified by the integral linear
combinations of the periods of the 1-form
1/\eta
. As
f
is non-constant, there are 3 options for the periods:
(1) they are zero; (2) they form a cyclic group
\mathbb{Z}\cdot\omega
for some
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
;
(3) they form a lattice
L
in
\mathbb{C}
.
In the case (1)
u(x)
is rational and
f\in\mathbb{C}(u(x)-u(y)~|~x,y\in\Psi)
. In the case (2)

Q(x):=\exp(\frac{2\pi i}{\omega}u(x))
is rational and
f\in\mathbb{C}(Q(x)/Q(y)~|~x,y\in\Psi)
.
In the case (3), let
\wp_{L}(z):=\frac{1}{z^{2}}+\sum_{\lambda\in L\smallsetminus\{0\}}\left(\frac{1}{(z-\lambda)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\right)
be the Weierstraß
\wp
-function, so

\wp^{\prime}_{L}(z)^{2}=4\wp_{L}(z)^{3}+2a\wp_{L}(z)+b
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{C}
. Then
g(x):=\wp_{L}(u(x))
,

h(x):=\wp^{\prime}_{L}(u(x))
,
\wp_{L}(u(x)-u(y))=\left[\frac{1}{2}\frac{h(x)+h(y)}{g(x)-g(y)}\right]^{2}-g(x)-g(y)

and
\wp^{\prime}_{L}(u(x)-u(y))=\frac{1}{2}(h(x)+h(y))\left[\frac{(g(x)-g(y))(6g(x)^{2}+a)-(h(x)+h(y))h(x)}{(g(x)-g(y))^{3}}\right]-h(x)
are rational and

f\in\mathbb{C}(\wp_{L}(u(x)-u(y)),\wp^{\prime}_{L}(u(x)-u(y))~|~x,y\in\Psi)
.
∎

Lemma
5.2
and Corollary
5.10

list all indecomposable injectives.

Let
K
be of type
K_{\mathrm{a}}
.
Any indecomposable injective object is an injective envelope of a non-zero cyclic subobject,
so we only have to show that any smooth finitely generated
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V
can be embedded
into a direct sum of
x^{\lambda}K
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle

and of
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for several integer
s\geq 1
.

By Proposition
4.6
, there is a subset
\Psi^{\prime}\subset\Psi
with finite
complement
J
and an isomorphism of
K\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-modules

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle^{\kappa_{s}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V
for some
integer
N,\kappa_{0},\dots,\kappa_{N}\geq 0
. In particular,
V^{\prime}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{I\subset\Psi^{\prime}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|I}}
,
where
I
runs over the finite subsets of
\Psi^{\prime}
, can be embedded into

\bigoplus_{s=0}^{N}K\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle^{\kappa_{s}}
.

By Lemma
4.4
, it suffices to show that the
K^{\prime}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-module

K\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle
is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of modules

x^{\lambda}K^{\prime},K^{\prime}\langle\Psi^{\prime}\rangle,K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{2}\rangle,\dots
. (Obviously,

K_{\mathrm{a}}^{\prime}=F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(u^{\gamma},u_{s}/v_{s}~|~\gamma\in\Gamma,~s\in S,~u,v\in\Psi^{\prime})
.)

As
K\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle=K\otimes_{K^{\prime}}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle
, this can be reduced
to the case
s=0
as follows. Assuming that
K
is a direct sum of the required type, we only have
to (i) note that
K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle\to x^{\lambda}K^{\prime}\otimes_{K^{\prime}}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle
,

[J]\mapsto(\sum_{t\in J}t)^{\lambda}\cdot[J]
, is an isomorphism for any
s\geq 1
, and (ii) to check that

K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{n}\times\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{j=0}^{n+s}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{j}\rangle^{\oplus N_{j}}
. It is clear that

K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{n}\times\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\min(n,s)}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{j,n-j,s-j}\rangle
,

[(I,J)]\mapsto[(I\cap J,I\smallsetminus I\cap J,J\smallsetminus I\cap J)]
,
where
\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{j,n-j,s-j}
denotes the set of triples of disjoint subsets of
\Psi^{\prime}
of orders
j,n-j,s-j
.
According to Example
3.16
,
K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{j,n-j,s-j}\rangle

is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{n+s-j}\rangle
.

Now we treat the case of
s=0
. By Lemma
4.13
, the objects

x^{\lambda}K^{\prime},K^{\prime}\langle\Psi^{\prime}\rangle,K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{2}\rangle,\dots
are injective, while by
Corollary
4.15
, the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{\prime}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J})
is locally noetherian,
so all direct sums of their copies are injective as well.
This means that we can split off any direct sum of isomorphic copies of the above objects from
K
. It is thus
sufficient to show that the
K^{\prime}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-submodule in
K\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle

generated by any element
\alpha
is contained in a submodule isomorphic to a finite direct sum

\bigoplus_{\lambda}(x^{\lambda}K^{\prime})^{m_{\lambda}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\geq 1}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s_{i}}\rangle^{n_{i}}
.

As
F_{\Psi}
is the fraction field of
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}\otimes_{k}F_{J}
, in the case
K=F_{\Psi}
it suffices
to show that, for any field extension
L|k
in which
k
is the algebraically closed, any element
\alpha

in the fraction field of
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}\otimes_{k}L
is contained in a
