K^{\prime}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}\rangle
-submodule
isomorphic to a finite direct sum
\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s_{i}}\rangle^{n_{i}}
.

For any group
G
and a
G
-field
M
, let
M(t)|M
be a simple (purely) transcendental
G
-field
extensions such that the line
tM
is
G
-invariant. Consider the partial fraction decomposition

(2)

M(t)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}t^{n}M\oplus\bigoplus_{m=1}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{O}\bigoplus_{j=0}^{\deg O-1}\left(t^{j}\bigoplus_{P\in O}P(t)^{-m}M\right),

where
O
runs
over the
G
-orbits of (non-constant) irreducible polynomials
P
over
M
such that
P(0)=1
. Here
the
M\langle G\rangle
-module
\bigoplus_{P\in O}P(t)^{-m}M
is isomorphic to
M\langle G/\mathrm{Stab}_{P}\rangle
.

We proceed by induction on minimal order
d
of a subset
T\subset L
such that
\alpha
is algebraic over
the fraction field of
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}\otimes_{k}k(T)
, the case
d=0
being trivial.
We may assume that
L|k(T)
is finite. Then, in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{\prime}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J})
, the fraction field of

F_{\Psi^{\prime}}\otimes_{k}L
is isomorphic to
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(T)^{[L:k(T)]}
. Fix some
t\in T
. Taking
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}

and
M=F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(T\smallsetminus\{t\})
in decomposition (
2
), we see that

F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(T)=M(t)
is a direct sum of objects isomorphic to
M\langle G/U\rangle
for open subgroups
U
of
G
.
By induction assumption,

F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(T\smallsetminus\{t\})
is already of required type, while

\bigoplus_{P\in O}F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(T\smallsetminus\{t\})\cdot P(t)^{-m}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of objects

K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s_{i}}\rangle\otimes\mathbb{Z}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}/\mathrm{Stab}_{P}\rangle

for some open subgroups
\mathrm{Stab}_{P}\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}
. Applying Lemmas
3.7
and

3.15
and the above isomorphism

K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{n}\times\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{j=0}^{n+s}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{j}\rangle^{\oplus N_{j}}
, we see that the latter object is of required type.
This completes the induction step.

In the case
K=K_{\mathrm{a}}
,
K
is contained in
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\times S)
which is isomorphic in

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J})
to a direct sum of copies of

F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle=F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)\otimes_{K^{\prime}}K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle
for
s\geq 0
. To show that

F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)
is isomorphic in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{\prime}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J})
to a direct sum of copies of
injective objects
K^{\prime}\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle
for
s\geq 1
and
x^{\lambda}K^{\prime}
, it suffices to
check that any element of
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)
belongs to a direct sum of subobjects of such type.
We may, thus, assume
S
to be finite, and proceed by induction on
\#S
. As in the proof of
Lemma
5.2
, after a base change of
\mathbb{Z}\langle S\rangle
, we may assume
that
\Gamma
is
generated by the elements
m(i)[i]
for all
i\in S
.
As
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)=K^{\prime}(x_{i}~|~i\in S)
for any
x\in\Psi^{\prime}
, we again use decomposition
(
2
) with
M=K_{\mathrm{b},t}
for some
t\in S
and
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi^{\prime}}

to see that
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)
is a direct sum of objects isomorphic to
x_{t}^{d}K_{\mathrm{b},t}
for
all
d\in\mathbb{Z}
or
K_{\mathrm{b},t}\langle G/U\rangle
for open subgroups
U
of
G
. Applying arguments
similar to the above ones, we conclude that
F_{\Psi^{\prime}}(\Psi^{\prime}\times S)\langle\binom{\Psi^{\prime}}{s}\rangle

admits a decomposition of desired type. ∎

5.3.
The projective line
\mathbb{Y}
over
K_{\mathrm{d}}
;
invertible objects
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

For any field extension
L|K
, denote by
\mathrm{Val}_{L|K}
the set of all discrete
valuations
L^{\times}\longrightarrow\hskip-8.53581pt\to\mathbb{Z}
trivial on
K
.

Lemma 5.14
.

•

\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
can be identified
naturally with the set of closed points of a projective line
\mathbb{Y}
over
K_{\mathrm{d}}
;
the natural
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action on
\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
is smooth and degree-preserving.

•

For each triple of pairwise distinct
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\Psi
there is a unique

v_{\gamma}\in\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
with
v_{\gamma}\left(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\beta-\gamma}\right)>0
.
This
v_{\gamma}
does not depend on
\alpha,\beta
. The map
\Psi\to\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
,

\gamma\mapsto v_{\gamma}
, is an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariant injection;
it identifies
\Psi
with an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbit
\Theta:=\{v_{\gamma}~|~\gamma\in\Psi\}
.

•

For any
\gamma\in\Psi
, the valuation
v_{\gamma}
is trivial on the subfield

\varkappa_{\gamma}:=F_{\Psi}\left(\frac{u-v}{v-w}~|~u,v,w\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{\gamma\}\right)

of
K_{\mathrm{c}}
. The residue field of
v_{\gamma}
is identified naturally with
\varkappa_{\gamma}
.

•

If
v\in\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
is fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{\alpha,\gamma\}}
for some

\alpha,\gamma\in\Psi
then
v\in\{v_{\alpha},v_{\gamma}\}
.

Proof.

For any
\alpha^{\prime}\neq\alpha,\gamma
, the element
\frac{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha^{\prime}-\gamma}

is a
K_{\mathrm{d}}^{\times}
-multiple of
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\beta-\gamma}
, so their valuations coincide.
If
v\in\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
is positive on
\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\beta-\gamma}
then

0=v\left(1+\frac{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha^{\prime}-\gamma}\right)=v\left(\frac{\alpha-\gamma}{\alpha^{\prime}-\gamma}\right)
,
so
v\left(\frac{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha^{\prime}-\gamma}\right)=v\left(\frac{\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}}{\alpha-\gamma}\right)=v\left(\frac{\beta^{\prime}-\alpha^{\prime}}{\beta^{\prime}-\gamma}\right)
for arbitrary
\beta^{\prime}\neq\alpha^{\prime}
distinct from

\gamma
. Thus, the stabilizer of
v
is
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{\gamma\}}
, and sending

v
to
\gamma
gives rise to an isomorphism of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-sets
\Theta\xrightarrow{\sim}\Psi
.

Any element
f\in\varkappa_{\gamma}^{\times}
is fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}
for a finite subset

S\subset\Psi\smallsetminus\{\gamma\}
. Then
v_{\beta}(f)=v_{\gamma}(f)
for any
\beta
in
the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}
-orbit
\Psi\smallsetminus S
of
\gamma
. As only finitely many
valuations are non-zero on
f
,
v_{\gamma}(f)=0
.

By Lemma
5.4
, the elements of
K_{\mathrm{c}}
can be considered as rational functions over

K_{\mathrm{d}}
(and over
\varkappa_{\gamma}
) in the variable
T:=\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\beta-\gamma}\in K_{\mathrm{c}}
,
so each
v\in\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}\smallsetminus\{v_{\alpha}\}
corresponds to a closed point of

\mathbb{A}^{1}_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}
, i.e. to a maximal ideal in
K_{\mathrm{d}}[T]
, or to a monic irreducible polynomial

P=\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}T^{i}\in K_{\mathrm{d}}[T]
. For any
g\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{\alpha,\gamma\}}
,

P^{g}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}^{g}\left(\frac{(\alpha-\beta^{g})(\beta-\gamma)}{(\alpha-\beta)(\beta^{g}-\gamma)}\right)^{i}T^{i}
,
so if
v
is fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{\alpha,\gamma\}}
then
a_{i}\left(\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\beta-\gamma}\right)^{n-i}\in K_{\mathrm{c}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{\alpha,\gamma\}}}=F_{\{\alpha,\gamma\}}
,
i.e.
a_{i}=0
for
i\neq n
, and thus,
v=v_{\gamma}
(and
n=1
). ∎

Notation 5.15
.

•

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Y}}:U\mapsto\{f\in K_{\mathrm{c}}~|~v(f)\geq 0\ \mbox{for all }v\in U\}

is the structure sheaf of
\mathbb{Y}
;

•

for each integer
n
,
\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}
is the
n
-th tensor
power of the dualizing sheaf
\Omega_{\mathbb{Y}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
of
\mathbb{Y}
;

•

for each point
v
of
\mathbb{Y}
,
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},v}^{n}
is the stalk of
\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}
at
v
;

•

\eta
is the general point of
\mathbb{Y}
, so

•

\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}\cong(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
is the
one-dimensional
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-vector space of rational sections of
\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}
.

Lemma 5.16
.

For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is a unique
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodule
of
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
isomorphic to
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
. Namely, a natural isomorphism

\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}
is determined by

\left(\frac{z-x}{y-z}\mathrm{d}\frac{y-z}{z-x}\right)^{n}\mapsto(x-y)^{n}

for all pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
.

Proof.

Fix pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
. Set
T:=\frac{y-z}{z-x}\in K_{\mathrm{c}}
and

\xi_{w}:=\frac{(w-x)(y-z)}{(w-y)(x-z)}\in K_{\mathrm{d}}\cup\{\infty\}
.
As
\frac{x-y}{x-w}=\xi_{w}^{-1}T+1
, for any
u,v\in\Psi
, one has

\frac{u-v}{x-y}=\frac{x-v}{x-y}-\frac{x-u}{x-y}=\frac{1}{\xi_{v}^{-1}T+1}-\frac{1}{\xi_{u}^{-1}T+1}=\frac{\xi_{v}}{T+\xi_{v}}-\frac{\xi_{u}}{T+\xi_{u}}=\frac{(\xi_{v}-\xi_{u})T}{(T+\xi_{v})(T+\xi_{u})}
, so for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,

T^{\sigma}=\frac{\frac{z^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}{x-y}}{\frac{x^{\sigma}-z^{\sigma}}{x-y}}=\frac{\frac{(\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{z^{\sigma}})T}{(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})(T+\xi_{z^{\sigma}})}}{\frac{(\xi_{z^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}})T}{(T+\xi_{z^{\sigma}})(T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}})}}=\frac{\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{z^{\sigma}}}{\xi_{z^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}\cdot\frac{T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}{T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}}}
.
Then
\frac{\mathrm{d}T^{\sigma}}{T^{\sigma}}=\frac{\mathrm{d}T}{T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}-\frac{\mathrm{d}T}{T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}}}=\frac{(\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}})\mathrm{d}T}{(T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}})(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})}=\frac{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}{x-y}\frac{\mathrm{d}T}{T}
. This shows that if a
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-linear map

\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}\to F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
sends
\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}T}{T}\right)^{n}
to

(x-y)^{n}
then it sends
\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}T^{\sigma}}{T^{\sigma}}\right)^{n}
to
(x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma})^{n}
,
i.e. it is
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariant, and gives the required isomorphism.
As
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n},F_{\Psi}(\Psi))\cong F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
, one
has
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n},F_{\Psi}(\Psi))\cong F_{\Psi}(\Psi)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=k
, i.e. any morphism is a
k
-multiple of this isomorphism.
∎

5.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.4
in the cases
K=K_{\mathrm{b}}
and
K=K_{\mathrm{c}}

For a field
k
of characteristic
p
, let
\mathbb{D}
be the completed free divided power
k
-algebra
on one generator, i.e. the elements of
\mathbb{D}
are formal series
a_{0}+\sum_{i\geq 1}a_{i}D^{(i)}
,
where
a_{i}\in k
, with multiplication
D^{(i)}D^{(j)}=\binom{i+j}{i}D^{(i+j)}
and PD-structure

(D^{(i)})^{(j)}=\frac{(ij)!}{(i!)^{j}j!}D^{(ij)}
for all integer
i,j\geq 1
.
This PD-
k
-algebra coincides with
k[[D^{(1)}]]
if
p=0
; it coincides with

k[[D^{(1)},D^{(p)},D^{(p^{2})},\dots]]/((D^{(1)})^{p},(D^{(p)})^{p},(D^{(p^{2})})^{p},\dots)
if
p>0
.

By Lucas’s theorem,
[
10
]
, if
n=\sum_{i\geq 0}n_{i}p^{i}
and
m=\sum_{i\geq 0}m_{i}p^{i}
for some

0\leq n_{i},m_{i}<p
then
\binom{n}{m}\equiv\prod_{i}\binom{n_{i}}{m_{i}}\pmod{p}
. The set
\mathbb{N}

is partially ordered by
n\succeq_{p}m
if
p\nmid\binom{n}{m}
, i.e.
m_{i}\leq n_{i}
for all
i
.

For each integer
n\geq 0
, denote by
\delta_{p}(n)
the sum of the digits of
n
in the base
p
, if
p>0
.

Proposition 5.17
.

The socle series
\Phi_{\bullet}
of

K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]
is exhausting,
\Phi_{0}=K_{\mathrm{c}}
and

\Phi_{s}
is the
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-linear envelope of the products of the
s
-tuples of elements of
\Phi_{1}
.
The socle series of the object
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is exhausting as well,
and coincides with
K_{\mathrm{b}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\Phi_{\bullet}
.
Let
p
be the characteristic of
k
.

Then
\Phi_{s}
is the
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-linear span of

\frac{x^{n}}{(x-y)^{n}}
for all
n\geq 0
with
\delta_{p}(n)\leq s
, where
\delta_{0}(n):=n
.

If
p=0
then
the module
K_{\mathrm{c}}[\frac{x}{x-y}]
is uniserial
(
its submodules are totally ordered by inclusion
);

the module
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<s}
admits the injective resolution

0\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<s}\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{d}^{s}/\mathrm{d}^{s}x}K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\to 0
,
so
\mathrm{Ext}^{\geq 2}_{K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(-,V)=0
for any
V
of finite length.

For any
s>0
, there exist indecomposable modules of length
s+1
with the socle
\cong K_{\mathrm{b}}^{s}
.

The
k
-algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x])
is isomorphic to

\mathbb{D}
, so it is local
;\ \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x],K_{\mathrm{b}})=0
.

Each smooth indecomposable
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V
of length
2
is isomorphic to
a unique submodule of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
, which corresponds to a point of
\mathbb{P}_{k}((K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}})
.

Proof.

The
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-linear map
\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1}\to\bigoplus_{n:~\delta_{p}(n)=s}(x-y)^{-n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
,

\left(\frac{x}{x-y}\right)^{n}\mapsto(x-y)^{-n}
,
is an isomorphism, since

\sigma\left(\frac{x}{x-y}\right)^{n}=\sigma\left(\left(\frac{x}{x-y}\right)^{p^{i_{1}}+\cdots+p^{i_{s}}}\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{s}\left(\left(\frac{x-y}{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}\right)^{p^{i_{j}}}\left(\frac{x}{x-y}\right)^{p^{i_{j}}}+\left(\frac{x^{\sigma}-x}{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}\right)^{p^{i_{j}}}\right)\\
\equiv\left(\frac{x-y}{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}\right)^{n}\left(\frac{x}{x-y}\right)^{n}\bmod{\Phi_{s-1}\cap K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]^{\leq n-p^{i_{1}}}}

for any
n=p^{i_{1}}+\cdots+p^{i_{s}}
,

i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}
, and
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. In particular,
\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1}
and

K_{\mathrm{b}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1})
are semisimple. It remains to check that
\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1}
(resp.

K_{\mathrm{b}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1})
) is the socle of
K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]/\Phi_{s-1}
(resp.
of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\Phi_{s-1}
).
We proceed by induction on
s\geq 0
(with
\Phi_{-1}:=0
), the case
s=0
being evident.

The operators
D^{(i)}:K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
,
x^{n}\mapsto\binom{n}{i}x^{n-i}
, commute with
the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action for all
i\geq 1
, so
D^{(i)}
induce
(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=k
.
If
p>0
and
n=mp^{i}
with
m>1
prime to
p
then
D^{(p^{i})}x^{n}=\binom{n}{p^{i}}x^{n-p^{i}}=mx^{n-p^{i}}\neq 0
,
and therefore,
(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\subseteq\{\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}x^{p^{i}}~|~a_{i}\in K_{\mathrm{b}}\}
. If

Q=\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}x^{p^{i}}\in(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
then
Q^{\sigma}\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{n}a_{i}^{\sigma}x^{p^{i}}\pmod{K_{\mathrm{b}}}

for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, so
a_{i}\in K_{\mathrm{b}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=k
. If
p=0
then
(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=K_{\mathrm{b}}\cdot x
.
This identifies
\Phi_{1}
.

Let us check that the operators
D^{(i)}
map
\Phi_{s}
to
\Phi_{s-1}
for all
i\geq 1
. This is trivial if
p=0
,
so assume that
p>0
. By Lucas’s theorem,
p\nmid\binom{n}{i}
if and only if
n\succeq_{p}i
, so if
x^{n}\in\Phi_{s}

then either
D^{(i)}x^{n}=0
or
n\succeq_{p}i
and
x^{n-i}\in\Phi_{s-1}
. Therefore, all
D^{(i)}
induce

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/\Phi_{s}\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/\Phi_{s-1}
and
(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/\Phi_{s})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\to(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/\Phi_{s-1})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=(\Phi_{s}/\Phi_{s-1})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
. Moreover, if
x^{n}\notin\Phi_{s-1}
and
n\succeq_{p}p^{j}
then

x^{n-p^{j}}\notin\Phi_{s-2}
.
This shows that if an element does not belong to
\Phi_{s+1}
then some
D^{(i)}
maps it outside of
\Phi_{s}
,
so it is not fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
modulo
\Phi_{s}
, thus completing the induction step.

An
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-endomorphism
\varphi
of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
sends the element
x^{n}\in K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}}
to some

P_{n}^{x}(x)\in K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|x}}=F_{x}[x]
. Then
y^{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\binom{n}{i}x^{i}(y-x)^{n-i}

is sent to
\sum_{i=0}^{n}\binom{n}{i}(y-x)^{n-i}P_{i}^{x}(x)
if
\varphi
is
K_{\mathrm{b}}
-linear, so

P_{n}^{y}(y)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\binom{n}{i}(y-x)^{n-i}P_{i}^{x}(x)
. Comparing the coefficients of the polynomials,
we see that
P_{n}^{y}(y)\in k[y]
. As the left hand side is independent of
x
, we get

P_{n}(y)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\binom{n}{i}P_{i}(0)y^{n-i}
, and thus, the map
\varphi
is given by

\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}D^{(i)}
.

If
p=0
then
D^{(i)}=D^{i}/i!
, so
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x])=k[[D]]
.
If
p>0
then
D^{(i)}:x^{n}\mapsto(-1)^{i}x^{n-i}
for any
n\equiv-1\pmod{p^{m}}
with
p^{m}>i
,
so
D^{(i)}
are (topologically) linearly independent.

Given a smooth indecomposable
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V
of length 2, an isomorphism of the
socle of
V
onto
K_{\mathrm{b}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
can be extended to a morphism to
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
, which is injective, since the
socle is essential. To check uniqueness, it suffices to show that the submodules corresponding to distinct
points of
\mathbb{P}_{k}((K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}})
are non-isomorphic. Indeed, as
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
is injective,
any isomorphism between submodules of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
extends to an endomorphism of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
, which is scalar on

(K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]/K_{\mathrm{b}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
.

For any
s\geq 0
, the dual of
\{a+b_{0}x+\cdots+b_{s}x^{p^{s}}~|~a,b_{0}\dots,b_{s}\in K_{\mathrm{b}}\}
is

\{(a_{0}+bx,\dots,a_{s}+bx^{p^{s}})~|~a_{0},\dots,a_{s},b\in K_{\mathrm{b}}\}
(where the pairing is

\langle(a_{0}+bx,\dots,a_{s}+bx^{p^{s}}),a+b_{0}x+\cdots+b_{s}x^{p^{s}}\rangle=ab-a_{0}b_{0}-\cdots-a_{s}b_{s}
;
this pairing is equivariant).
It is indecomposable, since so is the former. ∎

Theorem 5.18
.

In the notation of Theorem
1.4
, the indecomposable injectives
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are injective hulls
P_{s}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})}
of
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

for all integer
s\geq 0
, where
P_{0}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})}=K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
,
P_{s}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})}=K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

for all
s\geq 1
, except that
P_{2}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})}=K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle

if
F=k
and
p=2

(
and
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(P_{2}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})})\cong k[X]/(X^{2}))
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.7
,

F_{\Psi}(\Psi)=F(X)_{\Psi}
is an injective cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. For any
x\in\Psi
,
one has
F(X)_{\Psi}=K_{\mathrm{b}}(x)=K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\oplus\bigoplus_{R}\bigoplus_{m\geq 1}V^{(m)}_{R}
, where
R
runs over
the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbits of non-constant irreducible monic polynomials in
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
and
V^{(m)}_{R}
is
the
K_{\mathrm{b}}
-linear envelope of
P(x)/Q^{m}
for all
Q\in R
and
P\in K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
with
\deg P<\deg Q
. As
F(X)_{\Psi}

is injective, its direct summand
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
is also injective, as well as
V^{(m)}_{R}
are for all
R
and
m
.

Each
V^{(m)}_{R}
is filtered by
V^{(j,m)}_{R}
,
0\leq j<\deg R
, where
V^{(j,m)}_{R}
is the

K_{\mathrm{b}}
-linear envelope of
P(x)/Q^{m}
for all
Q\in R
and
P\in K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
with
\deg P\leq j
. Clearly,
these decomposition and filtration are independent of
x
. Moreover, for all
0\leq j<\deg R
and
m
the map

V^{(j,1)}_{R}\to V^{(j,m)}_{R}
,
P(x)/Q\mapsto P(x)/Q^{m}
, is an isomorphism of
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules.

We separate the case where
F=k
and the characteristic
p
of
k
is 2. We denote this case by
\star
.

Let us show that,

•

for any
R
and
0\leq j<\deg R
, the

K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V^{(j,1)}_{R}
is non-canonically isomorphic to

(K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}\rangle)^{j+1}
, where
Q\in R
is arbitrary, but in the
\star

case we exclude those
R
with
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x,y\}}
for some
x\neq y
in
\Psi
;

•

in the
\star
case, if
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x,y\}}
for some
R
,
Q\in R
and
x\neq y
in
\Psi
,
and
0\leq j<\frac{1}{2}\deg R
, the
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-module
V^{(2j+1,1)}_{R}
is non-canonically
isomorphic to
(K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle)^{j+1}
.

(In particular, by Lemma
3.13
,
there are no simple
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules in
V^{(1)}_{R}
).

We proceed by induction on
0\leq j<\deg R
, the case
j=0
being obvious, since the morphism

K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}\rangle\to V^{(0,1)}_{R}
,
[g]\mapsto(gQ)^{-1}
, is an isomorphism.

Let
R
contain
Q=x_{1}+\sum_{i=2}^{s}(x_{i}-x_{1})^{2}
for some
s\geq 1
and pairwise
distinct
x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\in\Psi
. Then (i)
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}
is of index
s
in

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\}}
;
(ii)
V_{R}^{(1)}\cong K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi\rangle
if
s=1
; (iii)

V_{R}^{(1)}\cong K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
if
s=2
.
By Lemma
3.15
, this implies that
V^{(1)}_{R}
is isomorphic to

K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{s}
, unless
F=k
,
p=2
and
s=2
.
According to Example
3.16
, this implies that
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U\rangle
is injective
for any proper open subgroup
U\subset\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, with the exception of
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x,y\}}
for

x\neq y
in
\Psi
in the
\star
case.

Now
R
is arbitrary. By the induction hypothesis,
V^{(j-1,m)}_{R}
is isomorphic to

(K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}\rangle)^{j}
, and thus, injective. Then the inclusion

V^{(j-1,1)}_{R}\hookrightarrow V^{(j,1)}_{R}
splits:

V^{(j,1)}_{R}\cong V^{(j-1,1)}_{R}\oplus(V^{(j,1)}_{R}/V^{(j-1,1)}_{R})
. Then the canonical isomorphisms

x^{j}\cdot:V^{(0,m)}_{R}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{(j,1)}_{R}/V^{(j-1,1)}_{R}
complete the induction step.

Let
V\subseteq K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
be a non-zero
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodule. Let
Q\in V
be a monic
polynomial in
x
. Then
V
contains
Q-\sigma Q
for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. If
\sigma Q\neq Q
for some

\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
then
Q-\sigma Q
is a nonzero polynomial of degree
<\deg Q
. In particular, if
Q
is
of minimal degree then
Q-\sigma Q=0
for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, which means that
Q\in k
, i.e.
V

contains
K_{\mathrm{b}}
. This implies that
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
is an injective hull of
K_{\mathrm{b}}
, in particular, all

K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-submodules of
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
are indecomposable.

We know that any simple object is isomorphic to
K_{\mathrm{b}}
, so by Lemma
5.6
,
the injective hull
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]
of
K_{\mathrm{b}}
is a cogenerator.

The remaining indecomposable injectives are described in a way similar to the case of
K_{\mathrm{a}}
. Namely, we have to
decompose
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle=F_{\Psi}(\Psi)\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for all
s\geq 1
. The summands of type
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U\rangle
in

F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
give the same type of summands in
F_{\Psi}(\Psi)\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
.
Now, if
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
is injective then, by the above inductive argument,

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\cong K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\oplus n}
, so

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
.
Finally, if
p=2
then

K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle\to K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<2}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle
,

(x,y)\mapsto(x\{x,y\},y\{x,y\})
, is an isomorphism, so reasoning by induction we see that

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<2n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle
is injective for any
n\geq 1

and
K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]^{<2n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle\cong K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle^{\oplus n}
, and thus,

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
. ∎

Proposition 5.19
.

Any simple object of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is isomorphic to
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

The object
M^{(n)}:=\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]\cong(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]\subset F_{\Psi}(\Psi)
is an injective hull of

\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
.

The remaining isomorphism classes of indecomposable injective objects
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are presented by
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi\rangle
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
direct summands of
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
, and

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for
s\geq 3
.

Remark 5.20
.

By Proposition
5.17
, the socle series
\Phi^{(n)}_{\bullet}
of

M^{(n)}
is exhausting and coincides with
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\Phi_{\bullet}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
act on
K_{\mathrm{c}}[X]
as it does on
K_{\mathrm{c}}[x-y]
, i.e. by
\sigma:\sum_{i}a_{i}X^{i}\mapsto\sum_{i}a_{i}^{\sigma}\left(\frac{x^{\sigma}-y^{\sigma}}{x-y}\right)^{i}X^{i}
.
Then
K_{\mathrm{b}}=K_{\mathrm{c}}(x-y)=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\oplus\bigoplus_{R}\bigoplus_{m\geq 1}V_{R}^{(m)}
,
where
R
runs over the set of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbits of non-constant irreducible polynomials

Q(X)\in K_{\mathrm{c}}[X]
with
Q(0)=1
and
V_{R}^{(m)}
is the
K_{\mathrm{c}}
-vector subspace of
K_{\mathrm{b}}
spanned by

(x-y)^{j}/Q(x-y)^{m}
for all
0\leq j<\deg R
and all
Q\in R
, so

K_{\mathrm{b}}[x]=K_{\mathrm{b}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{(n)}\oplus\bigoplus_{R}\bigoplus_{m\geq 1}V_{R}^{(m)}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right].

In particular,

M^{(n)}
and
V_{R}^{(1)}
are injective for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
R
. Note that
for all
m
the map
V^{(1)}_{R}\to V^{(m)}_{R}
,
P(x-y)/Q(x-y)\mapsto P(x)/Q(x-y)^{m}
, is an isomorphism of

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle
-modules.

Any simple object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
can be embedded into a simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{b}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,
i.e. into
K_{\mathrm{b}}
.
As
K_{\mathrm{b}}
admits an
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant discrete valuation trivial on
K_{\mathrm{c}}
and positive on
x-y
,
K_{\mathrm{b}}

embeds
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-equivariantly into
K_{\mathrm{c}}((x-y))=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{m}\prod\limits_{n\geq-m}(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\subset\prod\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
, so
V
is isomorphic to
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
for some
n
.

The natural map
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(M,M^{\prime})\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(M\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\mathcal{L},M^{\prime}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}\mathcal{L})
is bijective
for any invertible
\mathcal{L}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, so

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}((x-y)^{m}K_{\mathrm{c}},M^{(n)})\cong(M^{(n-m)})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=\begin{cases}k,&\mbox{if $n=m$,}\\
0,&\mbox{if $n\neq m$.}\end{cases}

This means that the socle of
M^{(n)}
is
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
, so
M^{(n)}
is an injective hull of
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
.
As
M^{(n)}
is artinian, the socle series
\Phi^{(n)}_{\bullet}
of
M^{(n)}
is exhausting.

Let
R
contain
Q(x_{1}-x_{2})=(x_{1}-x_{2})\prod_{i=3}^{s}\frac{x_{i}-x_{1}}{x_{i}-x_{2}}+1
for some
s\geq 2
and pairwise
distinct
x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\in\Psi
. Then (i)
V_{R}^{(1)}\cong K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}\rangle
;
(ii)
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}
is of index
s(s-1)
in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\}}
for all
s\geq 2
, with the
exception of
s=2,3
when
p=2
, where
\mathrm{Stab}_{Q}
is of index
2s-3
in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\}}
.
By Lemma
3.15
, if
U
is an open subgroup in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,\{x_{1},\dots,x_{s}\}}

of a finite index
\kappa
for some
s\geq 3
then
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U\rangle
is isomorphic to

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\oplus\kappa}
if
s\geq 3
;

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle\to K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle\oplus(x-y)K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{2}\rangle
,
(x,y)\mapsto(\{x,y\},(x-y)\{x,y\})
, is an isomorphism for
p\neq 2
.
In particular,
V^{(1)}_{R}\cong K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\oplus\kappa_{s}}
if
s\geq 3
.

As
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
and
K_{\mathrm{b}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

are injective for
s\neq 0,2
(it suffices to use this fact for
s=1
), the objects

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
and

K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{t}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
are injective for
t\geq 3

and
s\neq 0,2
. The
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set
\binom{\Psi}{t}\times\Psi
contains a
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-subset
isomorphic to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U
for a subgroup
U
of finite index in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x_{1},\dots,x_{t}\}}
,
so
P_{t}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}:=K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{t}\rangle
are injective for all
t\geq 3
. Similarly, the

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-set
\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\times\Psi
contains a
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-subset isomorphic
to
\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}
, so
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle

(which is
P_{2}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}
if
p=2
,
P_{2}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}\oplus{P_{2}^{\prime}}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}
if
p\neq 2
) is
injective. The remaining summands
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
of
K_{\mathrm{b}}
give the injective objects

P_{1,n}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}:=(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi\rangle
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. Clearly, the
P_{s}^{(K_{\mathrm{b}})}

for
s\geq 1
are direct sums of such objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

There remains to decompose
V:=V_{R}^{(1)}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]
. The object

K_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\frac{x}{x-y}\right]
is filtered with the successive quotients
(x-y)^{n}K_{\mathrm{c}}
with
n\geq 0
.
If
V_{R}^{(1)}\cong K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\oplus\kappa}
with
s\geq 3
then
V
is isomorphic
to a direct sum of copies of
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
. Similarly, if
V_{R}^{(1)}
is isomorphic
to (a direct summand of)
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
then
V
is isomorphic
to a direct sum of copies of (direct summands of)
K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Psi}\rangle
.
∎

5.5.
Categories
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)
for open subgroups
U\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

Lemma 5.21
.

Let
K
be a field endowed
with a smooth action of a permutation group
G\times H
, where
H
is a finite group. Assume
that any simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
is isomorphic to
K
, and
H
acts faithfully on
K^{G}
.
Then any simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(G)
is isomorphic to
K^{H}
.

Proof.

One has
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(G)}(K^{H},K)=K^{G}
,
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(G)}(K^{H})=(K^{H})^{G}=(K^{G})^{H}
.
When considered as an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K^{H}}(G)
,
K
is isomorphic to a direct sum of copies of
K

if and only if
[K^{G}:(K^{G})^{H}]=[K:K^{H}]=\#H
, i.e. the
H
-action on
K^{G}
is faithful. ∎

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
U=H\times\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}
be an open subgroup of

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, where
S\subset\Psi
is a finite non-empty subset and
H
is a subgroup of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{S}
.
Assume that at least one of the following options holds:
(i)

F\neq k
,
(ii)

\#S\geq 5
,
(iii)

H=1
,
(iv)

\#S=4
and
H
contains no element of type
(12)(34)
.
Then the forgetful functor
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
induces a homeomorphism of the Gabriel spectra.

In particular, under this assumption, any object of finite length in the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)

is a direct sum of invertible objects if
\#S\geq 2;

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)=0
if
\#S\geq 3;

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)\cong\mathbb{Z}
if
\#S\in\{1,2\}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.2
,
H^{0}(H,-):\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})

is an equivalence of categories. Any object
W
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
can be embedded into
the object
W^{\prime}:=K_{\mathrm{d}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}W
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
, while
\dim_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}W=\dim_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}W^{\prime}
.
If
W
is simple it can be embedded into a simple quotient
W^{\prime\prime}
of
W^{\prime}
. We are going to show that
the
simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
are of dimension
\leq 23
,
and therefore, any object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
of finite length is finite-dimensional.

By Lemma
5.4
,
K_{\mathrm{d}}=F_{\Psi}(\xi_{u}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
, where

\xi_{w}:=\frac{(w-x)(y-z)}{(w-y)(x-z)}
, so the restriction of
K_{\mathrm{d}}

(1)

to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}

is of type
K_{\mathrm{c}}
, i.e. it is isomorphic to
F_{\Psi}(\frac{y-u}{y-z}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
, since

K_{\mathrm{d}}=F_{\Psi}(\frac{y^{\prime}-u^{\prime}}{y^{\prime}-z^{\prime}}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
,
where
v^{\prime}=(v-x)^{-1}
for all
v\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x\}
,

(2)

to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
is isomorphic to
F_{\Psi}(u/z~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
,
since
K_{\mathrm{d}}=F_{\Psi}(\frac{u^{\prime\prime}}{z^{\prime\prime}}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
,
where
v^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{x-v}{v-y}
for all
v\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y\}
,

(xy)\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
acts by
v^{\prime\prime}\mapsto(v^{\prime\prime})^{-1}
;

(3)

to any subgroup of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y,z\}}
is isomorphic
to
F_{\Psi}(\Psi\smallsetminus\{x,y,z\})
.

In each of these cases, the simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})
are invertible:

(1)

\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes n}:=((x-y)^{-1}-(x-z)^{-1})^{n}K_{\mathrm{d}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
(if
S=\{x\}
, doesn’t depend on
y,z
);

(2)

\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes n}\cong\left(\frac{y-z}{z-x}\right)^{n}K_{\mathrm{d}}\subset K_{\mathrm{c}}

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
(if
S=\{x,y\}
, do not depend on
z
);

(3)

K_{\mathrm{d}}
(if
\#S\geq 3
).

One has

K_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}}=F_{S}(\mbox{cross-ratios of quadruples in }S)
, so the
H
-action on
K_{\mathrm{d}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}}

is faithful if and only if one of the conditions (i)–(iv) holds. By Lemma
5.21
,
this implies that under these assumptions any simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S})

is isomorphic to
K_{\mathrm{d}}^{H}
if
\#S\geq 3
. ∎

Proposition 5.23

(“Borel–Weil theorem” for
K_{\mathrm{d}}
)
.

Let
p
be the characteristic of
k
. For each integer
n\leq 0
, let
W_{n}
be the socle of the object

\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})\cong\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta}(\alpha-\beta)^{n}K_{\mathrm{d}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}

of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Then the objects
W_{n}

(i)
are simple,
(ii)
are pairwise non-isomorphic,
(iii)
are self-dual,

(iv)
present all isomorphism classes of simple finite-dimensional objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

If
p=0
then
W_{n}=\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
, so
\dim_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}W_{n}=1-2n
.

If
p>0
then
\dim_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}W_{n}=\prod_{t\geq 0}(c_{t}+1)
, where
-2n=\sum_{t\geq 0}c_{t}p^{t}
and
c_{t}\in\{0,1,\dots,p-1\}
.
In particular,
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})

is simple if and only if
n=1/2-(a-1/2)p^{m}
for some integers
0<a\leq p/2
and
m\geq 0
.
The natural map
K_{\mathrm{d}}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}},\phi}W_{n}\xrightarrow{f\otimes\eta\mapsto f\eta^{p}}W_{pn}
,
where
\phi:K_{\mathrm{d}}\xrightarrow{f\mapsto f^{p}}K_{\mathrm{d}}
, is an isomorphism.

The infinite-dimensional
(
induced
)
object

\mathcal{J}_{m}:=K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}\rangle}\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes m}

of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is simple for any
m<0
. Here

\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes m}:=\frac{(y-z)^{m}}{(x-y)^{m}(x-z)^{m}}K_{\mathrm{d}}\subset K_{\mathrm{b}}
for any pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
.

The object
\mathcal{J}_{0}^{\circ}:=\ker[K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\Psi\rangle\xrightarrow{\sum_{\alpha\in\Psi}a_{\alpha}[\alpha]\mapsto\sum_{\alpha\in\Psi}a_{\alpha}}K_{\mathrm{d}}]

is simple if and only if
F=k
.

Any other infinite-dimensional simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
can be embedded either into
\mathcal{J}_{m}

for
m>0
or into

K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle U\rangle}V
for a simple object
V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)

with
\dim_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}V\leq 18
, where
U
is an open subgroup of finite index in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,S}
for a subset

S\subset\Psi
with
\#S\in\{3,4\}
.

Proof.

Fix some integer
n\leq 0
and pairwise distinct
x,y,z\in\Psi
, and set
T:=\frac{y-z}{z-x}\in K_{\mathrm{c}}
.
For any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
, the element
T^{\sigma}=\frac{(y-z^{\sigma})(z-x)}{(y-z)(z^{\sigma}-x)}T

is a
K_{\mathrm{d}}^{\times}
-multiple of
T
, so
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})=\bigoplus_{i=n}^{-n}V_{i}
,
where
V_{i}:=K_{\mathrm{d}}\cdot T^{i}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}T}{T}\right)^{n}
, is a canonical splitting into a direct sum
of pairwise non-isomorphic
K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}\rangle
-modules, and the isomorphism class of
V_{i}

is independent of
n
. Then any
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant subspace of
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})

coincides with
\bigoplus_{i\in S}V_{i}
for a subset
S\subseteq\{n,n+1,\dots,-n\}
.

As
T^{\sigma}=\frac{\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{z^{\sigma}}}{\xi_{z^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}\frac{T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}{T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}}}
for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,
one has

(T^{i-n}(\mathrm{d}T)^{n})^{\sigma}=(\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}})^{n}\left(\frac{\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{z^{\sigma}}}{\xi_{z^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}}}\right)^{i}(T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}})^{i-n}(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})^{-i-n}(\mathrm{d}T)^{n}

for any
i
. Assuming further
that
x,y\notin\{x^{\sigma},y^{\sigma}\}
, this shows that the minimal
S
containing
i
contains

s
if and only if
\binom{i-n}{j}\binom{-i-n}{s-n-j}\neq 0
in
k
for some
j
. In particular, (i)

S
contains
n
, i.e. the socle of
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
, denoted
W_{n}
,
contains
V_{n}
; (ii)
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
is simple if
p=0
.

Let
p>0
. By Lucas’s theorem, the non-vanishing of
\binom{i-n}{j}\binom{-i-n}{s-n-j}
is equivalent to

i-n\succeq_{p}j
and
-i-n\succeq_{p}s-n-j
. For
i=n
we see that the element
(\mathrm{d}T)^{n}
spans

\bigoplus_{0\leq s\preceq_{p}-2n}V_{s+n}
. The latter module coincides with the whole

\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
if and only if
-2n\succeq_{p}s
for all
0<s<-2n
, which means that

-2n=ap^{m}-1
for some integer
0<a<p
and
m\geq 0
. In particular,
p\neq 2
if
m>0
and
a
is odd. On
the other hand, for any
i-n=\sum_{t\geq 0}i_{t}p^{t}\preceq_{p}-2n
and
s-n=\sum_{t\geq 0}s_{t}p^{t}\preceq_{p}-2n
,
where
i_{t},s_{t}\in\{0,1,\dots,p-1\}
, set
j=\sum_{t\geq 0}\min(i_{t},s_{t})p^{t}
. Then

i-n\succeq_{p}j
and
-i-n\succeq_{p}s-n-j
, so
p\nmid\binom{i-n}{j}\binom{-i-n}{s-n-j}
, i.e., the span
of
V_{i}
contains
V_{s}
, and thus,
\bigoplus_{0\leq s\preceq_{p}-2n}V_{s+n}
is simple and coincides with
the socle
W_{n}
of
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
.

By Remark
1.6
(
•
‣
3
), any simple object
W
of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
can be embedded into a simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, i.e. (by
Proposition
5.19
) into
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
for some

n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

For each integer
n
and
N\geq 0
, denote by
\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast N)
the subsheaf of the constant
sheaf
\omega_{\mathbb{Y},\eta}^{n}
, whose sections have poles of order
\leq N
at all points of
\mathbb{Y}
.
Then
W
is actually embedded into
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast N))
for some
N\geq 0
.
Take the minimal possible
N
. Obviously, if
N>0
then
W
is infinite-dimensional.

If
N=0
then
W
is embedded into
\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n})
, so
n\leq 0
and such
W
is
identified with
W_{n}
. The restriction of
W
to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
is self-dual, and its isomorphism
class determines
n
as
\min\{i~|~V_{i}\subset W\}
. This means that
W
is self-dual. Explicitly,

\sum_{s=n}^{-n}\binom{-2n}{s-n}(-T)^{s}\left(\frac{dT}{T}\right)^{n}\otimes T^{-s}\left(\frac{dT}{T}\right)^{n}\in(W_{n}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}W_{n})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
.

If
N>0
then
W
is also embedded into

\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast N))/\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast(N-1)))
.
The sheaf
\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}([N]):=\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast N)/\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast(N-1))

is a direct sum of skyscrapers with finite-dimensional stalks (non-canonically isomorphic to the
residue fields), so

\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast N))/\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}(\ast(N-1)))

embeds into

\Gamma(\mathbb{Y},\omega_{\mathbb{Y}}^{n}([N]))=\bigoplus_{O}V_{O}
, where
O
runs over the set of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbits
O
on
\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}
. Here

V_{O}:=\bigoplus_{v\in O}V_{v}
is the
K_{\mathrm{d}}
-semilinear representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
induced by a
finite-dimensional
K_{\mathrm{d}}
-semilinear representation
V_{v}
of the stabilizer
U
in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
of some
v\in O
.
Fix an orbit
O
such that the projection to
V_{O}
does not annihilate the image of
W
, thus assuming
that
W\subseteq K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle U\rangle}V_{v}
. Let
V^{\prime}
be a maximal

K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle U\rangle
-submodule of
V_{v}
such that
K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle U\rangle}V^{\prime}

does not contain
W
. Then
W
embeds into
K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle U\rangle}V

for a (unique) simple submodule
V
of
V_{v}/V^{\prime}
.

By Proposition
5.22
, the simple objects of the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(U)
are trivial in the cases:
1)
\#S\geq 5
, 2)
\#S\in\{3,4\}
and at least one of the following options holds: (i)
F\neq k
, (ii)
H=1
,
(iii)
\#S=4
and
\#(H\cap\mathfrak{A}_{4})\in\{1,3\}
. By Lemma
3.9
, if either
F\neq k

or
\#S\geq 5
then
K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U\rangle
(e.g.
\mathcal{J}_{0}
) embeds into a product of copies of

K_{\mathrm{d}}
, so there are no simple subobjects in
K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/U\rangle
.

By Lemma
5.14
, the stabilizer of any
v\in\mathrm{Val}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}|K_{\mathrm{d}}}\smallsetminus\Theta

is
H\times\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|S}
for a finite subset
S\subset\Psi
of order
\geq 3
and a subgroup
H\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{S}
.
Thus, there remain the cases
1)
S=\{x\}
; 2)
F=k
,
H\neq 1
,
\#S\in\{3,4\}
, and
2|\#(H\cap\mathfrak{A}_{S})
if
\#S=4
.

Any simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes m}
for
some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Lemma
4.8
, any non-zero submodule of
\mathcal{J}_{m}

is of finite codimension. Thus, if
\mathcal{J}_{m}
is not simple then it admits a non-zero morphism
to
W_{n}
for some
n\leq 0
. One has
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle}(\mathcal{J}_{m},W_{n})=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{d}}\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}\rangle}(\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes m},W_{n})=\left(\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\otimes(-m)}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{d}}}W_{n}\right)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}}=\left(\frac{(x-y)^{m}(x-z)^{m}}{(y-z)^{m}}W_{n}\right)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}}=\left(\bigoplus_{0\leq s\preceq_{p}-2n}\left(\frac{y-x}{T}\right)^{m}T^{s}(dT)^{n}K_{\mathrm{d}}\right)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}}
.
The summands in the latter direct sum are
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}
-invariant, and
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}

fixes a non-zero element
\eta
only for
s=m-n
, i.e.
\eta=a(x-y)^{m}\left(\frac{dT}{T}\right)^{n}

for some
a\in F_{\{x,y\}}^{\times}
. For any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}
,

\eta^{\sigma}=a^{\sigma}(x-y^{\sigma})^{m}\left(\frac{dT^{\sigma}}{T^{\sigma}}\right)^{n}=\frac{a^{\sigma}}{a}\xi_{y^{\sigma}}^{m}(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})^{-m}\xi_{y^{\sigma}}^{n}(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})^{-n}\eta
,
so
\eta
is fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x\}}
if and only if
m=-n
and
a\in F_{\{x\}}^{\times}
.
This means that
\mathcal{J}_{m}
is simple for any
m<0
.

Similarly, any morphism
\mathcal{J}_{0}^{\circ}\to W_{n}
sends
[x]-[y]
to an element of
W_{n}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\{x,y\}}}
,
i.e. to
a\left(\frac{dT}{T}\right)^{n}
for some
a\in F_{\{x,y\}}^{\times}
, so
[x^{\sigma}]-[y^{\sigma}]\mapsto a^{\sigma}\left(\frac{dT^{\sigma}}{T^{\sigma}}\right)^{n}=a^{\sigma}\left(\frac{(\xi_{y^{\sigma}}-\xi_{x^{\sigma}})dT}{(T+\xi_{x^{\sigma}})(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})}\right)^{n}
.
When
x^{\sigma}=y
we get
a\left(\frac{dT}{T}\right)^{n}+a^{\sigma}\left(-\frac{dT}{T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}}}\right)^{n}=a^{\tau}\left(\frac{\xi_{y^{\sigma}}dT}{T(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})}\right)^{n}
, where
x^{\tau}=x
and

y^{\tau}=y^{\sigma}
. Equivalently,
a+a^{\sigma}(-T)^{n}(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})^{-n}=a^{\tau}\xi_{y^{\sigma}}^{n}(T+\xi_{y^{\sigma}})^{-n}
, which is possible only when
n=0
. If
F=k
then
the condition
a+a^{\sigma}=a^{\tau}
implies
a=0
, and therefore,
\mathcal{J}_{0}^{\circ}
is simple.
∎

5.6.
(Finite)
G
-extensions and finite-dimensional representations

Lemma 5.24
.

Let
G
be a group,
\widetilde{K}
be a
G
-field, and
K\subseteq\widetilde{K}

be a
G
-invariant subfield. Set
k:=\widetilde{K}^{G}
. Let
V\neq 0
be a
K\langle G\rangle
-module of
a finite dimension
d
. Then
(i)

\dim_{k}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V,\widetilde{K})\leq d;

(ii)

V
is simple and
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V)=k
if
k\subseteq K
and there is
a
K\langle G\rangle
-module embedding
V^{\oplus d}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{K}
.
In particular, if
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are isomorphic invertible

K\langle G\rangle
-submodules of
\widetilde{K}
then
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
if
k\subseteq K
.

Proof.

(i) As it is well-known, the multiplication map
\widetilde{K}\otimes_{k}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V^{\vee})^{G}\to\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V^{\vee}

is injective, so
\dim_{k}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V,\widetilde{K})=\dim_{k}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V^{\vee})^{G}=\dim_{\widetilde{K}}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{k}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V^{\vee})^{G})\leq\dim_{\widetilde{K}}(\widetilde{K}\otimes_{K}V^{\vee})=\dim_{K}V=:d
.

(ii) For any submodule
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
, any embedding
V^{\oplus d}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{K}
restricts to
an embedding
{V^{\prime}}^{\oplus d}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{K}
. If
V^{\prime}
is simple then the existence of such
an embedding implies that
\dim_{D}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V^{\prime},\widetilde{K})\geq d
, where
D:=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V^{\prime})
,
so
e:=\dim_{k}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V^{\prime},\widetilde{K})\geq d\cdot\dim_{k}D
.
By (i),
d\geq\dim_{K}V^{\prime}\geq e
, and therefore,
d=\dim_{K}V^{\prime}=d\cdot\dim_{k}D
, i.e.
V^{\prime}=V
and

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{K\langle G\rangle}(V)=D=k
. ∎

Example 5.25
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p
,
L:=k(u/v~|~u,v\in\Psi)
,

a\in k^{\times}
. Fix some distinct
x,y\in\Psi
. For each integer
n\geq 2
,
set
L_{n}:=L(x^{n})
. Let
K:=L^{\langle\iota\rangle}
(resp.,
K_{n}:=L_{n}^{\langle\iota\rangle}
) be the
(
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant) fixed subfield of the
k
-field involution
\iota:k(\Psi)\to k(\Psi)
,
u\mapsto a/u

(
u\in\Psi
), restricted to
L
(resp., to
L_{n}
). As before,
\mathrm{Spec}_{?}
is
the Gabriel spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{?}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
. Then

•

the isomorphism classes of 2-dimensional simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

(resp., of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
) are
x^{j}L
for all
j\geq 1
(resp., are
xL_{n},\dots,x^{[(n-1)/2]}L_{n}
,
so there are no such objects for
n=2
); they are pairwise distinct and injective;

•

the remaining simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(resp., of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
) are invertible;
they are all injective if
p\neq 2
;

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})=0
if
p=2
;
E(K)\cong L
,

E(K\langle\Psi\rangle)\cong L\langle\Psi\rangle
and
L/K\xrightarrow[\mathrm{tr}_{L|K}]{\sim}K
if
p=2
;

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\cong\{\pm 1\}
is generated by
(x/y-y/x)K
if
p\neq 2
; the only
non-trivial
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
-orbit on
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

is
\{[K\langle\Psi\rangle],[(x/y-y/x)K\otimes_{K}K\langle\Psi\rangle]\}
if
p\neq 2
;

•

\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\cong\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\oplus\mathbb{Z}/(2,n)
;
for any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, one has
E(\mathcal{L})/\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{L}
if
p=2
;

•

non-trivial
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
-orbits on

\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{n}}\smallsetminus\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
occur only if
n
is even,
in which case they are
\{[x^{i}L_{n}],[x^{n/2-i}L_{n}]\}
for
1\leq i<n/4
, while the subgroup

\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
acts trivially;

•

the closed points of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
and of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{n}}
are presented by
injective hulls of the simple objects and, in the case of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
, by
E(K\langle\Psi\rangle)

and, if
p\neq 2
, by
(x/y-y/x)K\otimes_{K}K\langle\Psi\rangle
;

•

the non-closed points of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
(resp., of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K_{n}}
)
are presented by
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for all integer
s>1
(resp.,
by
K_{n}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for all integer
s>0
).

Proof.

As
\iota
commutes with the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action,
K_{n}
is
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant. The involution
\iota
induces
isomorphisms
\iota_{i}:x^{i}L\xrightarrow{\sim}x^{-i}L
. The decomposition
k(\Psi)=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}x^{i}L_{n}

into a direct sum of subobjects shows that
x^{i}L_{n}
are injective, while Lemma
5.24
(with

\widetilde{K}=k(\Psi)
) implies that
x^{i}L_{n}
is irreducible if
i\not\equiv-i\pmod{n}
, and
x^{i}L_{n}
’s are
pairwise non-isomorphic for
1\leq i<n/2
. If
i=n-i
then the
K_{n}
-eigenspaces

\mathcal{L}_{\pm}:=(x^{n/2}\pm a^{n/2}x^{-n/2})K_{n}
of
\iota_{i}
are
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant. If
p\neq 2

then
L
and
L_{n}=L\otimes_{K}K_{n}
split into the sums of eigenspaces of
\iota
as
L=K\oplus(x/y-y/x)K

and
L_{n}=K_{n}\oplus(x^{n}-a^{n}x^{-n})K_{n}
. If
p=2
then, by Lemma
5.24
(with
\widetilde{K}=L_{n}
),
the sequence
0\to K\to L\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}_{L|K}}K\to 0
does not split, even
under the functor
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\xrightarrow{(-)\otimes_{K}K_{n}}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
; clearly,

E(\mathcal{L}_{+})\cong\mathcal{L}_{+}\otimes_{K_{n}}E(K_{n})\cong x^{n/2}L_{n}
.

The multiplication induces an isomorphism
(x^{n}-a^{n}x^{-n})K_{n}\otimes_{K_{n}}x^{i}L_{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}x^{i}L_{n}
and an
isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{+}\otimes_{K_{n}}x^{n/4}L_{n}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\iota_{3n/4}}x^{n/4}L_{n}
if
4|n
.

The indecomposable injectives in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(resp., in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{n}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
) are direct summands
of the indecomposable injectives in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(resp., in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
), i.e. direct
summands of
x^{i}L
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
,
L\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for all
s>0
(resp., of

k(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for all
s\geq 0
), so their list comes from the isomorphism of
Lemma
3.21
:
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\oplus K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}L\otimes_{K}K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

for
s\geq 2
(resp.,

K_{n}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle^{\oplus 2n}\xrightarrow{\sim}k(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle

for
s\geq 1
). ∎

5.6.1.
Torsion of the Picard group, and its trivialization

Lemma 5.26
.

Let
G
be a permutation group,
\widetilde{K}
be a smooth

G
-field, and
H
be a profinite automorphism group of the
G
-field
\widetilde{K}
. Set

k:=\widetilde{K}^{G}
. Then there is a natural exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{Pic}_{k}(H)\to\mathrm{Pic}_{\widetilde{K}^{H}}(G)\to\mathrm{Pic}_{\widetilde{K}}(G)^{H}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.2
,
\mathrm{Pic}_{\widetilde{K}}(G\times H)=\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
,
so the above exact sequence is the inflation-restriction sequence

0\to H^{1}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(H,k^{\times})\to H^{1}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(G\times H,\widetilde{K}^{\times})\to H^{1}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(G,\widetilde{K}^{\times})^{H}
. ∎

For an integer
n>0
, denote by
{}_{n}\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
the
n
-torsion subgroup in
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
.

Proposition 5.27
.

Let
G
be a permutation group,
K
be a smooth
G
-field and

n>0
be an integer. Set
k:=K^{G}
and
\mu_{n}:=\{z\in K^{\times}~|~z^{n}=1\}
. Then there is a natural exact sequence

H^{1}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(G,\mu_{n})\to{}_{n}\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)\xrightarrow{\beta}(K^{\times}/K^{\times n})^{G}/k^{\times}\xrightarrow{\xi}H^{2}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(G,\mu_{n}).

Assume that
G
admits no open subgroups of finite index. Then any invertible object
\mathcal{L}

of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
of order
n
is contained in the
G
-field
K(a^{1/n})

for some
a\in(K^{\times}/K^{\times n})^{G}
.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{L}
is an object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
with
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{n}}\cong K
,
choose non-zero
\pi\in\mathcal{L}
and
\lambda\in(\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{n}})^{G}
, and set

\beta([\mathcal{L}]):=[\pi^{\otimes n}/\lambda]
. In terms of 1-cocycles,
\beta((f_{\sigma}))=[g]
,
where
g^{\sigma}/g=f_{\sigma}^{n}
for all
\sigma\in G
.

Define
\xi
by
a\mapsto[(b_{\sigma}b_{\tau}^{\sigma}b_{\sigma\tau}^{-1})]
, where
\sigma a/a=b_{\sigma}^{n}

for some 1-cochain
(b_{\sigma})
with values in
K^{\times}
. Obviously,
\xi\beta=0
.
If
\xi a=0
then
(b_{\sigma}\zeta_{\sigma})
is a 1-cocycle for some 1-cochain
(\zeta_{\sigma})
with values in

\mu_{n}
, so
(b_{\sigma}\zeta_{\sigma})
defines an element of
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(G)
. Obviously, it is of order
n
.

If

\beta((f_{\sigma}))=0
then there exists
g\in K^{\times}
such that
(g^{n})^{\sigma}/g^{n}=f_{\sigma}^{n}
for all

\sigma\in G
, and therefore,
(gf_{\sigma}/g^{\sigma})
is a 1-cocycle with values in
\mu_{n}
.

Let
R=R_{\mathcal{L},\lambda}
be the quotient of the symmetric
K
-algebra of
\mathcal{L}
by the ideal
generated by
\lambda-1
. The natural map
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{i}}\to R

is a
K\langle G\rangle
-module isomorphism, so the
K
-algebra
R
is finite-dimensional
(
\dim_{K}R=n
), and thus, it has only finitely many maximal ideals.

If
G
admits no open subgroups of finite index then the
G
-action on the set of maximal ideals is trivial,
i.e. any maximal ideal is
G
-invariant. As
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{i}}
are pairwise non-isomorphic for

0\leq i<n
, any
G
-invariant
K
-vector subspace in
R
is a direct sum of several
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{i}}
.
As any non-empty direct sum of
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes_{K}^{i}}
’s contains invertible elements, we conclude that 0 is
the only maximal ideal in
R
, i.e.
R
is a field. Fix a non-zero
\pi\in\mathcal{L}
and set

a:=\pi^{n}/\lambda\in K^{\times}
, so
R\cong K[\pi]/(\pi^{n}-a)
. As
\pi^{\sigma}/\pi\in K^{\times}
for any

\sigma\in G
, one has
(\pi^{\sigma}/\pi)^{n}=a^{\sigma}/a\in K^{\times n}
, so
a
is fixed by
G
modulo

K^{\times n}
. ∎

Proposition
5.27
means that torsion in the Picard group is a particular case of finite smooth

G
-field extensions
L|K
with
L^{G}=K^{G}
.

Example 5.28
.

The following
G
-fields
K
admit no non-trivial finite smooth

G
-field extension
L|K
with
L^{G}=K^{G}
, so the Picard group is torsion free:
(i) any
K
such that it is a cogenerator of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, e.g.
K_{\mathrm{a}}
with
\Xi=0
,
(ii) any
K
of characteristic 0 such that all simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
are isomorphic,
(iii)
K_{\mathrm{a}}
with
\Gamma=0
, (iv)
F_{\Psi}(x-y~|~x,y\in\Psi)
.

Proof.

(i) If
L
is a finite smooth
G
-extension of
K
then
L

is a trivial
K
-semilinear representation of
G
, so
1=\dim_{K^{G}}L^{G}=[L:K]
.

(ii) If
L
is a non-trivial finite smooth
G
-extension of
K
and
[L:K]
is invertible in
K
then

[L:K]^{-1}\mathrm{tr}_{L|K}
splits the inclusion
K\hookrightarrow L
, so the socle of
L
contains
a subobject isomorphic to
K\oplus K
, contradicting
\dim_{K^{G}}L^{G}=1
.

(iii) If
L
is a finite smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-extension of
K_{\mathrm{a}}
then
L\cong\bigoplus x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
is an integral
ring with a
K_{\mathrm{a}}
-semilinear action of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. As
L\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}L\xrightarrow{\times}L
is injective
on each
K_{\mathrm{a}}\cdot x^{\lambda}\otimes_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}K_{\mathrm{a}}\cdot x^{\lambda}\cong K_{\mathrm{a}}\cdot x^{2\lambda}
, taking
\lambda
with
maximal absolute value of
i
-th coordinate, we see that
L\cong K_{\mathrm{a}}^{[L:K_{\mathrm{a}}]}
. By the argument of (i),
L=K_{\mathrm{a}}
.

(iv) Let
K=F_{\Psi}(x-y~|~x,y\in\Psi)
. By Theorem
1.4
(
•
‣
4
),
K[x]
is injective in

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
, so if
L
is a finite smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-extension of
K
then the inclusion
K\hookrightarrow K[x]

extends to a morphism
\varphi:L\to K[x]
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. As
L^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=k
,
K
is the only simple object
and
\ker\varphi
is of finite length, the map
\varphi
is injective. We may thus assume that

L\subseteq K[x]^{<d}
for a minimal
d\geq 1
. As
K[x]
is injective, any commutative
K
-algebra structure

L\otimes_{K}L\to L\subset K[x]
extends to a morphism
\xi:K[x]\otimes_{K}K[x]^{<d}\to K[x]

in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. One has
K[x]\otimes_{K}K[x]^{<d}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{d-1}(K[x]\otimes_{K}K)(1\otimes x-x\otimes 1)^{i}\xrightarrow{\sim}K[x]^{\oplus d}
,

f\otimes g=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}(fD^{(i)}g\otimes 1)(1\otimes x-x\otimes 1)^{i}\mapsto(fg,fD^{(1)}g,fD^{(2)}g,\dots,fD^{(d-1)}g)
,
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
. By Proposition
5.17
,

\xi(f\otimes g)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}a_{ij}D^{(i)}f\cdot D^{(j)}g

for some
a_{ij}\in k
. As
\xi(1\otimes 1)\neq 0
, the element
a_{00}
is non-zero, and therefore,

\deg\xi(f\otimes g)=\deg f+\deg g
. If
\deg f
is maximal on
L
then
\deg\xi(f\otimes f)=2\deg f\leq\deg f
,
i.e.
\deg f=0
, and thus,
L=K
. ∎

Consider a finite abelian group
A
as a trivial
\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi}
-module. By

[
5
, Theorem 3.5 and Corollary 3.7]
,
H^{>0}_{\text{{\rm cont}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi},A)=0
.
For each integer
n>0
, the sequences
0\to\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\times n}\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\to 0

and
0\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\xrightarrow{\times n}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}\to 0

show that
H^{>0}_{\text{cont}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Z})
and

H^{>0}_{\text{cont}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
are uniquely
n
-divisible, i.e.,
they are
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces. Obviously,
H^{1}_{\text{cont}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Z})=H^{1}_{\text{cont}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=0
.

5.6.2.
Representations over fixed subfields of
k(\Psi)

The most straightforward source of finite-dimensional semilinear representations
comes from the following

Proposition 5.29
.

Let
G
be a permutation group,
\widetilde{K}
be a
smooth
G
-field,
H
be a finite group of
G
-field automorphisms of
\widetilde{K}
,
K:=\widetilde{K}^{H}
, and

k\subseteq K^{G}
be a subfield. Then
V_{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k[H]}(\rho,\widetilde{K})
is a simple object of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
for any absolutely irreducible
\rho\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(H);

\dim_{K}V_{\rho}=\dim_{k}\rho
.

If
\#H
is invertible in
K
then
V_{\rho}
and

V_{\rho^{\prime}}
are dual in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
if and only if
\rho^{\prime}\cong\rho^{\vee}
.

Proof.

For each subfield
k\subseteq K
and each
k
-linear representation
\rho
of
H
,
set
V_{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k[H]}(\rho,\widetilde{K})
. This is a
D_{\rho}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{k}K
-module,
where
D_{\rho}:=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k[H]}(\rho)
. Assume from now on that
\dim_{k}\rho<\infty
. Then

V_{\rho}=(\rho^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\widetilde{K})^{H}
.
Moreover, by
[
22
, Satz 1]
,

\dim_{K}V_{\rho}=\dim_{K}(\rho^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\widetilde{K})^{H}=\dim_{K}(\widetilde{K}^{\oplus\dim_{k}\rho^{\vee}})^{H}=\dim_{K}(K^{\oplus\dim_{k}\rho^{\vee}})=\dim_{k}\rho^{\vee}=\dim_{k}\rho.

As the evaluation map
\rho\otimes_{k}\rho^{\vee}\to k
factors through
\rho\otimes_{D_{\rho}}\rho^{\vee}\to k
,
the evaluation map
\rho\otimes_{k}V_{\rho}\to\widetilde{K}
factors through
\rho\otimes_{D_{\rho}}V_{\rho}\to\widetilde{K}
.
Let us show that the latter map is injective if
K\otimes_{k}\rho
is irreducible.

By the normal basis theorem, there is an isomorphism of
K[H]
-modules
K[H]\xrightarrow{\sim}\widetilde{K}
.
Replacing
\widetilde{K}
by
K[H]
,
we get an isomorphism
K\otimes_{k}\rho^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{\rho}\subset\rho^{\vee}\otimes_{k}K[H]
,

\lambda\mapsto\widehat{\lambda}:=\sum_{h\in H}\lambda^{h}[h]
, so the evaluation map becomes

\xi=\sum_{i=1}^{r}v_{i}\otimes\widehat{\lambda}_{i}\mapsto\sum_{h\in H}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{h}(v_{i})[h]
,
which means that
\xi
is its kernel only if
\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{h}(v_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}(hv_{i})=0

for all
h\in H
. We may assume that
v_{i}\in K\otimes_{k}\rho
are linearly independent over

K\otimes_{k}D_{\rho}
and
\lambda_{1}\neq 0
. By the Jacobson density theorem, the natural ring
homomorphism
k[H]\to\mathrm{End}_{D_{\rho}}(\rho)
is surjective, so there exist
a_{h}\in K

such that
\sum_{h\in H}a_{h}[h]
annihilates
v_{2},\dots,v_{r}
and sends
v_{1}
to
v
such that
\lambda_{1}(v)\neq 0
.
Then
\sum_{h\in H}a_{h}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}(hv_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}((\sum_{h\in H}a_{h}[h])v_{i})=\lambda_{1}(v)
,
giving contradiction.

If
k\subseteq K^{G}
then
G
acts on
V_{\rho}
, so
V_{\rho}
becomes a smooth

D_{\rho}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes_{k}K\langle G\rangle
-module.
By Lemma
5.24
, this implies that if

\rho
is absolutely irreducible then
V_{\rho}
is a simple object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.

Assume that the
characteristic of
K
does not divide

\#H
, so any representation of
H
over
K

is semisimple. Let
\Pi
be the set of isomorphism classes of
k
-linear irreducible
representations of
H
. Then the
H
-bimodule decomposition

k[H]=\bigoplus_{\bar{\rho}\in\Pi}\rho\otimes_{D_{\rho}}\rho^{\vee}
corresponds to a decomposition

\widetilde{K}=\bigoplus_{\bar{\rho}\in\Pi}W_{\rho}
is into a direct sum
of objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G\times H)
, where
W_{\rho}:=\rho\otimes_{D_{\rho}}V_{\rho}
.
As
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K[H]}(W_{\rho}\otimes_{K}W_{\rho^{\prime}},K)\neq 0
if and only if
\rho^{\prime}\cong\rho^{\vee}
(i.e.
\rho
and

\rho^{\prime}
are dual),
W_{\rho}
and
W_{\rho^{\prime}}
are dual if and only if
\rho^{\prime}\cong\rho^{\vee}
.
In particular,
V_{\rho}
and
V_{\rho^{\prime}}
are dual in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
if and only if
\rho^{\prime}\cong\rho^{\vee}
.
∎

In the following examples,
\widetilde{K}=k(\Psi)
,
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
and
H
is a finite subgroup of
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(k)

acting ‘diagonally’ on
k(\Psi)
. If
\#H
is invertible in
k
then
such
H
can only be cyclic, dihedral,
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
,
\mathfrak{S}_{4}

or
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
. Except for the dihedral groups, there is only one conjugacy class for each of these
groups (with no exceptions if
k^{\times}=k^{\times 2}
), see
[
1
, Theorem 4.2]
. If
k

contains a finite subfield
\mathbb{F}_{q}
then
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is one more option for
H
,
while a complete list can be found in
[
6
]
.

Remark 5.30
.

The case of dihedral groups is covered by Example
5.25
. Namely, if
k

contains a primitive
n
-th root of unity
\zeta
then each such group is conjugated to

H_{a}=\langle\zeta,\iota_{a}\rangle\cong\mu_{n}\rtimes\mu_{2}
, where
\zeta
acts as
z\mapsto\zeta z
, and

\iota_{a}:z\mapsto a/z
for some
a\in k^{\times}
, so
k(\Psi)^{\langle\zeta\rangle}=L
and
K=k(\Psi)^{H_{a}}
.

Example 5.31
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p\neq 2,3
, and

L:=k(x^{2}+\frac{1}{x^{2}},\frac{u}{x}+\frac{x}{u},\frac{x^{2}-x^{-2}}{u/x-x/u}~|~u\in\Psi\smallsetminus\{x\})

for some
x\in\Psi
. Then (i) the automorphism
\theta:u\mapsto\frac{u+\sqrt{-1}}{u-\sqrt{-1}}
of
the field
k(\sqrt{-1})(\Psi)
over
k(\sqrt{-1})
preserves
L
,
(ii) the subfield
K=K_{A4}
of
L
fixed by
\theta
is
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant,
(iii)

•

(x-\frac{1}{x})L
presents the unique isomorphism class of simple 3-dimensional objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
,

•

if
\sqrt{-3}\in k
then the remaining simple objects are invertible and
\#\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})=3
,

•

if
\sqrt{-3}\notin k
then
\frac{x^{12}+15x^{8}+15x^{4}+1}{x^{2}(x^{4}-1)^{2}}K\oplus\frac{x^{8}+14x^{4}+1}{(x^{4}-1)^{2}}K\subset L
presents the unique remaining isomorphism class of non-invertible
simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
and
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})=0
.

Proof.

One has
L(x^{2})=k(x^{2},u/x~|~u\in\Psi)
and
[L(x^{2}):L]=2
(since
L
is fixed by

\iota:k(\Psi)\to k(\Psi)
,
u\mapsto 1/u
for all
u\in\Psi
, while
x^{2}
is not),

[L(x):L(x^{2})]=2
(since
L(x^{2})
is fixed by
u\mapsto-u
, while
x
is not),
[L(x):L]=4
.
This means that
L=k(\Psi)^{\langle-z,\iota\rangle}
, and therefore,
L
contains
u^{2}+u^{-2}
,

u/v+v/u
,
(u^{2}-u^{-2})(u/v-v/u)
,
\frac{u\pm u^{-1}}{x\pm x^{-1}}
and
\frac{u^{2}-u^{-2}}{x^{2}-x^{-2}}

for all
u,v\in\Psi
. In particular, (i) the subfield
L\subset k(\Psi)
is
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant,
(ii)
k(\Psi)=L\oplus(x-x^{-1})L\oplus(x+x^{-1})L\oplus(x^{2}-x^{-2})L
is a decomposition into a sum of

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant
L
-vector subspaces, (iii)
\theta
preserves
L
even if
\sqrt{-1}\notin k

(since the embeddings
\sigma\theta
and
\theta\iota
of
k(\Psi)
into
k(\sqrt{-1})(\Psi)
coincide, where

\sigma
is the generator of
\mathrm{Gal}(L(\sqrt{-1})|L)
, while
\theta\iota
and
\theta
coincide on
L
).

Assuming that
p\neq 3
, if
k
does not contain a third root of unity
\zeta\neq\zeta^{-1}

then we adjoin it, so
K(\zeta)
,

\left(\frac{(x^{2}-1)^{2}}{4x^{2}}-\frac{4\zeta x^{2}}{(x^{2}+1)^{2}}-\frac{(x^{2}+1)^{2}}{\zeta(x^{2}-1)^{2}}\right)K(\zeta)
,

\left(\frac{(x^{2}-1)^{2}}{4x^{2}}-\frac{4x^{2}}{\zeta(x^{2}+1)^{2}}-\frac{\zeta(x^{2}+1)^{2}}{(x^{2}-1)^{2}}\right)K(\zeta)

are the (
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-invariant) eigenspaces of
\theta
in the 3-dimensional
K(\zeta)
-vector space
L(\zeta)
.
By Lemma
5.24
, the isomorphisms
(x-x^{-1})L\xrightarrow[\widetilde{\phantom{--}}]{\sqrt{-1}\theta}(x+x^{-1})L\xrightarrow[\widetilde{\phantom{--}}]{\theta}(x^{2}-x^{-2})L
,

x-x^{-1}\mapsto-\frac{4}{x+x^{-1}}\mapsto-2\frac{x+x^{-1}}{x-x^{-1}}
, imply that
(x-x^{-1})L
,

(x+x^{-1})L
,
(x^{2}-x^{-2})L
are simple.

[Alternatively. If
(x-x^{-1})L(\zeta)
(resp.
(x+x^{-1})L(\zeta)
, resp.
(x^{2}-x^{-2})L(\zeta)
)
is reducible then either itself or its dual contains an invertible subobject. But this is impossible,
since by Lemma
5.26
,
\#\mathrm{Pic}_{K(\zeta)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})=3
,
and the invertible subobjects in
k(\zeta)(\Psi)
are multiplicity-free.] ∎

Example 5.32
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p
,
H
be a finite subgroup of
the group (isomorphic to
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}k
) of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field automorphisms of
k(\Psi)|k
, and
K:=k(\Psi)^{H}
.

(1)

Assume that
p
does not divide
\#H
, and the irreducible representations
of
H
over
k
are absolutely irreducible. It follows from Proposition
5.29
and
the classification of irreducible representations of
\mathfrak{S}_{4}
and
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
that

•

if
p\neq 2,3
,
k
contains
\sqrt{-1}
and
H=H_{S4}\supset H_{A4}
is
isomorphic to
\mathfrak{S}_{4}
then there are 5 isomorphism classes of simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
:
one of dimension 2 (fixed by
\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\cong\{\pm 1\}
), and two free

\mathrm{Pic}_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
-orbits of classes of dimensions 1 and 3;

•

if
p\neq 2,3,5
,
k
contains
\sqrt{-1}
and
\sqrt{5}
, and

H=H_{A5}\supset H_{A4}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
then there are 5 isomorphism classes of
simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
:
K
, unique ones of dimensions 4 and 5, and two classes of dimension 3.

(2)

Let
k
contain a finite subfield
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and
H
be isomorphic
to
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}\mathbb{F}_{q}
. It is easy to see (e.g.
[
2
, Lemma 3.4]
) that the

k
-representation
\rho:=k[\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})]^{\circ}
of
H
is absolutely irreducible.
By Proposition
5.29
,
\dim_{K}V_{\rho}=q
and
V_{\rho}
is irreducible.

5.7.
Isotrivial finitely generated
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-extensions of
F_{\Psi}

Proposition 5.33
.

Let
F|k
be a field extension with
k

algebraically closed in
F
,
Y
be a geometrically irreducible
k
-variety, and

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\xrightarrow{\tau\mapsto\tau^{\iota}}\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{field}}(K|k)

be a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action on the field
K:=F_{\Psi}(Y)
extending the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action
on
F_{\Psi}
. Then
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\cong k(Y^{\prime})
for a geometrically
irreducible
k
-variety
Y^{\prime}
such that
Y_{k^{\prime}}
and
Y^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
are birational for a finite extension
k^{\prime}|k
.

Proof.

As the pointwise stabilizer of
k(Y)
is open, it contains
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
for a finite subset

J\subset\Psi
. For each
\tau\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
,
\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))
is fixed by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\tau(J)}
,
so
\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))\subseteq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|\tau(J)}}\subseteq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J\cup\tau(J)}}=F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)
. If
\eta\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
is an involution such that
J\cap\eta(J)=\varnothing
then

\eta
and
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
generate
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. Let
F^{\prime}\subseteq F
be a finitely generated
field extension of
k
such that
\eta^{\iota}(k(Y))\subseteq F^{\prime}_{J\cup\eta(J)}(Y)
. Then

\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))\subseteq F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)\cap F^{\prime}_{\Psi}(Y)=F^{\prime}_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)
for any

\tau\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, so we may further assume that

F=F^{\prime}
. As
F_{J\cup\tau(J)}\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))\subseteq F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)
and

F_{J\cup\tau^{-1}(J)}(\tau^{-1})^{\iota}(k(Y))\subseteq F_{J\cup\tau^{-1}(J)}(Y)
(or equivalently,

F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)\subseteq F_{J\cup\tau(J)}\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))
),
we get
F_{J\cup\tau(J)}\tau^{\iota}(k(Y))=F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)
.

This gives rise to a field automorphism

\alpha_{\tau}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)|F_{J\cup\tau(J)})
extending the
F_{J\cup\tau(J)}
-algebra
homomorphism
F_{J\cup\tau(J)}\otimes_{k}k(Y)\xrightarrow{id\cdot\tau^{\iota}}F_{J\cup\tau(J)}(Y)
.

For each subset
I\subset\Psi
, the group
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(F_{I}(Y)|F_{I})
is naturally embedded into
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(K|F_{\Psi})
.
Denote by
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\xrightarrow{\tau\mapsto\tau_{Y}}\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits_{\mathrm{field}}(K|k(Y))
the embedding given
by the standard
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action on
F_{\Psi}
and the trivial
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action on
k(Y)
.
Clearly,
\sigma^{\iota}=\alpha_{\sigma}\sigma_{Y}
for any
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, and
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}

acts on
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(K|F_{\Psi})
by conjugation:
\sigma:\alpha\mapsto\alpha^{\sigma}:=\sigma_{Y}\alpha\sigma_{Y}^{-1}
for all
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
and
\alpha\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(K|F_{\Psi})
.

Then
\alpha_{\sigma\tau}(\sigma\tau)_{Y}=(\sigma\tau)^{\iota}=\sigma^{\iota}\tau^{\iota}=\alpha_{\sigma}\sigma_{Y}\alpha_{\tau}\tau_{Y}=\alpha_{\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}^{\sigma}(\sigma\tau)_{Y}

for all
\sigma,\tau\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, so
\alpha_{\sigma}\alpha_{\tau}^{\sigma}=\alpha_{\sigma\tau}
.

Fix some
\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
such that
|J\cup\sigma(J)\cup\sigma\eta(J)|=3|J|
. As the field

F_{J\cup\eta(J)}(Y)
is finitely generated over
k
and
k
is algebraically closed in
F_{J\cup\eta(J)}(Y)
,
there is a finite Galois extension
k^{\prime}|k
and a place

F_{J}\dashrightarrow k^{\prime}

such that the compositions of
\alpha_{\sigma}
and of
\alpha_{\sigma\eta}
with the
induced place
s:F_{J\cup\sigma(J)\cup\sigma\eta(J)}(Y)\dashrightarrow F_{\sigma(J)\cup\sigma\eta(J)}(Y)\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}

are well-defined. Then the cocycle condition implies
s(\alpha_{\sigma})\alpha_{\eta}^{\sigma}=s(\alpha_{\sigma\eta})
,
where
s(\alpha_{\xi})\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}F_{\xi(J)}(Y)|k^{\prime}F_{\xi(J)})
for both
\xi\in\{\sigma,\sigma\eta\}
. Equivalently,

\alpha_{\eta}=s_{\sigma}^{-1}s_{\sigma\eta}^{\eta}
, where

s_{\xi}:=s(\alpha_{\xi})^{\xi^{-1}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}F_{J}(Y)|k^{\prime}F_{J})
for both
\xi\in\{\sigma,\sigma\eta\}
.

Replacing the subfield
k^{\prime}(Y)
of the field
K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
by
s_{\sigma\eta}^{-1}(k^{\prime}(Y))
, we may assume
further that
\alpha_{\eta}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}F_{J}(Y)|k^{\prime}F_{J})
and still
\alpha_{\sigma}=1
for all

\sigma\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}
. The 1-cocycle relation
\alpha_{\eta}\alpha_{\eta}^{\eta}=1
implies that
