\alpha_{\eta}=(\alpha_{\eta}^{\eta})^{-1}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}F_{J}(Y)|k^{\prime}F_{J})\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}F_{\eta(J)}(Y)|k^{\prime}F_{\eta(J)})=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}(Y)|k^{\prime})
. Therefore, the map
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(k^{\prime}(Y)|k^{\prime})
,
\xi\mapsto\alpha_{\xi}
, is
a homomorphism with open kernel, which is trivial by the simplicity of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. This means that

(K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})^{\iota(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})}=k^{\prime}(Y)
. As the
\mathrm{Gal}(k^{\prime}|k)
-action on
K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime}
commutes
with the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-action,
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=((K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})^{\mathrm{Gal}(k^{\prime}|k)})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=((K\otimes_{k}k^{\prime})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}})^{\mathrm{Gal}(k^{\prime}|k)}=k^{\prime}(Y)^{\mathrm{Gal}(k^{\prime}|k)}=:k(Y^{\prime})
.
Then
K
is a finite
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-extension of
F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})
, but

K\cong F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})^{\oplus[K:F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})]}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, so

k(Y^{\prime})=K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\cong k(Y^{\prime})^{\oplus[K:F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})]}
, and thus,
K=F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})
. ∎

Remark 5.34
.

The
k
-varieties
Y
and
Y^{\prime}
in Proposition
5.33
need not be
birational. E.g. a plane quadric with no rational points
Y
and
Y^{\prime}:=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k}
are not birational,
while
Y_{F}
has a rational point if
F\cong k(Y)
, so
F_{\Psi}(Y)=F_{\Psi}(Y^{\prime})
.

5.8.
K_{0}
of categories of compact objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})

For a Roelcke precompact
G
, denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G)
the full subcategory of compact
objects in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(G)
.

For each extension
L|K
of smooth
G
-fields, the faithful functor

L\otimes_{K}(-):\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G)
induces a
\lambda
-ring
homomorphism
K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G))\to K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{L}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G))
.
This gives rise to a functor
\beta_{G}:K\mapsto K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(G))
from the category

\mathrm{SmFields}(G)
of smooth
G
-fields to the category of
\lambda
-rings.

Denote by
\Lambda_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
the category of
\lambda
-rings endowed with a surjective
\lambda
-ring
homomorphism onto the binomial ring
\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\subset\mathbb{Q}[X]
of integer-valued
polynomials in one variable. Its final object is the identity automorphism of
\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Let
\mathrm{SmFields}^{+}(G)
be the subcategory of non-trivial
G
-fields.

The following result generalizes
[
12
, Theorem 4.18]
.

Theorem 5.35
.

The functor
\beta_{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
factors through a functor

\mathrm{SmFields}^{+}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\xrightarrow{\xi}\Lambda_{{\mathbb{Z}}}
, sending to the final object the fields

F_{k,\Psi}
for all non-trivial field extensions
F|k
with
k
algebraically closed in
F
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.9
, for any smooth non-trivial
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field
K
and each object

V
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, there is a unique polynomial
P_{V}(X)\in\mathbb{Q}[X]
such that

P_{V}(\#T)=\dim_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T}}
for any sufficiently large finite subset
T\subset\Psi
.

By Corollary
4.19
,
P_{V_{2}}=P_{V_{1}}+P_{V_{3}}
for any short exact sequence

0\to V_{3}\to V_{2}\to V_{1}\to 0
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, and thus,
V\mapsto P_{V}

induces an additive homomorphism
\varphi_{K}
from
K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}))
.

As the polynomials
P_{K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle}(X)=\binom{X}{s}
are linearly independent for all

s\geq 0
, the classes of
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
are linearly independent as well. As the polynomials

\binom{X}{s}
additively generate the target,
\varphi_{K}
is surjective.

To check the multiplicativity and the compatibity with
\lambda
-structures, it suffices to notice that

(V_{1}\otimes_{K}V_{2})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}=V_{1}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}\otimes_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}}V_{2}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}

and
(\bigwedge^{r}_{K}V)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}=\bigwedge^{r}_{K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}}V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}

for all sufficiently big
J
. Namely, by Proposition
4.6
, this is
reduced to the case of
V_{i}=K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s_{i}}\rangle
, which is clear.

If
K=F_{k,\Psi}
then, as in
[
12
]
,
V
admits a finite
resolution by finite direct sums of
K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
’s, so the classes of

K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
’s form an additive basis of
K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}))
,
and thus,
\varphi_{K}
is bijective. ∎

Example 5.36
.

According to Example
3.16
, the classes of
K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for all
s\geq 0

generate a subring of
K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}))
isomorphic to
\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
;
the classes of
x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}
for all
\lambda\in\Xi
generate a subring isomorphic to the group ring

\mathbb{Z}[\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}]
. Let
\varepsilon:\mathbb{Z}[\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}]\to\mathbb{Z}
be
given by
\sum_{i}m_{i}[\mathcal{L}_{i}]\mapsto\sum_{i}m_{i}
. By Lemma
3.21
,

([K_{\mathrm{a}}]-[x^{\lambda}K_{\mathrm{a}}])[K_{\mathrm{a}}\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle]=0
for all
s>0
,
so
K_{0}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K_{\mathrm{a}}}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}))
is isomorphic to the ring

\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\oplus\ker\varepsilon
with the multiplication given by

(P,\alpha)(Q,\beta)=(PQ,Q(0)\alpha+P(0)\beta+\alpha\beta)
.

5.9.
Topology of the Gabriel spectra

Given a Grothendieck category
\mathcal{A}
, any proper closed subset of the Gabriel spectrum

\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{A})
of
\mathcal{A}
is an intersection of sets

[X]^{c}:=\{P~|~\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,P)\neq 0\}
for a collection of compact objects
X
.

Lemma 5.37
.

(1)

Let
X,Y
be compact objects of
\mathcal{A}

such that there is either an epimorphism
X\to Y
or a monomorphism
Y\to X
. Then
[Y]^{c}\subseteq[X]^{c}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{A}
is locally noetherian then the closure
\overline{\{P\}}

of a point
P
consists of the points
P^{\prime}
such that
P
admits a monomorphism into a product of
injectives presenting
P^{\prime}
.

(3)

Let
T
be a collection of compact generators
of
\mathcal{A}
, and
S
be a subset in
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{A})
such that for any

W\in T
there exists
P\in S
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(W,P)=0
. Then
S
is dense.

(4)

For any simple object of
\mathcal{A}
, the isomorphism class of its injective hull is a closed point.

Proof.

Let
S
be a non-dense subset. As the closure
\overline{S}
of
S
is the intersection of all
proper closed subsets containing
S
, one has

\overline{S}=\bigcap_{X:\text{ compact, $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,P)\neq 0$ for all
$P\in S$}}[X]^{c}=\bigcap_{X\subset\prod_{P\in S}P:\text{ compact, $\mathrm{pr}_{P}(X)\neq 0$ for all $P\in S$}}[X]^{c}.

If
S=\{P\}
consists of a single point
P
then we get

\overline{\{P\}}=\bigcap_{0\neq X\subset P:\text{ compact}}[X]^{c}
.
If
P^{\prime}\in\overline{\{P\}}
then for any non-zero subobject
X\subset P
there is a non-zero morphism

X\to P^{\prime}
, so extending it to
P
we see that
P
admits a monomorphism into a product of copies of
P^{\prime}
.
Conversely, if
P
admits an embedding into a product of copies of
P^{\prime}
then any non-zero subobject

X\subset P
admits a non-zero morphism to
P^{\prime}
.

If
P
is simple then
[P]^{c}
is the class of
E(P)
,
i.e. the class of
E(P)
is a closed point.
∎

Lemma 5.38
.

Let
K
be a smooth
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field,
and
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}))
.

(1)

Any proper closed subset in
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
consists of injective hulls of objects of bounded level.

In particular, any infinite subset of
\{P_{1}^{(K)},P_{2}^{(K)},\dots\}
is dense in
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
,
where
P_{i}^{(K)}
is the isomorphism class of
E(K\langle\binom{\Psi}{i}\rangle)
.

(2)

Let
Q=E(Y)
be a point of
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
for an object
Y
of level
\leq n
. Then the closure of
Q

does not contain points
P=E(X)
such that
N_{n}X=0
. In particular,
\overline{\{P_{i}\}}\not\ni P_{j}
for
j>i
.

(3)

One has
\overline{\{P_{n}^{(K_{?})}\}}\supseteq\{P_{0}^{(K_{?})},P_{1}^{(K_{?})},\dots,P_{n}^{(K_{?})}\}
for any
n\geq 0

with the following exceptions, where
F=k:

?=\mathrm{a,b,c}
,
n=1
, and
\Gamma=0
if
?=\mathrm{a};

?=\mathrm{d}
and
n=2
.

(4)

The points
P_{1}^{(K_{\mathrm{a}})}
and
P_{1,m}^{(K_{\mathrm{c}})}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
are closed.

(5)

For
?\in\{\mathrm{a},\mathrm{c}\}
, the closure of
E(\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{?}})

contains neither
P_{s}
for
s>1
, nor
P^{\prime}_{2}
if
?=\mathrm{c}
and
p\neq 2
, nor
P_{1,n}
for

n\in\mathbb{Z}
if
?=\mathrm{c}
.

Proof.

For any compact object
X
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{K}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
, there is an integer

n\geq 0
such that
X=N_{n}X
. Then
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,E(Y))=0
for all
Y
such that
N_{n}Y=0
, in particular,

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(X,P_{j})=0
for all
j>n
, i.e.
[X]^{c}
contains only finitely many
P_{s}
’s. This implies that any closed
set containing infinitely many
P_{s}
’s is not proper, i.e. coincides with
\mathrm{Spec}_{K}
.

To describe closures of points, we apply Lemmas
5.37
(
2
)
and
3.9
to
U=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}
,
V=K\langle\binom{\Psi}{s}\rangle
for
s<\#I
, and

V\xrightarrow{\lambda}K
,
\sum_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}a_{J}[J]\mapsto\sum_{J\in\binom{\Psi}{s}}a_{J}
.
As the subgroups
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}
are maximal, we have only to check that
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}

and that there is an element
A\in K_{I}\langle\binom{I}{s}\rangle^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}
with
\lambda(A)=1
. The

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}
-action on
K_{?}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|I}}
is non-trivial, so
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}}\neq K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
,
while in the exceptional cases
K_{?}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I}}=k=K_{?}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}
.
Then the composition of
K^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,I,J}}=K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{U}
,

a\mapsto A_{a}:=\sum_{g\in\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}/\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}a^{g}[gJ]\in V^{U}
, with
\lambda
is the trace

\mathrm{tr}_{K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}|K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}}:K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}\to K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}
. As the field
extension
K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}|K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}
is separable, there is an element
a
with

\mathrm{tr}_{K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I,J}}|K_{I}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{I}}}(a)=1
, and then
\lambda(A_{a})=1
.

As the
k
-vector space
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits((x-y)^{m}K_{?}\langle\Psi\rangle,(x-y)^{n}E(K_{?}))=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(K_{?}\langle\Psi\rangle,(x-y)^{n-m}E(K_{?}))
is at most one-dimensional and
E(K_{?})
is injective,

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\langle(x-y)^{2m-n}(y^{n-m}[x]-x^{n-m}[y])\rangle,E(K_{?}))=0
,

The
k
-vector space
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits((x-y)^{m}K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi\rangle,(x-y)^{n}E(K_{\mathrm{c}}))=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(K_{\mathrm{c}}\langle\Psi\rangle,(x-y)^{n-m}E(K_{\mathrm{c}}))
is one-dimensional if
F=k
and
n\geq m
;
it vanishes if
F=k
and
n<m
.
As
E(K_{\mathrm{c}})
is injective,
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\langle(x-y)^{2m-n}(y^{n-m}[x]-x^{n-m}[y])\rangle,E(K_{\mathrm{c}}))=0
,
i.e. the
\mathrm{Pic}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}
-orbit of
P_{0}
does not meet the closure of
P_{1,m}
.
The remaining points are not in the closure of
P_{1,m}
for trivial reasons.

For any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P_{1,m},P_{0,n})\neq 0
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, i.e.
P_{0,n}\in[P_{1,m}]^{c}
,
while
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P_{1,m},P_{s})=0
for all
s>1
, i.e.
P_{s}\notin[P_{1,m}]^{c}
, meaning that the closure of

\{P_{0,n}~|~n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\subset\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{?}}
contains neither
P_{s}
for
s>1
.

As
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P_{1},P^{\prime}_{2})=0
, the closure of
\{P_{0,n}~|~n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\subset\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}
does not
contain
P^{\prime}_{2}
if
p\neq 2
.

As
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(P_{1,m},P_{1,n})=0
for all
n\neq m
, the closure of
\{P_{0,n}~|~n\in\mathbb{Z}\}\subset\mathrm{Cl}_{K_{\mathrm{c}}}

contains neither
P_{1,n}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

5.10.
Examples of non-injective tensor units of type
F_{S}
in positive characteristic

Proposition 5.39
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
p>0
,
F^{\prime}
and
F
be its
field extensions in which
k
is algebraically closed,
F^{\prime}\neq k
, and
s\geq p>t\geq 1
be integers. Then
(i)

any morphism of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-modules
\varphi:F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}\to F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
vanishes at 1;
(ii)

the object
F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
is not injective in

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Proof.

Fix some
x\in F^{\prime}\smallsetminus k
and
J\in\binom{\Psi}{u}
for some

t<u\leq s
. For each
I\in\binom{\Psi}{t}
, denote by
x_{I}
the decomposable tensor in

\bigotimes_{k,\binom{\Psi}{t}}F^{\prime}\subset F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}
whose only
non-identical tensor multiple is
x
on the
I
-th place. For such
I
with
I\subset J
, let

a_{I}:=\varphi\left(\frac{x_{I}}{\sum_{I^{\prime}\subset J}x_{I^{\prime}}}\right)\in F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
. Then

a_{I}\in F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}=F_{\binom{J}{s}}
, so either
a_{I}\in k
or
u=s
and

a_{I}
is a decomposable tensor in
\bigotimes_{k,\binom{\Psi}{s}}F\subset F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}

whose only non-identical tensor multiple is on the
J
-th place.

As
F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}=F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi,J}}
,
a_{I}=a
is independent of
I
,
and thus
\sum_{I\subset J}\frac{x_{I}}{\sum_{I^{\prime}\subset J}x_{I^{\prime}}}\in F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}
is sent to
\binom{u}{t}a
.
Taking
u=p
, we get that 1 is sent to 0.

As any morphism
F^{\prime}_{\Psi}\to F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
in
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
vanishes at 1, the restriction
of any morphism
\xi\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}}(F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}\otimes_{k}F^{\prime}_{\Psi},F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}})

to
k\otimes_{k}F^{\prime}_{\Psi}
vanishes at 1, so the restriction of
\xi
to

F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}\otimes_{k}k=F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
cannot be identical, and thus, the natural embedding

F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}\xrightarrow{(-)\otimes 1}F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}\otimes_{k}F^{\prime}_{\Psi}
in

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
admits no splitting, which means that the object

F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is not injective. ∎

Remarks 5.40
.

1. Now Lemma
A.9
implies that the object
k
of
the category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
is injective if and only if the characteristic of
k
is
0
.

2. For any tower of fields
k\subset F\subseteq\widetilde{F}
with
k
algebraically closed
in
\widetilde{F}
and any pair of integers
s>t\geq 1
, there are no
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-field embeddings of

F_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
into
\widetilde{F}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}
over
k
. [If there is one,
we may restrict it to
F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}
for a subfield
k\neq F^{\prime}=k(t)
. Then, we may choose
a subfield
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\subseteq\widetilde{F}
finitely generated over
k
such that,
for some
I\in\binom{\Psi}{s}
,
{F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|I}}=F^{\prime}
is embedded into

\widetilde{F}^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}{}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|I}}=\mathrm{Frac}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime}{}^{\otimes_{k}^{\binom{I}{t}}})
.
But
\mathrm{tr.deg}(F^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{s}}{}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}|k)=\binom{\#J}{s}
for
any finite
J\subset\Psi
, while
\mathrm{tr.deg}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime}_{\binom{\Psi}{t}}{}^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|J}}|k)=\binom{\#J}{t}\cdot\mathrm{tr.deg}(\widetilde{F}^{\prime}|k)
is less than

\binom{\#J}{s}
for sufficiently large
J
.]

6.
Relation between representations of automorphism groups of universal
domains and of symmetric groups: some examples

We keep notations of §
3.1
. Let
C
be an algebraically closed extension of infinite
transcendence degree of an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic 0,
G
be the group of all
automorphisms of the field
C
leaving
k
fixed.

6.1.
0-cycles and representations

Denote by
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
the full subcategory of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(G)
consisting of all objects
V

such that
V^{G_{L}}=V^{G_{L^{\prime}}}
for any purely transcendental field subextension
L^{\prime}|L
in
C|k
.
It follows from
[
9
, Lemma 1.1]
and
[
19
, Proposition 1.4]
that this category is
equivalent to the category of contravariant functors
\mathcal{F}
from the category of smooth

k
-varieties with smooth morphisms to the category of
k
-vector spaces that (i) are stably
birational (i.e.
\mathcal{F}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
and

\mathcal{F}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}(U)
for any smooth
X
and any open dense
U\subseteq X
)
and (ii) have Galois descent (i.e.
\mathcal{F}(X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}(Y)^{H}

for any Galois covering
Y\to X
with group
H
).

There are some reasons to expect that the following holds
(
[
15
, Conjecture on p.513, Prop.7.6]
).

Conjecture 6.1
.

Any simple object of
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
can be embedded
into the exterior algebra
\Omega^{\bullet}_{C|k}=\bigwedge_{C}^{\bullet}\Omega^{1}_{C|k}
of
the space
\Omega^{1}_{C|k}
of Kähler differentials on
C
over
k
.

This conjecture has consequences for the Chow groups
CH_{0}(-)^{0}
of 0-cycles of degree 0.

Corollary 6.2

(
[
15
]
, Corollary 7.9;
[
19
]
, Corollary 3.2)
.

Assume that
Conjecture
6.1
holds and a rational map
f:Y\dasharrow X
of smooth proper

k
-varieties induces injections
\Gamma(X,\Omega^{q}_{X|k})\to\Gamma(Y,\Omega^{q}_{Y|k})

for all
q\geq 0
. Then
f
induces a surjection
CH_{0}(Y)\to CH_{0}(X)
.

If
\Gamma(X,\Omega^{q}_{X|k})=0
for all
q\geq 2
then the Albanese map induces an
isomorphism between
CH_{0}(X)^{0}
and the group of
k
-points of the Albanese variety of
X
.
(The converse for ‘big’ fields
k
, due to Mumford, is well-known.)

Example 6.3
.

Let
r\geq 1
be an integer and
X
be a smooth proper
k
-variety with

\Gamma(X,\Omega^{j}_{X|k})=0
for all
r<j\leq\dim X
. Let
Y
be a sufficiently general

r
-dimensional plane section of
X
. Then the inclusion
Y\to X
induces an injection

\Gamma(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{X|k})\to\Gamma(Y,\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y|k})
.

Remark 6.4
.

Though the direct summands of
\bigotimes_{C}^{\bullet}\Omega^{1}_{C|k}

are the only known explicit simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
,
there is a continuum of others, at least if
C
is countable, cf.
[
18
, Prop.3.5.2]
.
However, Conjecture
6.1
relates the ‘interesting’
simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
to Kähler differentials.

6.2.
A remark on structure of objects of
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)

Let
\Psi
be a transcendence base of
C
over
k
, so
G_{\Psi}
is the subgroup of
G
consisting of the
elements identical on
\Psi
(or equivalently,
G_{\Psi}
is the absolute Galois group of the subfield

k(\Psi)
of
C
generated over
k
by
\Psi
).

Let
\Pi
be the set of isomorphism classes of smooth irreducible
k
-representations of
G_{\Psi}
.
For any
\overline{\rho}\in\Pi
the subgroup
\ker\overline{\rho}\subseteq G_{\Psi}
is open and normal,
so the subfield
C^{\ker\overline{\rho}}
is a finite Galois extension of
k(\Psi)
, and thus, it
coincides with
L_{\overline{\rho}}(\Psi\smallsetminus T_{\overline{\rho}})
for a finite subset

T_{\overline{\rho}}\subset\Psi
and a finite Galois extension
L_{\overline{\rho}}
of
k(T_{\overline{\rho}})
.
Then
\overline{\rho}
factors through the restriction

G_{\Psi}\to\mathrm{Gal}(L_{\overline{\rho}}|k(T_{\overline{\rho}}))
.

The set
\Pi
is endowed with a natural action of the group
\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}:=N_{G}(G_{\Psi})/G_{\Psi}
.
This
\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}
-action is smooth, since, for each point
\overline{\rho}\in\Pi
, its stabilizer

\overline{\mathrm{Stab}}_{\overline{\rho}}
contains the subgroup

\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}\cap\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)|T_{\overline{\rho}}}
.

We restrict the objects of
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
to the subgroup
N_{G}(G_{\Psi})=\{g\in G~|~g(k(\Psi))=k(\Psi)\}
.

For any
\overline{\rho}\in\Pi
and any smooth
k
-representation
W
of
G
, denote by

W_{\overline{\rho}}=\rho\otimes_{k}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G_{\Psi}\rangle}(\rho,W)

the
\overline{\rho}
-isotypical part of
W
, where
\rho
is a representation
in
\overline{\rho}
, so
W=\bigoplus_{\overline{\rho}\in\Pi}W_{\overline{\rho}}=\bigoplus_{O}W_{O}
, where
O
runs over the
\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}
-orbits
on the set
\Pi
and
W_{O}=\bigoplus_{\overline{\rho}\in O}W_{\overline{\rho}}
.

Let
\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}
be the pullback of
\overline{\mathrm{Stab}}_{\overline{\rho}}

in
N_{G}(G_{\Psi})
, so
\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}=\{g\in N_{G}(G_{\Psi})~|~g(W_{\overline{\rho}})=W_{\overline{\rho}}\}
if
W_{\overline{\rho}}\neq 0
. Then

\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}^{1}:=\{g\in N_{G}(G_{\Psi})~|~g|_{W_{\overline{\rho}}}=id\}

is a normal subgroup of
\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}
and

W_{O}=k\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi})\rangle\otimes_{k\langle\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}\rangle}W_{\overline{\rho}}=k\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi})/\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}^{1}\rangle\otimes_{k\langle\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}/\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}^{1}\rangle}W_{\overline{\rho}}
.

If
W\in\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
then
W_{\overline{\rho}}\subseteq W^{G_{L_{\overline{\rho}}}}
, so

\mathrm{Stab}_{\overline{\rho}}^{1}
is open, since contains
N_{G}(G_{\Psi})\cap\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{C|L_{\overline{\rho}}}
.
Then the
k\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi})\rangle
-module
W_{O}
is isomorphic to

k\langle N_{G}(G_{\Psi})/U\rangle\otimes_{k\langle H\rangle}W^{\prime}
for an open subgroup
U
,
a subgroup
H
of
N_{N_{G}(G_{\Psi})}(U)/U
and a (faithful)
k
-representation
W^{\prime}
of
H
.

6.3.
The functor
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)

Let
\Upsilon:=\{g\in G~|~g(\Psi)=\Psi\}

be the setwise stabilizer of
\Psi
in
G
, so
\Upsilon/G_{\Psi}=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.
It follows from

[
17
, Proposition 2.5]
that any open subgroup of
G
containing
\Upsilon_{T}

contains the subgroup
G_{T}
.

The functor
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)\xrightarrow{H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
factors through
the forgetful functor
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(\Upsilon)
, so
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)
is a composition
of a forgetful functor with an equivalence of categories (by Proposition
2.2
)

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(\Upsilon)\xrightarrow{\Gamma_{G_{\Psi}}}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
(with the quasi-inverse

V\mapsto V\otimes_{k(\Psi)}C
), and therefore, the functor
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)
is faithful
and exact, but it is not full:
\Omega^{1}_{C|k}
and
\mathrm{Sym}^{2}_{C}\Omega^{1}_{C|k}
are distinct
simple objects of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)
, while
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},\Omega^{1}_{C|k})=\Omega^{1}_{k(\Psi)|k}
and

H^{0}(G_{\Psi},\mathrm{Sym}^{2}_{C}\Omega^{1}_{C|k})\cong\Omega^{1}_{k(\Psi)|k}\oplus\Omega^{2}_{k(\Psi)|k}
.

Example 6.5
.

1. Let
W_{0}:=V^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}\otimes_{k}C
correspond to the sum of all copies of

k(\Psi)
in
V
. Then
W_{0}=W^{\Upsilon}\otimes_{k}C
. On the other hand, any vector of
W^{\Upsilon}
is
fixed by an open subgroup of
G
containing
\Upsilon
, i.e.,
W^{\Upsilon}=W^{G}
,
and thus,
W_{0}=W^{G}\otimes_{k}C
is a direct sum of copies of
C
.

2. If
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W)\cong\Omega^{i}_{k(\Psi)|k}
then
W\cong\Omega^{i}_{C|k}
, at least if
k

is the field of algebraic numbers.

Proof.

Indeed,
W
is admissible and irreducible, so we can apply
[
16
]
. ∎

Proposition 6.6
.

For any
admissible
object
V
of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)

(
i.e.,
\dim_{C^{U}}V^{U}<\infty
for any open subgroup
U\subset G)
, one has

H^{0}(G_{\Psi},V)\cong\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\infty}(\Omega^{i}_{k(\Psi)|k})^{\oplus m_{i}}
in

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
for some integer
m_{i}\geq 0
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.13
, the objects
k(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{i}\rangle
of

\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
are injective, so using Corollary
4.15
and
identifications
k(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{i}\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{i}_{k(\Psi)|k}

of Example
3.20
,
we may assume that
V
is cyclic. For any finite
T\subset\Psi
consider the
k(T)
-semilinear
representation
V^{G_{T}}
of the group of
k
-linear automorphisms of the
k
-linear span of
T
.
As it follows from
[
16
]
,
V^{G_{T}}
admits a filtration whose
quotients are direct summands of
k(T)
-tensor powers (Schur functors) of
\Omega^{1}_{k(T)|k}
.
Moreover, for
T\subset T^{\prime}
one has
V^{G_{T}}\otimes_{k(T)}k(T^{\prime})\subseteq V^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
and these
filtrations are compatible, thus giving rise to an ascending filtration on
V^{G_{\Psi}}
whose
quotients are direct summands of
k(\Psi)
-tensor powers of
\Omega^{1}_{k(\Psi)|k}
.

By Lemma
3.15
(combined with Example
3.16
), for any
integer
N\geq 0
,
k(\Psi)\langle\Psi\rangle^{\otimes_{k(\Psi)}^{N}}=k(\Psi)\langle\Psi^{N}\rangle

is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{N}k(\Psi)\langle\binom{\Psi}{i}\rangle^{\oplus a_{i,N}}
,
where

t^{N}=:\sum_{i=0}^{N}a_{i,N}\binom{t}{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[t]
.
∎

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
W\in\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
. Then
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W\otimes_{k}C)
is an
injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})
.

Proof.

We keep notation of §
6.2
.
Then
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W\otimes_{k}C)=\bigoplus_{\overline{\rho}\in\Pi}H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})=\bigoplus_{O}V_{O}
, where
\overline{\rho}^{\vee}
is the dual of
\overline{\rho}
,

O
runs over the
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-orbits in
\Pi
and

V_{O}=\bigoplus_{\overline{\rho}\in O}H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})
.
For any
\overline{\rho}\in\Pi
one has
W_{\overline{\rho}}\subseteq W^{\ker\overline{\rho}}\subseteq W^{G_{L_{\overline{\rho}}}}
,
so the pointwise stabilizer
\mathrm{Stab}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}
of
W_{\overline{\rho}}
is open.

The image
\overline{\mathrm{Stab}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}}
of
\mathrm{Stab}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}
in

\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}
is an open subgroup, i.e.,
\overline{\mathrm{Stab}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}}\supseteq\mathrm{Cr}_{\Psi}\cap\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)|T_{\overline{\rho}}}
for a finite

T_{\overline{\rho}}\subset\Psi
such that
\overline{k(T_{\overline{\rho}})}\supseteq L_{\overline{\rho}}
.

Denote by
\mathrm{St}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}(W)
the image of

\mathrm{Stab}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}(W)\cap\Upsilon

in
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
. Then
\mathrm{St}^{1}_{\overline{\rho}}(W)
is an open subgroup of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
, i.e.,

\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}\supseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T_{\overline{\rho}}}
for a finite

T_{\overline{\rho}}\subset\Psi
such that
\overline{k(T_{\overline{\rho}})}\supseteq L_{\overline{\rho}}
, so
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})

is a smooth
k(\Psi)
-semilinear representation of
\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}
with “trivial”
restriction to
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T_{\overline{\rho}}}
, i.e.,
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})=H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T_{\overline{\rho}}}}\otimes_{k(T_{\overline{\rho}})}k(\Psi)
.
By Lemma
4.26

the
k(\Psi)\langle\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}\rangle
-module

H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})
is “trivial”, i.e.,

H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})=H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T_{\overline{\rho}}}}\otimes_{k(\Psi)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi|T_{\overline{\rho}}}}}k(\Psi)
,
and therefore,
V_{O}=H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W_{\overline{\rho}}\otimes_{k}C_{\overline{\rho}^{\vee}})^{\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}}\otimes_{k(\Psi)^{\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}}}k(\Psi)\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}\rangle
is a direct sum of
several copies of
k(\Psi)\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}/\mathrm{St}_{\overline{\rho}}\rangle
. ∎

Remark 6.8
.

It is not true in general that
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W)
is injective, even if
W=V\otimes C

for a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear smooth representation
V
of
G
. E.g., if
V
is the kernel of the degree
morphism
\mathbb{Q}\langle C\smallsetminus k\rangle\to\mathbb{Q}
then one has an exact sequence

0\to H^{0}(G_{\Psi},W)\to H^{0}(G_{\Psi},C\langle C\smallsetminus k\rangle)\to k(\Psi)\to 0
,
which is not split, since
H^{0}(G_{\Psi},C\langle C\smallsetminus k\rangle)^{\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}}=H^{0}(G,C\langle C\smallsetminus k\rangle)=0
.

6.4.
Smooth semilinear representations of symmetric groups with quasi-trivial connections

For field extensions
K|k
and
L|K
, a
K
-vector space
V
and a connection

\nabla:V\to V\otimes_{K}\Omega_{K|k}
, denote by

\nabla_{L}:V\otimes_{K}L\to V\otimes_{K}\Omega_{L|k}
the unique extension of
\nabla
.
If
V
is endowed with an action of a group
H
then a connection on
V
is called
an
H
-
connection
if it commutes with the
H
-action.

A connection
\nabla:V\to V\otimes_{K}\Omega_{K|k}
is called
trivial

(resp.,
quasi-trivial
) if the natural map
\ker\nabla\otimes_{k}K\to V

is surjective (resp., if
\nabla_{\overline{K}}
is trivial).

If
k
is algebraically closed and
H
is a group of automorphisms of
K
then the functor
of horizontal sections
\ker\nabla_{\overline{K}}:(V,\nabla)\mapsto\ker\nabla_{\overline{K}}

is an equivalence of categories

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{smooth}\\
\mbox{$k\langle\widetilde{H}\rangle$-modules}\end{array}\right\}&\xleftarrow[\sim]{\ker\nabla_{\overline{K}}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{smooth $K\langle H\rangle$-modules with}\\
\mbox{quasi-trivial $H$-connection over $k$}\end{array}\right\}\end{array},

where
\widetilde{H}
is the group of all field automorphisms of
\overline{K}
inducing elements
of
H
on
K
, so
\widetilde{H}
is the extension of
H
by

\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}|K)
. The inverse
functor is given by
W\mapsto((W\otimes_{k}\overline{K})^{\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{K}|K)},\nabla_{W})
,
where
\nabla_{W}
is restriction of the connection on
W\otimes_{k}\overline{K}
vanishing on
W
.

Consider the following diagram of functors.

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(G)&\xrightarrow[\sim]{\otimes_{k}C}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{smooth}\\
\mbox{$C\langle G\rangle$-modules with}\\
\mbox{trivial $G$-connection}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\mbox{for}}&\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{C}(G)\\
\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\mbox{restriction}$\hss}&&\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)$\hss}&&\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle H^{0}(G_{\Psi},-)$\hss}\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(\Upsilon)&\xleftarrow[\sim]{\ker\nabla_{C}}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\mbox{smooth $k(\Psi)\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}\rangle$-modules}\\
\mbox{with quasi-trivial}\\
\mbox{$\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}$-connection}\end{array}\right\}&\xrightarrow{\mbox{for}}&\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k(\Psi)}(\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi})\\
\downarrow\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\mbox{restriction}$\hss}&&\\
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(G_{\Psi})&&\end{array}

By
[
17
, Lemma 4.14]
, restriction to
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
of the composition of
the upper row is fully faithful.

It is explained in
[
18
, §4.5]
, that some conjectures (a conjectural relation to Chow groups
of 0-cycles of projective generators of the category
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
and the motivic conjectures)
imply that there are only finitely many (or no) isomorphism classes of simple objects of

\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
containing a given irreducible representation of
G_{\Psi}
. In fact, (in the
spirit of Howe–Bushnell–Kutzko–et al.) one can expect that any simple object of

\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
is determined uniquely by its restriction to
G_{\Psi}
.

From this point of view, the smooth
k(\Psi)
-semilinear representations
of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
with quasi-trivial
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
-connection should carry
interesting information on the corresponding simple objects of
\mathcal{I}_{G}(k)
.

Appendix A
Smooth linear representations of infinite symmetric groups

A.1.
Induction and coinduction

Let
A\to R
be a continuous homomorphism
of associative unital topological rings, admitting a base of neighbourhoods of 0 given
by a collection of left ideals.

Example A.1
.

Let
G
be a group,
U\subseteq G
be a subgroup and
K
be a ring
endowed with a smooth
G
-action. Our only examples of such continuous homomorphisms
A\to R

will be inclusions of skew group rings
K\langle U\rangle\hookrightarrow K\langle G\rangle
for
open subgroups
U\subset G
; the base of open left ideals
\{I_{H}\}

is indexed by a base
B
of open subgroups
H\subset G
, where
I_{H}
is generated by elements

u-1
for all
u\in H
.

Let
R\mbox{-mod}^{\mathrm{sm}}
be the category of left
R
-modules
such that any element is annihilated by an open left ideal.
For any
R
-module
W
denote by
W^{\mathrm{sm}}:=\mathop{\underrightarrow{\lim}}\limits_{I}\{w\in W~|~Iw=0\}

the smooth part of
W
, where
I
runs over the open left ideals in
R
.

The restriction functor
\mathrm{Res}^{R}_{A}:R\mbox{-mod}^{\mathrm{sm}}\to A\mbox{-mod}^{\mathrm{sm}}

admits a right adjoint (coinduction), sending a smooth

A
-module
V
to the smooth part of the
R
-module
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)
:

(3)

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(\mathrm{Res}^{R}_{A}W,V)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{R}(W,\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)^{\mathrm{sm}})

for any smooth
A
-module
V
and any smooth
R
-module
W
, where
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)
is
endowed with the standard left
R
-module structure:
R\times\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)
,

(r,\varphi)\mapsto[r^{\prime}\mapsto\varphi(r^{\prime}r)]
.

If
A\to R
is an open embedding then the functor
\mathrm{Res}^{R}_{A}
admits a left adjoint
(induction):
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(V,\mathrm{Res}^{R}_{A}W)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{R}(R\otimes_{A}V,W)
for any
V
and
W
as above.

It follows from the adjunction (
3
) that if a
smooth
A
-module
V
is injective then
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,V)^{\mathrm{sm}}

is an injective smooth
R
-module. In particular, if the restriction
to
A
of a smooth
R
-module
W
is injective the adjunction
morphism
W\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{A}(R,W)^{\mathrm{sm}}
gives an embedding into
an injective smooth
R
-module.

Notation.
For a group
H
and a pair of
H
-sets
M,N
denote by

\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(M,N)
the set of
H
-equivariant maps from
M
to
N
.

Example A.2
.

One has

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{K\langle U\rangle}(K\langle G\rangle,V)^{\mathrm{sm}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{U}(G,V)^{\mathrm{sm}}

for any open subgroup
U\subset G
and any
K\langle U\rangle
-module
V
. The standard

K\langle G\rangle
-action on
\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{U}(G,V)^{\mathrm{sm}}

is given by
r\cdot g:\varphi\mapsto[\xi\mapsto r^{\xi}\varphi(\xi g)]
.

Lemma A.3

(
[
13
]
, Lemma 4.2)
.

For any group
G
, any finite
collection
H_{1},\dots,H_{N}
of subgroups of
G
of infinite index and any finite collection of

\xi_{ij}\in G
one has
G\neq\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}(\coprod_{j=1}^{M}\xi_{ij}H_{i})
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
N\geq 1
, the case
N=1
being trivial.
Suppose that
G=\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}(\coprod_{j=1}^{M}\xi_{ij}H_{i})
for some

\xi_{ij}\in G
. If
H_{s}
and
H_{t}
are commensurable for some
s\neq t

then
G=\bigcup_{i\neq t}(\coprod_{j=1}^{M[H_{t}:H_{s}\cap H_{t}]}\xi_{ij}^{\prime}H_{i})

for some
\xi_{ij}^{\prime}\in G
, contradicting the induction assumption, so
we further assume that
H_{i}
are pairwise non-commensurable.

On a set of pairwise non-commensurable subgroups of
G
define the following relation

S_{1}\preceq S_{2}
if
[S_{1}:S_{1}\cap S_{2}]<\infty
. This is a partial order: if
S_{1}\preceq S_{2}

and
S_{2}\preceq S_{3}
then
[S_{1}\cap S_{2}:S_{1}\cap S_{2}\cap S_{3}]\leq[S_{2}:S_{2}\cap S_{3}]<\infty

(since the natural map
S_{1}\cap S_{2}/S_{1}\cap S_{2}\cap S_{3}\to S_{2}/S_{2}\cap S_{3}
is injective).

Let
H_{s}
be maximal among
H_{i}
with respect to
\preceq
. Fix
\xi\in G

outside the set
\xi_{s1}H_{s}\coprod\cdots\coprod\xi_{sM}H_{s}
. Then

\xi H_{s}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\neq s}(\coprod_{j=1}^{M}\xi_{ij}H_{i})
. Omitting those

\xi_{ij}
for which
\xi H_{s}\cap\xi_{ij}H_{i}=\varnothing
and replacing appropriately
the remaining
\xi_{ij}
’s, we may assume that
\xi^{-1}\xi_{ij}\in H_{s}
.

Finally,
H_{s}=\bigcup_{i\neq s}(\coprod_{j=1}^{M}\xi^{-1}\xi_{ij}H_{i}\cap H_{s})
,
contradicting induction assumption. ∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
G
be a group,
R
be a ring,
A
be a subring of the
centre of
R
and
E
be an
R
-module. We endow
R
and
E
with the trivial
G
-action.

For any subgroup
H\subseteq G
the map
\mu:\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,A)\otimes_{A}E\to\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)
,

\varphi\otimes e\mapsto[g\mapsto\varphi(g)e]
, is an injective morphism of

R\langle G\rangle
-modules; there is a morphism of
R\langle G\rangle
-modules

\kappa_{H}:E\langle G/H\rangle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E),\ \text{{\rm given by}}\ e[g]\mapsto\left(\xi\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}e&\text{{\rm if} $\xi^{-1}\in[g]$}\\
0&\text{{\rm otherwise}}\end{array}\right.\right)\ \text{{\rm for any $e\in E$,
$g\in G$}}.

Let
\{H_{i}\}
be a collection of pairwise non-commensurable subgroups of
G
.
Then the
G
-equivariant map
\sum_{i}\kappa_{H_{i}}:\bigoplus_{i}E\langle G/H_{i}\rangle\to\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{\bigcap_{i}H_{i}}(G,E)

is injective.

Proof.

For any
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)
and any
[g]\in H\backslash G
the element

f(\tilde{g})\in E
is independent of a choice of lifting
\tilde{g}
of
[g]
to
G
,
so there is a canonical isomorphism of
A
-modules
\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{\{1\}}(H\backslash G,E)=\prod_{H\backslash G}E
, given by

f\mapsto[[g]\mapsto f(\tilde{g})]=(f(\tilde{g}))_{[g]\in H\backslash G}
.
Then the injectivity of
\mu
follows from the injectivity of the natural map

(\prod_{H\backslash G}A)\otimes_{A}E\to\prod_{H\backslash G}E
.

The
G
-action on
\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{\{1\}}(H\backslash G,E)
is given by
f^{g}:[\xi]\mapsto f([\xi g])
.

Suppose that
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{\{1\}}(H\backslash G,E)^{U}
for a subgroup
U\subseteq G
such that

H\backslash G/U
is finite, i.e.,
H\backslash G/U=\coprod_{i=1}^{N}Hg_{i}U
for some
g_{i}\in G
.
Then
f=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}
, where
f_{i}
is supported on
Hg_{i}U
. Then

f_{i}=\mu(\delta_{Hg_{i}U}\otimes f_{i}(g_{i})):hg_{i}u\mapsto f_{i}(g_{i})

Suppose that
f\in\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)^{U}
for a subgroup
U\subseteq G
such that
H\backslash G/U

is finite, i.e.,
H\backslash G/U=\coprod_{i=1}^{N}Hg_{i}U
for some
g_{i}\in G
.
Then
f=\sum_{i=1}^{N}f_{i}
, where
f_{i}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)
is supported on
Hg_{i}U
. As

f_{i}(g_{i})=f_{i}(g_{i}U)=f_{i}((g_{i}Ug_{i}^{-1})g_{i})
, the element
f_{i}(g_{i})
is fixed by

H\cap(g_{i}Ug_{i}^{-1})
and
f_{i}(hg_{i}u)=f_{i}(g_{i})
for all
h\in H
and
u\in U
.

To check the
G
-equivariantness (
\kappa_{H}(e[g^{\prime}g])(\xi)=\kappa_{H}(e[g])(\xi g^{\prime})
),
it suffices to note that
\xi^{-1}\in[g^{\prime}g]
if and only if
(\xi g^{\prime})^{-1}\in[g]
. As
\kappa_{H}

transforms
e[g]
to the function supported on the left
H
-coset
[g^{-1}]\in H\backslash G

and taking there the value
e
, the map
\kappa_{H}(e[g])
is constant on
H\xi
, i.e.,
\kappa_{H}

factors through
\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E)
.

Suppose that
\sum_{i}\kappa_{H_{i}}
is not injective, so its kernel contains an element

\sum_{t=1}^{N}\alpha_{t}
for some non-zero
\alpha_{t}\in E\langle G/H_{i_{t}}\rangle
. Let
H_{i_{s}}
be
a maximal subgroup with respect to the partial order
\preceq
defined
in the proof of Lemma
A.3
. Then looking at the support of both sides
of the equality
-\kappa_{H_{i_{s}}}(\alpha_{s})=\sum_{t\neq s}\kappa_{H_{i_{t}}}(\alpha_{t})
,
we get an inclusion
H_{i_{s}}\subseteq\bigcup_{t\neq s}(\coprod_{j}H_{i_{t}}\xi_{tj})
into
a finite union of cosets. But this contradicts Lemma
A.3
,
so
\sum_{i}\kappa_{H_{i}}
is injective. ∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
G
be a permutation group
and
H\subseteq G
be an open subgroup such that
H\backslash G/U
is finite for any open
subgroup
U\subset G
. Let
R
be an associative unitary ring endowed with a smooth
G
-action
and
E
be a smooth left
R\langle H\rangle
-module. Suppose that there is a collection

\{G_{\Lambda}\}
of pairwise non-commensurable subgroups of
G
containing
H
such that

\sum_{\Lambda}\#\{\text{{\rm finite $U$-orbits in $G/G_{\Lambda}$}}\}=\#(H\backslash G/U)

for any
U
from a base of open subgroups of
G
. Then the left
R\langle G\rangle
-module
W

coinduced by
E

(
i.e., the smooth part of
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{R\langle H\rangle}(R\langle G\rangle,E)=\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G,E))
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{\Lambda}E\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle
.

Proof.

Lemma
A.4
provides a natural injective morphism

\sum_{\Lambda}\kappa_{\Lambda}:\bigoplus_{\Lambda}E\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle\to W
. To check its
surjectivity, we verify the surjectivity of the induced maps
\bigoplus_{\Lambda}E\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle^{U}\to W^{U}=\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G/U,E)
, where
U
runs over a base of open subgroups of
G
. One has
(i)
\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(G/U,E)=\prod_{O}\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(O,E)
, where
O
runs over the
H
-orbits in
G/U
and
(ii)
E^{sUs^{-1}\cap H}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{H}(O,E)
for a fixed
s\in O
,
where
e\mapsto[hs\mapsto he]
. One has (i)
\bigoplus_{\Lambda}E\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle^{U}=\bigoplus_{\Lambda}\bigoplus_{\omega\subset G/G_{\Lambda}}E\langle\omega\rangle^{U}
, where
\omega

runs over the finite
U
-orbits in
G/G_{\Lambda}
; (ii)

E^{\sigma G_{\Lambda}\sigma^{-1}\cap U}\xrightarrow{\sim}E\langle\omega\rangle^{U}
for a fixed
\sigma\in\omega
, where
e\mapsto\sum_{u\in U/\sigma G_{\Lambda}\sigma^{-1}\cap U}e^{u}\cdot u\sigma
.

As
W^{U}
is spanned by the delta-functions on
G_{J}\backslash G/U
, it suffices to check
the surjectivity in the case
E=\mathbb{Z}
, which in turn is equivalent to the cases where

E
runs over all prime fields. If
E
is a field then the surjectivity is equivalent
to the coincidence of dimensions of the source and the target, i.e., to the equality

\sum_{\Lambda}\#\{\text{finite $U$-orbits in $G/G_{\Lambda}$}\}=\#(H\backslash G/U)
.
∎

Proposition A.6
.

Let
G
be the group of automorphisms
of a countable
\Psi
, which is either a set or a vector space over a finite field,
fixing a finite subset of
\Psi
. Let
R
be an associative unitary ring endowed
with the trivial
G
-action,
J\subset\Psi
be a
G
-closed finite subset and
E
be
a left
R
-module considered as a trivial
G_{J}
-module. Then the left
R\langle G\rangle
-module
coinduced by
E
is isomorphic to
\bigoplus_{\Lambda}E\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle
, where

\Lambda
runs over the
G
-closed subsets of
J
.

Proof.

As
G_{\Lambda}
are pairwise non-commensurable, by Lemma
A.5
,
we only need to construct a bijection
\mu:H\backslash G/U\xrightarrow{\sim}\coprod_{\Lambda}\{\text{{\rm finite $U$-orbits in $G/G_{\Lambda}$}}\}
, where

H=G_{J}
and
U=G_{T}
for
T
running over the
G
-closed subsets of
\Psi
.
To each element
[\sigma]\in H\backslash G/U
we associate the subgroup

\langle H,\sigma U\sigma^{-1}\rangle
(generated by
H
and
\sigma U\sigma^{-1}
)
as
G_{\Lambda(\sigma)}
and the class
[\sigma^{-1}]_{\Lambda}
of

\sigma^{-1}
in
G/G_{\Lambda(\sigma)}
. Clearly, these
G_{\Lambda(\sigma)}

and
[\sigma^{-1}]_{\Lambda}
are well-defined and, in particular,

[\sigma^{-1}]_{\Lambda(\sigma)}
is fixed by
U
. This gives rise to
a map
\eta:H\backslash G/U\to\coprod_{\Lambda}(G/G_{\Lambda})^{U}
.
As mentioned in the beginning of §
3.1
,
G_{\Lambda(\sigma)}=G_{J\cap\sigma(T)}
.
It is easy to see that (i)
\eta
is bijective and (ii) any finite
G_{T}
-orbit
in
G/G_{\Lambda}
consists of a single element, so we are done. ∎

A.2.
Groups of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type

Definition A.7
.

A Roelcke precompact group
G
is of

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type
if (i) the maps
(G/V)^{V}\to(G/U)^{V}
are surjective for
all
V\subseteq U
from a base
B
of open subgroups of
G
, (ii) for
each
U\in B
the natural projection
N_{U}(V)\backslash(G/U)^{V}\to U\backslash G/U
is injective for sufficiently small
V\in B
.

Remarks.
1. The condition (i) is the transitivity condition from

[
7
, p.5, §3.1]
; (ii) is the bijectivity condition of
[
7
, p.5, §3.2]
.

2. Clearly, (i) any product of groups of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type is again of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type,
(ii) a locally compact group is of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type if and only if it is profinite
(as
B
we take the set of all normal open subgroups of
G
).

Examples.
The following examples of groups
G
of

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type are constructed as the groups of all permutations of an infinite
set
\Psi
respecting an extra structure on
\Psi
. Thus,
G
is a closed
subgroup of the group
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
of all permutations of the plain set

\Psi
. As a base
B
of open subgroups we take the subgroups
G_{T}
for
some exhausting collection of finite
G
-closed subsets
T\subset\Psi
.

(1)

\Psi
is a plain set, i.e.,
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
.
Then (i)
G/G_{T}
is the set of all embeddings
T\hookrightarrow\Psi
,
(ii)
(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
consists of the embeddings
T\hookrightarrow T^{\prime}
(it is
clear that
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T^{\prime}):=N_{G}(G_{T^{\prime}})/G_{T^{\prime}}=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{T^{\prime}}
acts transitively on
(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
)
and (iii) the rule
[\sigma]\mapsto(\sigma(T)\cap T,\sigma|_{\sigma(T)\cap T})

identifies the sets
G_{T}\backslash G/G_{T}
and

N_{G_{T}}(G_{T^{\prime}})\backslash(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
(if
\#T^{\prime}\geq\#T
) with the set of
pairs
(T_{0},\iota)
consisting of a subset
T_{0}\subseteq T
and an
embedding
\iota:T_{0}\hookrightarrow T
.

(2)

Let
\Psi
be an infinite-dimensional vector space over a finite
field with a marked finite-dimensional subspace
V
. Let
G
be the group of automorphisms of
the vector space
\Psi
identical on the subspace
V
, so all
G
-closed
T\subset\Psi
contain
V
.
Then (i)
G/G_{T}
is the set of all linear embeddings
T\hookrightarrow\Psi
identical on
V
, and
(ii)
(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
consists of the embeddings
T\hookrightarrow T^{\prime}
identical on
V
.
Clearly, (i)
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T^{\prime})
is transitive on
(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
, (ii) the natural
projection
N_{G_{T}}(G_{T^{\prime}})\backslash(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}\to G_{T}\backslash G/G_{T}

is injective, at least if
\dim T^{\prime}\geq 2\dim T
.

(3)

The automorphism group of an infinite-dimensional projective space

\Psi=\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{S})
over a finite field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Then
G/G_{T}
consists of the projective embeddings
T\hookrightarrow\Psi

and
(G/G_{T})^{G_{T^{\prime}}}
consists of the projective embeddings
T\hookrightarrow T^{\prime}
.
The conditions of Definition
A.7
are verified in the same way as
in Example (
2
).

Lemma A.8
.

Let
G
be a group of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type.
Then, for any left artinian
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
R
,
the Grothendieck category
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{R}(G)
of smooth left
R\langle G\rangle
-modules is
locally of finite length, so the Gabriel spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{R}(G)
is cofinite.

Proof.

Any smooth finitely generated
R\langle G\rangle
-module is a quotient of
a finite sum of
R\langle G\rangle
-modules of type
R\langle G/U\rangle
for some subgroups
U

from a fixed base
B
of open subgroups of
G
. Therefore, it suffices
to check that the
R\langle G\rangle
-modules
R\langle G/U\rangle
are of finite length.

Fix a base
B
of open subgroups of
G
as in definition of group of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type, some
U\in B
and
set
\Psi:=G/U
. As
G
is a group of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type,
\Psi^{V}\cong H_{V}/(H_{V}\cap(U/V))
is an
H_{V}
-orbit
for all
V\in B
,
V\subseteq U
, where
H_{V}:=N_{G}(V)/V
, so

(4)

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{F\langle H_{V}\rangle}(F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle)\cong F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle^{H_{V}\cap(U/V)}\cong F\langle(H_{V}\cap(U/V))\backslash\Psi^{V}\rangle

for any finite field extension
F|\mathbb{Q}
. The natural projection

(H_{V}\cap(U/V))\backslash\Psi^{V}=(N_{G}(V)\cap U)\backslash\Psi^{V}\to U\backslash\Psi
is injective
for sufficiently small
V
, and therefore,
length of the
F\langle H_{V}\rangle
-module
F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle
does not exceed

\dim_{F}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{F\langle H_{V}\rangle}(F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle)=\dim_{F}F\langle(H_{V}\cap(U/V))\backslash\Psi^{V}\rangle\leq\dim_{F}F\langle U\backslash\Psi\rangle=\#[U\backslash\Psi].

For a fixed
V
, we choose
F
so that the
F\langle H_{V}\rangle
-module
F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle

is a sum of absolutely simple modules. Clearly, length of the

R\langle G\rangle
-module
R\langle\Psi\rangle
does not exceed length of the

(R\otimes F)\langle G\rangle
-module
(R\otimes F)\langle\Psi\rangle
. By Lemma

A.14
, length of the
(R\otimes F)\langle G\rangle
-module

(R\otimes F)\langle\Psi\rangle
does not exceed

\sup_{V\in B}\mathrm{length}_{(R\otimes F)\langle H_{V}\rangle}((R\otimes F)\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle).

On the other hand, by Lemma
A.16
(i),

\mathrm{length}_{(R\otimes F)\langle H_{V}\rangle}((R\otimes F)\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle)=\mathrm{length}_{(R\otimes F)}(R\otimes F)\cdot\mathrm{length}_{F\langle H_{V}\rangle}(F\langle\Psi^{V}\rangle)
, so finally,

\mathrm{length}_{(R\otimes F)\langle G\rangle}((R\otimes F)\langle\Psi\rangle)\leq\mathrm{length}_{R\otimes F}(R\otimes F)\cdot\#[U\backslash\Psi]
,
which is finite by Lemma
A.16
(ii). ∎

A.3.
Injectivity of trivial representations and local admissibility

For an abelian group
P
and a set
S
we denote by
P\langle S\rangle^{\circ}

the subgroup of the abelian group
P\langle S\rangle
consisting of the finite
formal sums
\sum_{i}a_{i}[s_{i}]
for all
a_{i}\in P
,
s_{i}\in S
with

\sum_{i}a_{i}=0
. If
P
is a module over a ring
A
then
P\langle S\rangle^{\circ}

is naturally an
A
-submodule of the
A
-module
P\langle S\rangle
.

The assumptions of the following lemma holds for all groups of
\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits
-type (from Definition
A.7
).

Lemma A.9
.

Let
G
be a permutation group admitting a base of open subgroups

B
such that for any pair
V\subseteq U
in
B
the group
N_{G}(V)/V
is finite
and the
(N_{G}(V)/V)
-action on the set
(G/U)^{V}\supseteq(N_{G}(V)U)/U=N_{G}(V)/(U\cap N_{G}(V))

is transitive, i.e.,
(G/U)^{V}=(N_{G}(V)U)/U
.
Let
R
be an associative ring and
M
be an
R\otimes\mathbb{Q}
-module considered as a trivial
G
-module.

Then any essential extension
E\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{R}(G)
of

M
is a trivial
G
-module. In particular, if
M=M\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is an injective
R
-module
then (when endowed with the trivial
G
-action) it is an injective object of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{R}(G)
.

Proof.

Let
E
be an essential extension of
M
in the category of smooth left

R\langle G\rangle
-modules. Any element of
E
spans a smooth cyclic
R\langle G\rangle
-module.
Any smooth cyclic
R\langle G\rangle
-module is isomorphic to a quotient of a permutation module

R\langle G/U\rangle
for an open subgroup

U
in a base of open subgroups. I claim that the image of

R\langle G/U\rangle^{\circ}
in
E
has zero
intersection with
M
.

Indeed, suppose that the image
\beta\in E
of an element
\alpha\in R\langle G/U\rangle^{\circ}

belongs to
M
.

The support of the element
\alpha
, i.e., a finite subset in
G/U
, is pointwise fixed by
an open subgroup
V\in B
; in particular,
\alpha
is fixed by
V
. Then
g\alpha
is well-defined
for any
g\in N_{G}(V)/V
. As the image
\beta
of
\alpha
is fixed by
G
(and in particular, by
N_{G}(V)
),
the image of
\alpha^{\prime}:=\sum_{g\in N_{G}(V)/V}g\alpha
in
E
is
\#(N_{G}(V)/V)\beta\in M
.
By one of the assumptions of the Lemma, the support of
\alpha
is contained in a (finite)

(N_{G}(V)/V)
-orbit, so the same holds for
\alpha^{\prime}
, unless
\alpha^{\prime}=0
. On the other hand,
\alpha^{\prime}

is fixed by
N_{G}(V)
, and thus, it is a multiple of the sum of the elements of a
N_{G}(V)
-orbit.
But
\alpha^{\prime}\in R\langle G/U\rangle^{\circ}
, so this multiple is zero, and therefore,
\beta=0
.

This means that the image of
R\langle G/U\rangle^{\circ}
in
E

does not meet
M
. Therefore,
E
is a quotient of a sum of trivial
G
-modules

R\langle G/U\rangle/R\langle G/U\rangle^{\circ}=R
, i.e.,
E
is a trivial
G
-module. ∎

Example A.10
.

The following groups satisfy the assumption of Lemma
A.9
on the group
G
:

\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
for an infinite set
\Psi
;
\mathrm{GL}(\Psi)
for an infinite-dimensional vector space
\Psi
over
a finite field; the group of all automorphism of an algebraically closed field extension of infinite transcendence
degree of an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic 0 preserving a given transcendence base.

Corollary A.11
.

The group
H^{\geq 1}_{\mathrm{Sm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},k(\Psi)^{\times})

consists of torsion;
H^{i}_{\mathrm{Sm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i+1}_{\mathrm{Sm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Z})
for all
i\geq 1
, in particular,

H^{2}_{\mathrm{Sm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},\mathbb{Z})=0
.

Proof.

Modulo scalar multiples, any rational function is a product of uniquely determined powers of irreducible
polynomials, so
k(\Psi)^{\times}/k^{\times}\cong\bigoplus_{P}\mathbb{Z}\langle\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi}/U_{P}\rangle

for the stabilizers
U_{P}
of irreducible polynomials
P\in k(\Psi)^{\times}/k^{\times}
. By
Lemma
A.9
,
\mathbb{Q}\langle\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi}/U\rangle
is injective in

\mathrm{Sm}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi})
for any open subgroup
U\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi}
, so
the short exact sequence
1\to k^{\times}\to k(\Psi)^{\times}\to k(\Psi)^{\times}/k^{\times}\to 1
shows
that
H^{\geq 1}_{\mathrm{Sm}}(\mathfrak{S}_{\Psi},k(\Psi)^{\times}\otimes\mathbb{Q})=0
. ∎

Lemma A.12
.

Let
G
be a Roelcke precompact group,
B
be a base
of open subgroups of
G
and
k
be a field. Suppose that the trivial representations
k
of any

V\in B
are injective as smooth
k\langle V\rangle
-modules. Then any smooth finitely generated

k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
is admissible, i.e.,
\dim_{k}W^{V}<\infty
for any
V\in B
.

Proof.

As
H^{0}(V,W)
is a direct summand of the
k\langle V\rangle
-module
W

for any
V\in B
, the natural map
H^{0}(V,W)\to H_{0}(V,W)
is injective, so

\dim_{k}H^{0}(V,W)\leq\dim_{k}H_{0}(V,W)
. The
k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
is a quotient
of
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}k\langle G/U_{i}\rangle
for some open subgroups
U_{i}\subseteq G
,
in particular,
H_{0}(V,W)
is a quotient of

H_{0}(V,\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}k\langle G/U_{i}\rangle)
, and thus,
\dim_{k}H_{0}(V,W)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\dim_{k}H_{0}(V,k\langle G/U_{i}\rangle)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\#[V\backslash G/U_{i}]<\infty
.
Combining all these inequalities, we get
\dim_{k}H^{0}(V,W)<\infty
. ∎

Theorem A.13
.

Let
G
be a Roelcke precompact
group such that the group
N_{G}(V)
acts transitively on
(G/U)^{V}

for all
V\subseteq U
from a base
B
of open subgroups of
G

and
k
be a field of characteristic 0. Then any smooth finitely generated

k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
is admissible (but there exist
infinite direct sums among admissible
k\langle G\rangle
-modules).

Proof.

For any triple
U_{2}\subseteq U_{1}\subseteq V
in
B

the projection
(G/U_{1})^{U_{1}}\to(G/U_{2})^{U_{1}}
is surjective, so its
restriction
(V/U_{1})^{U_{1}}\to(V/U_{2})^{U_{1}}
is surjective as well.
This means that
N_{V}(U_{1})
acts transitively on
(V/U_{2})^{U_{1}}
,
and thus, any open subgroup
V
of
G
satisfies assumptions of
Lemma
A.9
. Then Theorem follows from
Lemma
A.12
, since any open subgroup
V

of
G
satisfies assumptions of Lemma
A.9
.
∎

A.4.
Filtered representations and local length-finiteness

Lemma A.14
.

Let
G
be a group,
W
be a
k\langle G\rangle
-module for a ring
k
,

B
be a partially ordered set. Let
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in B}

be a partially ordered exhausting collection of
k
-submodules
in
W
:
W=\bigcup_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
. Let
G_{\alpha}
be a
subgroup of the stabilizer in
G
of the
k
-submodule
U_{\alpha}
.

Then length of the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
does not exceed

\inf_{\beta}\sup_{\alpha\geq\beta}\mathrm{length}_{k\langle G_{\alpha}\rangle}U_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
n:=\inf_{\beta}\sup_{\alpha\geq\beta}\mathrm{length}_{k\langle G_{\alpha}\rangle}U_{\alpha}
is finite and

0=W_{-1}\subsetneqq W_{0}\subsetneqq W_{1}\subsetneqq W_{2}\subsetneqq\dots\subsetneqq W_{n}\subseteq W
is a chain of
k\langle G\rangle
-submodules in
W
.
Choose some
e_{i}\in W_{i}\smallsetminus W_{i-1}
and
\beta\in B

such that
e_{0},\dots,e_{n}\in U_{\beta}
. Then
0\subsetneqq W_{0}\cap U_{\beta}\subsetneqq W_{1}\cap U_{\beta}\subsetneqq W_{2}\cap U_{\beta}\subsetneqq\dots\subsetneqq W_{n}\cap U_{\beta}\subseteq U_{\beta}

is a chain of
k\langle G_{\beta}\rangle
-submodules in
U_{\beta}

of length
n+1
, contradicting our assumptions. ∎

Corollary A.15
.

Let
G
be a permutation group,
B
be a base
of open subgroups of
G
. Let
W
be a smooth representation of

G
over a field
k
. Then
\mathrm{length}_{k\langle G\rangle}W\leq\inf\limits_{V\in B}\sup\limits_{H\in B,~H\subseteq V}\mathrm{length}_{k\langle N_{G}(H)\rangle}W^{H}
.

Proof.

In Lemma
A.14
we take

G_{H}=N_{G}(H)
and
U_{H}=W^{H}
. ∎

The following result is standard.

Lemma A.16
.

Let
C
be a finite-dimensional
k
-algebra
for a field
k
and
A,B
be associative unital
k
-algebras. Let
M
be a
simple
A
-module and
N
be a simple
B
-module. Suppose that
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{B}(N)=k
.
Then
(i)

M\otimes_{k}N
is a simple
A\otimes_{k}B
-module,
(ii)

the
A
- (and
A\otimes_{k}C
-) module
M\otimes_{k}C
is of finite
length.

Proof.

(ii) is a trivial. (i) Fix a non-zero
A\otimes_{k}B
-submodule in
M\otimes_{k}N

and a shortest non-zero element
\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{a}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}

in it, i.e.,
a\geq 1
is minimal possible. Clearly,
\alpha
is
a generator of
M\otimes_{k}N
if
a=1
, so assume that
a>1
.
If the annihilators of
n_{i}
in
B
are not the same, say

\mathrm{Ann}(n_{i})
is not contained in
\mathrm{Ann}(n_{j})
,
then
(1\otimes\xi)\alpha
is a shorter non-zero element
for any
\xi\in\mathrm{Ann}(n_{i})\smallsetminus\mathrm{Ann}(n_{j})
,
contradicting the minimality of
a
. There remains the case
of coincident annihilators of
n_{1},\dots,n_{a}
. In that case

B/\mathrm{Ann}(n_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cdot n_{i}}}{{\longrightarrow}}N
are
isomorphisms. As
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{B}(N)=k
, all these isomorphisms differ
by a non-zero multiple, and therefore, the images of the element

1\in B/\mathrm{Ann}(n_{1})
under
\cdot n_{i}
differ by a
non-zero multiple as well, i.e., the elements
n_{1},\dots,n_{a}

are proportional, so finally,
a=1
. ∎

A.5.
More notations and a description of injective cogenerators

Let
G\subseteq\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
be a permutation group. For a finite
G
-closed subset

T\subset\Psi
, denote by
V^{\Psi}_{T}
the common
kernel of all morphisms of
G
-modules
\pi:\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\to\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle

for all proper
G
-closed subsets
T^{\prime}\subsetneqq T
.

Remarks.
1. In this definition it suffices to consider only maximal
T^{\prime}
and only
the morphisms
\pi
induced by the natural projections
G/G_{T}\to G/G_{T^{\prime}}
,
since the elements of the
\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T^{\prime})
-orbits of such
\pi
’s generate

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{G}(\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle,\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)
.

2. Clearly, any endomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
preserves
V^{\Psi}_{T}
,
so
V^{\Psi}_{T}
is an
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{G}(\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle)
-module.

3. For any subset
S\subseteq T
such that
G_{T}=G_{S}
the
induced map
\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\to\mathbb{Z}\langle\prod_{t\in S}G/G_{\{t\}}\rangle
is injective and
identifies
V^{\Psi}_{T}
with
\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\cap\bigotimes_{t\in S}V^{\Psi}_{\{t\}}\subseteq\bigotimes_{t\in S}\mathbb{Z}\langle G/G_{\{t\}}\rangle
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero and
\Pi(T)
be the set of isomorphism classes of simple

k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle
-modules. (E.g., in the case
G=\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{\Psi}
the elements of
\Pi(T)
,
called
Specht

k\langle\mathop{\mathfrak{S}}\nolimits_{T}\rangle
-modules, correspond to the partitions of cardinality of

T
and they are absolutely irreducible.) For each
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
we choose its representative

\Gamma_{T,\alpha}
and set
D_{\alpha}:=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha})
, so

k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{D_{\alpha}}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha})
. Then
there are canonical decompositions of
k\langle G\times\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle
-modules

\displaystyle k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}\otimes_{D_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{T,\alpha}\quad\mbox{and}\quad k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}\otimes_{D_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{T,\alpha},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha},k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle)\quad\mbox{and}\quad V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha},k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}).

Theorem A.17
.

Let
G
be the group of automorphisms
of a countable
\Psi
, which is either a set or a vector space over a finite field, fixing
a finite subset of
\Psi
.
Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero.

Then the Gabriel spectrum of
\mathop{\mathrm{Sm}}\nolimits_{k}(G)
is cofinite and
the following holds.

(1)

The
k\langle G\rangle
-module

V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is simple for any
T
and any
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
. In particular,
the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
is semisimple. Two
k\langle G\rangle
-modules

V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
and
V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}
are isomorphic if and only if

T^{\prime}=g(T)
for some
g\in G
and
g
transforms
\alpha
to
\alpha^{\prime}
.

(2)

Any simple smooth
k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
is isomorphic to

V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
for some finite
G
-closed subset
T\subset\Psi
and
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
.

(3)

The minimal essential submodule of any module
M
of finite
length coincides with its maximal semisimple submodule (the socle), which
is
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
in the case
M=k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
.

(4)

The smooth
k\langle G\rangle
-module
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is
an injective hull of
V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
.

(5)

The
k\langle G\rangle
-module
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is indecomposable
for any
T
and any
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
. Two
k\langle G\rangle
-modules
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}

and
S^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}
are isomorphic if and only if
T^{\prime}=g(T)
for some
g\in G

and
g
transforms
\alpha
to
\alpha^{\prime}
. Any indecomposable injective smooth

k\langle G\rangle
-module is isomorphic to
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
for some
T\subset\Psi
and
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
.

(6)

The simple subquotients of the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle

are isomorphic to
V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime},\alpha}
for all finite
G
-closed subsets
T^{\prime}\subset T

and all
\alpha\in\Pi(T^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let us show first that (i) any smooth simple
k\langle G\rangle
-module

W
can be embedded into
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
for a finite subset
T\subset\Psi
,
(ii) the smooth
k\langle G\rangle
-module
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
is injective for any finite
T
.
Let
J\subset\Psi
be a finite
G
-closed subset such that
W^{G_{J}}\neq 0
.
By Lemma
A.9
, the trivial
k\langle G_{J}\rangle
-module
k
is
injective, so (i) any surjection of
k
-vector spaces
W^{G_{J}}\to k
extends
to a surjection of
k\langle G_{J}\rangle
-modules
\pi:W\to k
, (ii) the smooth part of

\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{G_{J}}(G,k)
is injective. Then
\pi
induces a non-trivial, and thus
injective, morphism
W\to\mathop{\mathrm{Maps}}\nolimits_{G_{J}}(G,k)
,
w\mapsto[g\mapsto\pi(gw)]
. By
Proposition
A.6
, this implies (i) that
W
can be embedded into

k\langle G/G_{\Lambda}\rangle
for a subset
\Lambda\subseteq J
,
(ii) the injectivity of
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
.

To see that any smooth simple
k\langle G\rangle
-module
W
can be embedded
into
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
for a finite
G
-closed subset
T\subset\Psi
,
we embed
W
into
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
for a minimal
T
. Then
W
is, in fact,
embedded into
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}\subseteq k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
: otherwise
W

embeds into
k\langle M^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
for a proper
G
-closed
T^{\prime}\subset T
.

For any pair of finite
G
-closed subsets
T\subseteq T^{\prime}\subset\Psi
, let us construct natural
isomorphisms

k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle),\quad\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle,k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T},k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}}),

where the latter space vanishes if
T\subsetneqq T^{\prime}
.
As
k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{D_{\alpha}}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha})
,
this will imply that any endomorphism of
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
or of
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
is
a composition of a projector and an automorphism. One has
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle)\cong k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle^{G_{T}}=k\langle N_{G}(G_{T})/G_{T}\rangle=k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle
. The restriction

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle,k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)\to\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T},k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)
is surjective,
since
k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
is injective. On the other hand, it is injective, since any

\varphi\in\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle,k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)

vanishing on
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
factors through a
k\langle G\rangle
-submodule of a finite
cartesian power of
k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime\prime}}\rangle
for a proper
G
-closed
T^{\prime\prime}\subsetneqq T
, while

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime\prime}}\rangle,k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)=0
.
It follows from definition of
V^{\Psi}_{T}
that
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T},k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle)=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T},k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}})
.

Next, let us show that the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
is semisimple by
constructing a complement
W^{\prime\prime}
to each
k\langle G\rangle
-submodule
W^{\prime}
of
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
.
There is an embedding
\iota
of
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}/W^{\prime}
into a direct sum of
k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle

for some finite
G
-closed
T^{\prime}\subset\Psi
. As
k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
are injective,
\iota

extends to a morphism from
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle/W^{\prime}
, so gives rise to non-zero morphisms

k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle\xrightarrow{\varphi}k\langle G/G_{T^{\prime}}\rangle
, and thus,
\#T\geq\#T^{\prime}

if
T
is
G
-closed. If
\#T^{\prime}<\#T
then
\varphi|_{k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}}=0
,
so
\varphi|_{W^{\prime}}=0
, which is contradiction. Thus,
\#T=\#T^{\prime}
, which means that (i)

\varphi
can be considered as endomorphisms in
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle)
,
(ii)
W^{\prime}
is the common kernel of the restrictions of
\varphi
’s to
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
.
As
each
\varphi
is a composition of a projector and an automorphism, we may assume that

\varphi
is a projector. Therefore,
W^{\prime}\cong\varphi(W^{\prime})\oplus(id-\varphi)(W^{\prime})
,
so
\varphi
embeds
W^{\prime}/W_{1}
into
\varphi(W^{\prime})
and
id-\varphi
embeds

W^{\prime}/W_{1}
into
W_{1}\cong(id-\varphi)(W^{\prime})
, so
(\varphi,id-\varphi):(W^{\prime}/W_{1})\oplus W_{1}\to\varphi(W^{\prime})\oplus(id-\varphi)(W^{\prime})
is injective.
As the source and the target of
(\varphi,id-\varphi)
are of the
same finite length,
(\varphi,id-\varphi)
is an isomorphism.
In particular,
(W^{\prime}/W_{1})\oplus W_{1}\cong W^{\prime}

contradicting the indecomposability of
W^{\prime}
.

Our next task is to show that (i) the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is simple
for any
T
and any
\alpha\in\Pi(T)
and (ii) two
k\langle G\rangle
-modules
V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}

and
V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}
are isomorphic if and only if
T^{\prime}=g(T)
for some

g\in G
and
g
transforms
\alpha
to
\alpha^{\prime}
. As we already know,
V^{\Psi}_{T}

and
V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}}
are semisimple and
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T},V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}})=0

for any finite
G
-closed subsets
T\subsetneqq T^{\prime}\subset\Psi
. This implies that

\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T},V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}})=0
if
T
and
T^{\prime}
are not of same length.
Then it suffices to check that
\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha},V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha^{\prime}})

is a division ring if
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime}
or is zero otherwise. One has

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T})=\bigoplus_{\alpha,\beta\in\Pi(T)}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}\otimes_{D_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{T,\alpha},V^{\Psi}_{T,\beta}\otimes_{D_{\beta}}\Gamma_{T,\beta})
. The natural map

k\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}\nolimits(T)\rangle\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{D_{\alpha}}(\Gamma_{T,\alpha})\to\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\Pi(T)}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}\otimes_{D_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{T,\alpha})

is bijective, and therefore, since the composition of this map with the inclusion into

\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T})
is also a bijection, the complementary summand

\bigoplus_{\alpha,\beta\in\Pi(T),~\alpha\neq\beta}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}\otimes_{D_{\alpha}}\Gamma_{T,\alpha},V^{\Psi}_{T,\beta}\otimes_{D_{\beta}}\Gamma_{T,\beta})

of
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits_{k\langle G\rangle}(k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T})
vanishes.

The coincidence of the minimal essential submodule, the maximal
semisimple submodule and the essential semisimple submodule is a general statement:
any simple subobject of an object
M
is contained in any essential subobject
of
M
, while any non-zero object of finite length contains a simple subobject.
By Corollary
4.3
(
3
),
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}

is essential in
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
. As
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T}
is semisimple, we are done.

As we already know,
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
is injective, so its direct summand
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}

is injective as well. As
V^{\Psi}_{T}
is the socle of
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
, the

k\langle G\rangle
-module
V^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is the socle of
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
.
As the socle of
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is simple,
S^{\Psi}_{T,\alpha}
is indecomposable.

By the evident induction on
\#T
, the simple
subquotients of the
k\langle G\rangle
-module
k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
are isomorphic
to the direct summands of the
k\langle G\rangle
-modules
k\otimes V^{\Psi}_{T^{\prime}}

for all
T^{\prime}\subseteq T
. As any simple
k\langle G\rangle
-module is a quotient of

k\langle G/G_{T}\rangle
for an appropriate
T\subset\Psi
, we get the claim. ∎
